## K. Director decisions (2026-11-17)

Approved with these decisions; they supersede the sections above where they differ.

1. **Order for tonight:** S1 -> S5 (wedge parity on the GPU), S6 fins, S7 alpha, S8 one path, S9 perf (table <= 300 s), S10 delete 2D. After that: S14 gamma transition (opt-in), S11-S13, S15 (item 5 below). Stretch items never pre-empt S8-S10.
2. **Turbulence baseline (answer to Q1):** fully turbulent walls are the baseline tonight (the standard assumption for painted rockets; OpenRocket's too) and must be disclosed in the docs. Gamma transition is the first physics item after S10. Keep the scalar-list structure of the turbulence module so it is an addition.
3. **Tier policy (answer to Q2) approved:** `Resolution::Coarse` on a finned body = automatic wall treatment with fin y+ ~ 15; the alpha table runs on the lift tier and must not be worse than the existing Euler ANF gate (`out/gate/anf_3d.md`: WT 5/5, FF 2/3 gated points).
4. **Field export: ONE contract, `Field3`**, owned by the app agent as new files `crates/bowshock-core/src/field3.rs` and `engine/field3.rs` (it builds a `Field3` from `Grid3` + state + wall faces; `solver3d` adapts and wires it in S8). **No derived meridian `Field`**: the viewer gets a 2D mode fed from `Field3`, and `field.rs::Field` is deleted with the 2D solver in S10 (this replaces the "derived meridian `Field` + `Surface`" of F and F2 and the S8/S8b split). Wall faces are exported as quads (node indices) from the same face list as the force integration, with cp, the shear vector and a part tag; remote: the final field per point in its own frame; live snapshots local only. The final type comes from the director when the app agent's design is in; nothing in S1-S7 depends on it.
5. **Solids are a general per-cell mask end to end** (solver, GPU, forces, export) **plus a per-cell part id written by the mesher** (body, fin set n, appendage n). Wall classes come from part id + face direction, not from geometry tests such as `x <= nose length` inside the solver. Only the mesher knows boxes. This keeps the door open for S15: rasterised appendages (lugs, rail buttons, later pods as blockage) on the same grid, tiled by the sector symmetry but with their force counted once.
6. **Work split** (four sessions in parallel, each in its own worktree, file ownership strict):
   * `solver3d` (worktree `bowshock-cfd-3d`, branch `cfd-3d` = the integration branch): topology and mesher, `Solver3` integration of every physics piece, CPU implicit, engine (`engine/*` except `gpu3.rs`), facade (`analysis.rs`). Critical path: S1 now; then integrate the viscous kernels (S3) and the turbulence kernels (S4); S6; S7; the solver side of S8; S10.
   * `phys3d` (branch `cfd-3d-phys`): standalone CPU physics kernels as NEW files with unit tests, written against today's `Grid3` / `solid` mask / padded `w` conventions and independent of the axis representation: `viscous3.rs`, `walldist3.rs`, `turbulence3.rs` (SST-2003 + automatic wall treatment + Aupoix), `lowmach5.rs` (5x5 preconditioning matrices). `solver3d` integrates them into `Solver3` / `implicit3.rs` and does not write those kernels; any signature `solver3d` needs that differs from the 2D pattern is raised with the director early.
   * `gpu3d` (branch `cfd-3d-gpu`): S0 now (2D reference fixture, gate test skeletons), then every GPU port (`gpu/*`, `engine/gpu3.rs`, `bowshock-gpu-cases`, benches) trailing the CPU stages by one, and the perf work. `solver3d` writes no GPU code. CPU kernels stay plain per-face / per-cell functions, and each stage ends with a "port notes" list in the commit body (function -> what changed) so the port is mechanical.
   * `app-integrate` (branch `cfd-3d-viz`): viewer, app, later the app/remote side of S8.
   * The director directs merges into `cfd-3d` at stage boundaries; `solver3d` runs them in its worktree when told.
7. **Luma:** an A/B of the local Luma HEAD against crates.io 0.4.1 is running; if it passes the GPU suites the branch gets a `[patch.crates-io]` to a Luma worktree where Luma bugs are fixed by a separate agent. A crates.io release cannot be cut. Luma issues keep being reported to the director with a repro.
8. **GPU jobs of all sessions go through `tools/gpuq`** so they do not overlap and timings stay valid.
9. **Every stage, without exception:** `cargo nextest` for the crates touched, `cargo +stable fmt --all --check`, `cargo +stable clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, commit(s) on `cfd-3d`, never push. A numerics problem unresolved after ~30 minutes is written up with its evidence and either put to one Opus-class sub-agent or reported to the director; Sonnet/Haiku-class sub-agents only for mechanical slices, never Fable-class.
10. **Shell rule:** the session cwd is the main checkout (`/home/sandvoxel/Documents/bowshock`, branch `ui`, with the user's uncommitted files): nothing is written or run there; every command uses absolute paths or `cd /home/sandvoxel/Documents/bowshock-cfd-3d`.

## L. S1 and the mesher half of S6 as built (2026-11-18): what differs from sections A-H

1. **Wedge `k` faces carry the pressure only, and the pressure source is implicit in the `j` factor.** A Riemann flux across the wedge's two `k` faces (against the cell's own mirror image) carries acoustic waves round the wedge: explicit time stepping amplified the radial momentum ~70x per iteration at the first axis cell. The `k` faces of a wedge now carry `[0, p n]` (the axisymmetric hoop term, exact for the cell's pressure), `compute_dt` has no `L_k`, and the implicit `k` factor is the identity. The pressure source's Jacobian `(I - dt J)_mom += dt/V (A1 n1 - A0 n0) dp/dU`, `dp/dU = (g-1) [|u|^2/2, -u, -v, -w, 1]`, goes into the diagonal block of the **`j`** factor, as in 2D (`implicit::cell_blocks` `src`; `implicit3::assemble3`, `if wedge && dir == 1`). Measured on the CPU (f64, `Block`, base-wake wedge, M 0.5): without the block the iteration is stable to `cfl_max` ~3 and blows up at 6; with the same block as a separate `k` factor it diverged by iteration 50 at `cfl_max` 10; with the block in the `j` diagonal it converges at `cfl_max` 10 (`Numerics3::auto`) in 350-1600 iterations at all four Mach numbers. The GPU measurements of `gpu3d` (ISSUES #22: no `k` factor and no source block: stable only to `cfl_max` 1; keeping the `k` factor: stable at 10) agree: the missing piece is the implicit pressure source, either as a one-cell `k` line (GPU) or as the `j` diagonal block (CPU). `DiagonalCorrected` solves `i` and `j` diagonally and carries the pressure block only in its block defect operator (`diag3::du3`); untested. **Viscous wedge (director's decision, `phys3d`'s `viscous3`):** the inviscid `k` flux stays pressure-only and the viscous `k` flux is added, computed against the mirror-image ghost (plane mirror, reflected gradient): that is the hoop term.

2. **Cells upstream of the nose are dead cells, not walls.** The 2D body block has cells at negative radius upstream of the tip (nodes clamped to the axis by `revolve`). They are `PART_DEAD`: never updated, not walls, so the axis ghost shows through them (the second-order stencil of the axis row reads padded ghost states). `Grid3.solid` boxes still list them (the GPU masks solid boxes).

3. **Solids are a per-cell part id** (`Grid3.part`, `Part::{Body(segment), Sting, Fin(n), Appendage(n)}`, `PART_FLUID = 0`, `PART_DEAD = 255`); wall classes come from the part and the face direction (`Wall::{Body(s), Base, Sting, FinSide, FinLe, FinTe, FinTip, Appendage}`), never from geometry tests. On the sting grid the body is the `j = 0` wall, so those cells have no part: `Grid3.wall_j0` lists the `(i range, Part)` runs. `Grid3.axis_cells` is replaced by `Grid3.axis: Vec<AxisRun>` (a base-wake grid has two axis runs, upstream of the body and in the wake).

4. **Axis ghost.** `axis3::AxisMap` builds per axis cell the ghost's source: wedge: the cell itself, matrix `-I` on `(v, w)`; half domain: the mirror cell, `diag(-1, 1)` (the mirror plane images); sector, even `n`: the same cell with `-I`; odd `n`: the mirrored cell, interpolated linearly in the azimuth when the folded azimuth is not a cell centre (`fold` is the dihedral-group folding). At most two sources per ghost, each a `k`, a weight and a 2x2 matrix on `(v, w)`.

5. **Revolved grids are area-preserving; wedge angle default 2 deg.** The inscribed polygon of flat-faced cells lacks the cross-section `sin(d)/d`, `~ -d^2/6`, of the circle (every body that much slimmer, the wake narrower; the drag bias was `-0.17 d^2` to `-0.27 d^2`: wedge 12 deg -0.76 % (M 0.9) and -1.17 % (M 2) against 2 deg, a half domain of 8 cells (22.5 deg) -2.9 %). `bowshock_cfd::grid3::polygon_scale` scales the nodes of every `k` line by `s_k = sqrt(w / sin w)`, `w` the mean width of the two adjacent cells (the one cell at the ends, in the wedge), so every ring has the circle's area (`revolve` and the sting-grid mesher; the cells of a fin layer that is solid do not count, `polygon_scale_where`, else the radii of the lines beside it jump and shear the first cell of the fin's boundary layer; the fin side nodes are placed at `asin(h / (s r))` so the fin is exactly `h` thick). Remaining: wedge 12 deg -0.02 % (M 0.9) / -0.15 % (M 2) against 2 deg, 8 cells on the base grid +0.7 % (M 1.3) / +0.3 % (M 2), 24 cells -0.01 % / -0.02 %: of opposite sign and four times smaller; [INFERENCE, not isolated] the flat-face slope: the cells' volume is the circle's, their faces are chords. Planform and wall distance are right to second order: the nodes sit at `1 + d^2/12` of their radius (`d` the mean cell width at the line), so the fin tip is that much beyond `r + span` (the ANF test grid's 24 cells of 7.5 deg: 0.14 %).

6. **Mesher.** `Domain3::{Half { phase: FinPhase::{Between, InPlane} }, Sector, Wedge { dtheta }}`; `Grid3Params.base` selects the revolved base-wake grid, with or without fins (item 15). Sector layers: the half fin sits on `k = 0` (`Part::Fin(0)`), the mid-gap plane on `k = nk` at `theta = pi/N`, N = 1..16 (N = 1 is the half domain). Sector `nk` counts the fin layer.

7. **Forces** are integrated per wall class (`run3::wall_forces`, `drag_parts`) and scaled by `2 pi / theta_span` (wedge: `2 pi / dtheta`; sector: `2 pi / (2 pi / N) = N`). Wedge and sector report `cn = 0`, `x_cp = NaN`. `run3_on` waits on `ca` instead of `cn` for wedges, sectors and for any half domain at zero incidence (CN is round-off there and never "stationary": the half domain on the base grid ran to its iteration cap before).

8. **The 2D Euler wake is not always steady.** The 2D solver's own cone-cylinder run at M 0.5 hits its iteration cap (10 000) with a base `CD` that has no steady value, so G-W1 compares nose and aft only there; at M 0.9 the cone-cylinder's base `CD` differs by 2.4 % from the 2D one with both stationary (total -1.84 %).

9. **G-W3 "N = 3 vs N = 1" is not an invariant.** The per-fin fin drag of a three-fin sector is 6 % (M 0.8) to 8 % (M 2) above a one-fin sector's on cells of 15 and 20 deg (fins interfere through the body flow; the coarse cells resolve them differently). The nose drag, supersonic, is invariant: N = 1..6 give 0.10434, 0.10463, 0.10518, 0.10479, 0.10549, 0.10522 (1.1 % spread, no odd/even alternation, so the odd-`n` ghost is no outlier). The test is the nose-drag one.

10. **Sector against the half domain is not 1 % at level 0 except where the cells are the same.** Two fins in the pitch plane (a half fin on each plane: the same cells in both): 0.00 % at M 0.8 and 2. Four fins "x" and three fins (the half domain's fins are full layer cells, the sector's a half cell on the symmetry plane): M 2 +1.4 % and +1.1 % (fin sides 0.05 %, edge faces 3 %, nose 1 %), M 0.8 -3.5 % and -1.3 % with the sharp leading edge of item 12 (the polygon bias did not cause it; a sector of 6 gap cells per 45 deg did not close it: -9 %, unconverged at 3000 iterations). The cause is not established; the gate for the GPU is 2 % at M 2 on these cells, 1 % for the replicated cases.

11. **Fin k stack (A3).** `Grid3Params.fin_stack: Option<FinStack{y1, delta, growth_bl, growth_out}>` (`FinStack::from_flow(re_c, mach_max, root_chord, y_plus)`: `y1 = y+ / (Re_d sqrt(cf/2))`, `cf = 0.027 Re_x^(-1/7)` as `Case::viscous`, `delta = 0.37 c Re_x^(-1/5)`, growth 1.25 / 1.35). The cells of every gap grow from the fin face(s): one side in a sector, both sides between two fins (the stacks meet, mirrored), one side next to a symmetry plane in the half domain; the first cell is exactly `y1` (at the root radius: the nodes of a `k` line share one azimuth, so cells are `r / r_root` times higher above), the growth increments are scaled together by `lam <= 1` so the stack closes the gap, no ratio exceeds 1.25 / 1.35. `nk` follows from the stacks. Measured on the ANF fins with the example rocket's flow (Re/m 9.84e6, d 0.0572 m, Mach 2): `y1` 5.9e-4 d (6.1e-4 in the design), normal first cell 5.887e-4 d on the grid, 21 gap cells per fin side, growth 1.335 at most everywhere in the flow; sector N = 4 `nk` 22 (y+ 15; 210 x 72 x 22 = 333 000 cells, design estimate 443 000), 34 (y+ 1; design 30), half domain Between 86, InPlane 87. The fin layer cell against its first neighbour jumps by 64 (double-wedge fins) to 136 (square) at the root, as wide as the fin against `y1`: by design (one cell thick fin).

12. **Sharp-edge floor (A4).** `half_thickness` floors at 2 % of `t/2` at an end where the section is really sharp (thickness there < 5 % of `t/2`: double wedge, airfoil, the circle of a rounded edge, the ANF section's leading edge), at 25 % (as before) at a blunt end; the taper over four node columns ahead of the leading edge, behind the trailing edge and above the tip was there. On the Euler ANF fins at Mach 2 (sector N = 4, 4 x 13-degree cells) the total CD fell by 26 %, from 0.3656 to 0.2705 (edge faces 0.185 -> 0.099; the design estimated +22 % from the flat leading-edge face). **Euler ANF numbers and the `out/gate/anf_3d.md` gate move with this; `gpu3d` / the director re-run the gate.**

13. **Roughness on the grid.** `Grid3.roughness: PartRoughness{body, fin}` (set from `Grid3Params.roughness`, in nose diameters) and `Grid3::wall_ks()`: per cell, the largest `ks` over the walls of its faces (solid parts, the `j = 0` wall runs; none on the axis rows), 0 elsewhere: `phys3d` takes that array.

14. **Tiers.** `Tier::{Euler, Coarse(WallFlow), Fine(WallFlow), Lift(WallFlow)}`, `WallFlow{re_c, mach_max, ks_body, ks_fin}`, `Case3::with_tier` (also on a wedge): body first cell for y+ 1 (60 on Lift) on both meridian grids through `case::{wall_spacing, resolve_walls}` (the 2D `Case::viscous` uses the same code: identical grids), the fin stack for y+ 15 (Coarse) / 1 (Fine), the lift tier keeps uniform azimuth (`32 << level` over the half domain, fins y+ 60 by the wall treatment). `Case3::sector(mach, body, fins, tier, level)` and `Case3::base_half(mach, alpha, body, fins: Option<Fins3>, tier, level)` take the tier (`Tier::Euler` as before) and build on the base-wake grid.

15. **Fins on the base-wake grid (the product grid).** `body_grid3` takes the fins on `Grid3Params.base` (finless it is still `base_grid3` = `revolve`). The meridian grid is `body_grid::{base_axial_nodes, base_rows_below}` (split out of `base_domain_grid`, which is unchanged), the fin segment is rebuilt by `plan_fins` on a `Host` (the sting grid's or the base grid's body, segment counts and radial fractions), the fins stand on the rows above the body surface `j >= j_off = n_sting + n_gap` (box `j: j_off..j_off + j_tip`, `Part::Fin(0)`), the body block below is solid (`Part::Body(s)`, dead cells upstream of the tip) as without fins. **Flush fins (`te_to_base = 0`, the common case): the fin's trailing-edge face (`+i`, rows `j_off..`) is on the base plane `x = x_base`, coplanar with the base face (rows below `j_off`, normal `+x`), the two meet along the root of the trailing edge: the closed body is one plane there (test: centroids at `x_base` to 1e-12, normal `+x`, free stream and closure to 1e-15 / 1e-12).** Behind the plane the layer continues as fluid cells above the base radius, tapering over four columns to the nominal width; the rows below the body surface (the body block and the wake rows down to the axis) keep the surface row's angles at every column, so the node lines are planes through the axis there: no cell is sheared (the stack cells folded otherwise: negative volumes at the first attempt) and behind the base the layer is a thin sector narrowing to the axis, never a width that jumps at the base radius. **The wake nodes behind a flush trailing edge are not regraded** (`Host.keep_wake`): the sting grid's fan of `plan_fins` into the following cells raised the Euler base drag by 10 % (fin cells 0.0094 d behind the base instead of 0.03 d, vanishing fins, M 2: base +10.7 %, +6.0 % with 16 fin cells, -2.1 % with 8; the inviscid base drag depends on the first wake cell); with the wake kept, fins of 0.002 d thickness change the wedge's nose by +0.3 %, base +0.2 %. Euler sanity, M 2, level 0, ANF-size double-wedge fins, N = 4 sector (nk 6) vs the finless wedge: fin drag (sides, edges, tips) 0.0485 against Ackeret's `N cd_w S / (pi/4)` = 0.0753 (-36 %: the fins' exposed span equals their chord, and in linear theory the tip Mach cones relieve `1/(2 beta AR)` = 29 % of the planform at M 2, so against the relieved Ackeret, 0.0535, it is -9 % on this uniform-azimuth grid (one cell, 0.1 d, between the fin faces' neighbours); G-F2 on the stack grid with a 0.15 d chord: -0.2 %), total difference +0.0557 (the base drag rises 3 % with fins); sector (nk 4) vs half domain (nk 14, the same cells) on the base grid: +0.18 %. A fin not flush (`te_to_base > 0`, on a tube ahead of a boattail) builds, its box ending before the base plane.

16. **CPU wedge runner of the S0 harness.** `tests/wedge_gates.rs`, block `cpu_wedge` (marked `BEGIN/END solver3d`): `facade_case` (Euler: the facade's 2D `Case` as a wedge, `Case3::wedge_of`), `cone` (`cone_grid` as a wedge through `grid3::wedge_of_meridian`, Cp and shock angle as `bowshock-cases::cone`), `ogive_forebody_cd` (the ogive body on the engine's base-wake grid). The duplicates in `tests/wedge.rs` (G-W1 against a live 2D run, the Taylor-Maccoll cone sample) are gone. `case2d` (transonic and subsonic smoke) and everything viscous are not on the CPU runner.

17. **S2 follow-ups (wedge line solve, stop rule, reference area).** (a) `LineSolve3::DiagonalCorrected` (the GPU default of `Numerics3::implicit_for`) is non-finite on the wedge at every `cfl_max` (iteration 72 to 88, CPU f64 and GPU alike; ISSUES #24): `Case3::on_wedge` (called by `Case3::wedge` and `Case3::wedge_of`) sets `numerics.line_solve = Some(Block)`, which `implicit_for` honours on both backends; whoever builds a wedge by hand calls `on_wedge`. Until the diagonal solve carries the pressure-source block the wedge is `Block`. (b) `StopRule3` counts the peak residual from the start of the second-order phase (`first_order_iters`, cold; from the start, warm): a drop against the first-order start-up's scale fired inside the start-up on the cone and ogive wedges (147 iterations, Cp 4 % low); the CPU (`run3_on`), the GPU runner (`GpuRunner3::run_on`) and gpu3d's gate runner now count alike. (c) The facade's coefficients refer to the largest cross-section, the engine's (2D and 3D, `drag_parts`, `lift_forces`) to the nose base: `BaseBody::nose_to_largest_area()` = `(d_nose / d_largest)^2` is the one factor (the 2D facade's `q`); the CPU wedge runner applies it, whatever reports 3D coefficients through the facade must (the two-stage body is 2.0087x off without it).

18. **Grid cache (DESIGN3D E, per-call reuse) and a mesher fix it found.** `engine::cache3`: `GridKey::of(&Grid3Params)` (the exact `Debug` text of the parameters: the body and the meridian grids, the fins, the domain, `nk`, the level, and through the tier the wall spacing, the fin stack, the roughness, i.e. the grid Mach), `GridCache::{grid, solver}` (least-recently-used, `Arc<Grid3>`), the process-wide `cache3::shared()` (3 grids) and `Case3::build_solver_cached()`. What is shared is the finished `Grid3`; `Solver3::new` rebuilds the axis map and the per-cell tables from a copy (the axis map is private to `Solver3`; sharing it needs a `Solver3::with_axis` of `phys3d`, and the wall distance joins the entry when it is a grid product). A GPU runner takes its host solver from `GridCache::solver` / `Case3::build_solver_cached` instead of `Case3::build_solver`. Measured (debug build, ANF sector, `Tier::Coarse`, 191 x 126 x 26): mesher + solver 1.14 s, cached grid + solver 0.15 s, lookup 23 us. **Mesher fix:** the timing case failed with `negative cell volume` for every wall-resolved fin grid on the base-wake grid at `Re_c >= 5e5` (not at 2e5): the rows of the body block under the fin root copied the surface row's layer width but not its node angles and polygon scale, and the fold appears between `Re_c` 2e5 and 5e5, as the wall spacing falls ([INFERENCE, the nodes were not dumped] the first body cells, about 1e-5 d, became lower than the lateral shift of the side nodes by the polygon scale between the surface row and the rows below). The rows below `j_off` now take the angles and the scale of the surface row at the column (planes through the axis), `tests/wedge.rs` builds the grids at `Re_c` 2e5, 1e6, 1e7 on `Coarse` and `Fine`.

19. **Half domain at incidence on the base-wake grid (S7, mesher side).** `Case3::base_half(mach, alpha, body, fins, tier, level)` with fins builds in both roll phases (`FinPhase::{Between, InPlane}`, set through `grid.domain`) on the lift tier (uniform `32 << level` azimuth: 191 x 88 x 32 at `Re_c` 1e6) and on the wall-resolved coarse tier (fin stacks: 191 x 132 x 114 / 115 at 1e6), `Re_c` 2e5 and 1e6 under test, 1e7 scanned (191 x 156 x 146 / 147); fin wall faces exist and no volume is negative (`tests/wedge.rs`). The roll is the phase of the fins against the pitch plane; there is no free roll angle on this grid (the half domain's symmetry plane is the pitch plane). Not done: the lift-tier fin treatment (wall functions at `y+` 60 on the fin stack) in the solver, `phys3d`'s.

20. **Motor on: the jet patch (mesher and engine done, solver hook specified).** *Mesher / engine (done):* `Case3::with_motor(jet: engine::case::Jet) -> Result<Case3, Error>` (after `with_tier`; needs the base-wake grid, no sting, an exit inside the base, a supersonic axial state) splits the gap behind the base as the 2D `Case::with_motor` does (`case::motor_rows`: `n_jet` rows at the axis, `n_ann >= 4` annulus rows, `BaseGridParams.nozzle = NozzleRows`), and `Case3::jet_state3() -> JetState3 { state: [rho, u, 0, 0, p], turb: [k, omega] }` is what the solver needs. `body_grid3` marks the two cell columns of the body block just ahead of the base plane (`i_end - 2..i_end`, rows `j < n_jet`, every `k`) `Part::Jet` (`mark_jet`, wedge, sector and half domain, finless or finned); their `+i` faces against the fluid are `Wall::Jet` (`run3::wall_class`), the annulus stays `Wall::Base`, the exit counts in no force (`forces_from`; `drag_parts` has no jet term: the base drag of a motor-on run is the annulus's, as in 2D), the viewer's part is `Jet` (`field3`). Tests: `tests/wedge.rs::the_nozzle_rows_become_a_jet_patch_on_the_base` (one face per row and `k`, on the base plane, exit area = pi r_exit^2 to 0.2 %), `a_motor_needs_a_supersonic_jet_inside_the_base`. *What `Solver3` must do at the `Part::Jet` cells and `Wall::Jet` faces (`phys3d`; mirrors `solver.rs` / `implicit.rs` / `viscous.rs::Walls::jet_iface` / `turbulence.rs`):* (a) `Config3` gets `jet: Option<JetState3>`, set by `Case3::solver_on` from `jet_state3()`; the GPU `from_host` uploads it with a per-cell mask of the `Part::Jet` cells. (b) A `Part::Jet` cell is solid (never updated) but **not a wall**: the `wall` flag of `Solver3::new` (`part != FLUID && != DEAD`) must exclude it, so no reflection ghost is built at its faces, and its primitive state in `w` is the jet state, for both ghost layers. (c) Inviscid flux of the `i` faces of the exit rows with the 2D stencil `(m2, m1, p0, p1)`: at the exit face `i = i_end` it is `(jet, jet, w[i], w[i+1])`, at `i = i_end + 1` it is `(jet, w[i_end], w[i_end+1], w[i_end+2])` (`solver.rs` 516-520); no flux at faces between two solid cells. (d) Implicit: in the `i` factor the lower neighbour of the cell `i_end` in a jet row is the fixed jet state, so the sub-diagonal block is zero and the boundary Jacobian `T` is zero (`implicit.rs` 609-625: "fixed ghost: no dependence on the interior"); the same in `diag3` and the GPU factor. `Block` and `DiagonalCorrected` alike. (e) Viscous: `noslip` is false at jet faces (`Walls::noslip_iface` excludes the nozzle rows), they are not in the wall-distance set (`wall_distance` skips them) and not wall-function or rough walls. The face sides are `l = jet state` (ghost, with the fixed `(mu, mu_t)` = `(mu(T_jet), rho_jet k / omega)` for SST, `mu_t = 0` otherwise: `viscous.rs::iface_sides`), `r = the fluid cell`; the face viscosity is the mean of the two, the face gradient from that pair, and the cell gradient of the fluid cell takes the jet state at that face (`phi = (u, v, w, T)`). (f) Turbulence: the `k`, `omega` ghost at the exit is `jet.turb` (`Kind::Fixed`, `turbulence.rs` 194), not the wall `omega` boundary condition. (g) The axis rows behind the exit take the ordinary axis ghost. (h) Verification: `tests/wedge.rs::the_jet_is_an_inflow_at_the_exit_faces` (ignored, **fails today**: the cell behind the exit row 0 has u = -0.02 against the jet's 2.0 after 30 iterations because the solver treats `Part::Jet` as a wall), then G-J (`wedge_gates.rs::gate_j_tp2005_jet_cases`, 11 TP-2005 cases, base pressure ratio, via the facade runner) and the 2D `bowshock-cases` jet tests.

21. **Migration of the 2D Euler cases to the wedge (started; nothing deleted).** `bowshock-cases::wedge::run_on_wedge` iterates a 2D case's own grid and free stream on the single-cell wedge (`wedge_of_meridian`, `Case3::wedge`'s numerics, the residual drop counted from the second-order phase) and writes the final state back into the 2D `Solver` (radial momentum from the cell's `y`, `z` momenta), so the cases' own post-processing reads it unchanged: `cone::run_cone_wedge`, `ogive::run_ogive_wedge` (the `--wedge` flag of `bowshock-validate`'s `cone` and `ogive` binaries). Parity tests: `cone::tests::wedge_10deg_100x50_matches_the_2d_case` (Cp within 0.5 % of the 2D run and 1 % of Taylor-Maccoll, shock angle within 0.1 deg), `ogive::tests::coarse_wedge_run_matches_the_2d_case` (forebody CD within 2 %); G-W4 against the frozen fixture (CPU runner): cone Cp -0.36 / -0.14 % (10 deg, 100x50 / 200x100), -0.28 / -0.15 % (15 deg), shock angle +0.03 to +0.08 deg, ogive M 1.98 +0.75 % (level 0), +0.25 % (level 1). **Cannot be re-pointed yet:** (1) the `transonic` and `subsonic` families: their runners are the 2D engine `Case` with its own schemes (the acceleration-scheme comparison `converged_state_is_independent_of_the_acceleration_scheme` has no `Solver3` counterpart), and the wedge is outside the 2 % of G-W1 at the transonic cone-cylinder (table-euler M 0.9: -2.43 % total, -2.41 % base; the 2D reference itself is unsteady at M 0.5); their G-W4 smoke cases (`case2d`) are `gpu3d`'s runner's, with no wedge numbers yet. (2) The sting-grid variants (`Wake::Sting`, the subsonic `--no-base` cases): `Case3::wedge` is on the base-wake grid; a sting wedge is what `gpu3d`'s runner builds for `wake_base == false` (`k.grid.base = None`) and has no CPU run or gate here. (3) Everything viscous and motor-on (item 20, RANS). (4) The fine grids (cone 400x200, ogive level 2) and the other Mach numbers of the sweeps (ogive 1.5, 3, 4; cone M 3) are not measured on the wedge: run the binaries with `--wedge` before trusting them. (5) The GPU drivers (`cone_gpu`, `ogive_gpu`, ...) are `gpu3d`'s.

22. **S11 design notes (superseded by items 23 and 24, which are built):** *What exists:* `plan_fins` fixes the fin's node columns once (`i_le`, `i_te`, `fin_chord_cells` between them) and `body_grid3` moves them per row `jj` above the root through `row(jj) -> (frac, x_le, chord)` (`x_le = x_le_root + sweep frac`, chord linear in `frac`), a piecewise-linear remap of the segment nodes with knots `(i_le, x_le(jj))`, `(i_te, x_le(jj) + chord(jj))`; the half thickness is `half_thickness(profile, t, chord(jj), xi)` at the row's chord; the fin box is the rectangle `i_le..i_te` x `j_off..j_off + j_tip`, a curvilinear one after the remap. Trapezoids are the special case where both edges are straight. *The generalisation:* replace the three numbers by a planform `x_le(s)`, `x_te(s)` of the span fraction `s = (r - r_t)/span` (`Planform::{Trapezoid, Ellipse { .. }, Freeform { le: Vec<[s, x]>, te: Vec<[s, x]> }}`, sampled at each row's `frac` by monotone interpolation; the ellipse is the quarter ellipse of the semi-axes root chord / 2 and span, `FinSpec.planform: Option<Planform>`, `None` = the trapezoid of the four numbers), keeping `row(jj)` the only function that knows the shape; the remap, the sharp-edge floor, the taper, the stack, the part ids and the wall classes (`FinLe` / `FinTe` are `i` faces however swept) do not change. *What has to be added:* (1) a chord floor at the tip: an elliptical or pointed tip has `chord -> 0` at `j_tip`, so the cells of the last rows lose their `i` width and fold; floor the chord at about 3 % of the root chord (rows beyond are the tip cap) and check with `fin_stack_report`-style minimum cell widths; (2) strongly curved edges skew the cells (the `i` lines follow `x_le(jj)`): a skewness gate on the grid beyond the sweep tests (max angle deviation, cell volume ratio of neighbours), and the radial fractions `sj` re-clustered where the edge turns (the tip rows); (3) the thickness law: constant `t` or constant `t / chord` (the facade's `thickness` is the maximum, so constant `t` with the section scaled per row, as now); (4) validation of the planform (`x_le < x_te` everywhere, the root trailing edge within the base for `te_to_base >= 0`, monotone in `s`); (5) the facade's empirical add-on (`fins.rs`) takes the equivalent trapezoid of the same area and mean chord; (6) tests: planform area and exposed span against the analytic ellipse, free stream and closure on the curved box, strip-theory Ackeret of the varying chord against the Euler fin pressure drag at M 2 (G-F2 with `chord(s)`), quality bounds. *Cost:* about a day of mesher work and a day of tests; it needs no solver change.

23. **Fins anywhere on the aft body.** `plan_fins` takes the aft segments under the fin root from the one that holds the root leading edge to the one that holds the root trailing edge (`k0..=k1`: tubes, boattails, flares, any number; the old "3D fins must sit on a tube" is gone). The stations between the segments inside the chord stay axial nodes (the nearest chord node moves onto each, so the body parts are still split there and the drag classes are right); the rebuilt node range runs from the start of `k0` to the end of `k1`. The fin tip stands on one cylinder, `r_tip = r_ref + span` with `r_ref` the **largest body radius under the root** (the span of a fin on a boattail is measured from the full-diameter tube, as on the Arcas); the tip row is per column (`FinPlan::remap`: the rows below it scale with `(r_tip - r_body_i) / (r_far - r_body_i)`, the rows above it fan to the boundary), the planform parameter of a row is its span fraction (`span_fraction`), and the first stack cell is at `r_ref` (finer, never coarser, where the body is narrower). The node angles of the layer are as before. Tubes are reproduced to round-off (all earlier tests unchanged). Refused with the reason: a root leading edge ahead of the aft body or a trailing edge behind the base, and an exposed span that varies by more than a factor 3 along the root (a steep boattail or flare: the rows cannot follow). Tests (`tests/wedge.rs`): the Arcas fins flush with the base (root over the tube and the 12 degree boattail) on the base-wake grid (sector, half domain of 12 cells: 8 cells of 22.5 degrees fold a cell beside these 0.93 d fins, also for the old tube-only fin) and on the sting grid: build, rest state to 7e-16, a uniform overpressure closes to 1e-11 on the base grids, the boattail pushes the body forward, the tip faces are on `r_tip` (0.15 % sector, 1 % half domain: the polygon of 15 degree cells); a fin on the boattail only. **Arcas G-F4 with the fins where they are (Euler, INFO):** increments (on - off, pressure) at M 0.6 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 0.95 / 1.0 / 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.3 / 2.96: +0.027 / +0.043 / +0.113 / +0.197 / +0.154 / +0.080 / +0.047 / +0.033 / +0.020 / +0.012 against the data +0.078 / +0.099 / +0.156 / +0.272 / +0.266 / +0.164 / +0.165 / +0.116 / +0.091 / +0.063, i.e. 35 / 43 / 72 / 72 / 58 / 49 / 29 / 29 / 22 / 20 %: supersonic as with the fin moved ahead of the boattail (0.051 / 0.034 / 0.020 / 0.012), but subsonic and transonic the Euler increment is now 35-72 % of the data, because the fins on the boattail change the pressure on it (body part +0.034 at M 0.6, +0.063 at M 0.9) while the fin sides are -0.007 at M 0.6: the interference the facade's empirical add-on has no term for. Fins-on iterations 400-1850, all CD stationary.

24. **S11 as built: elliptical and freeform fin planforms.** `engine::planform::Planform` (rows `[h, x_le, x_te]`, linear between rows, `h` the height above the body under the root, `x` from the fore end of the root, lengths in nose diameters): `Planform::trapezoid(root, tip, span, sweep)`, `Planform::ellipse(root_chord, span)` (the half ellipse of semi-axes `root_chord / 2` and `span`, 48 rows at `h = span sin(phi)`, clustered at the tip), `Planform::from_polygon(&[(z, height)])` (the model's `FreeformFin` points; first and last at height 0; one chordwise interval per height is required and checked at every band between vertex heights, a notched or multi-lobed outline is refused with "N chordwise intervals at height h"). `Fins3.planform: Option<Planform>` (`None` = the trapezoid of the four numbers), `Fins3::with_planform(pf)` sets `root_chord`, `tip_chord`, `span`, `sweep` from the outline so everything that reads them stays consistent. **The engine type `app-integrate` fills** from the model (`crates/bowshock-model`, metres, `d` the nose diameter): `RadialKind::EllipticalFin { root_chord, span, thickness }` -> `Fins3 { .. }.with_planform(Planform::ellipse(root_chord / d, span / d)?)` and `RadialKind::FreeformFin { points, thickness }` -> `.with_planform(Planform::from_polygon(&points.iter().map(|&(z, h)| (z / d, h / d)).collect::<Vec<_>>())?)`, `thickness / d`, `profile` as for a `FinSet`; `FinSet` stays `planform: None`. The mesher: `row(jj)` is the only function that knows the shape (`pf.x_le(h)`, `pf.chord(h)` at the row's `h = span_fraction * span`); the per-row x remap, the sharp-edge floor, the taper, the stack, the part ids and the wall classes are as for the trapezoid. **The tip:** a pointed or elliptical outline has a chord that goes to zero, which the grid cannot take, so the outline is cut where the chord has fallen to `TIP_CHORD_FLOOR` = 4 % of the root chord (the ellipse: at 0.9992 of its span) and the tip row is the node row at that height (the curved tip is the rows' stair of chords, the radial remap places the tip row on `r_ref + span_cut`). An outline that reaches ahead of the aft segments or behind the root trailing edge room (flush: the base) is refused. Tests (`tests/wedge.rs`): **a freeform outline that is a trapezoid reproduces the trapezoid grid to round-off** (largest node difference 0 on the base-wake grid, 1.8e-15 on the sting grid); an ellipse, a delta and a forward-swept polygon build on both grids, a gas at rest stays at rest (<= 2.6e-15), a uniform overpressure closes, and the fin side faces have the area of the outline within 0.44 % (ellipse), 1.22 % (delta, the pointed tip cut and the slanted faces) and 0.51 % (forward swept). Euler gate G-F2p (`tests/fin_gates.rs`, ignored): see the gate table.

25. **S12: fin sets and per-fin angles (classifier built, multi-set mesher not).** `engine::symmetry3::choose_domain(sets: &[Vec<f64>], incidence_plane: Option<f64>) -> Domain` takes the azimuths of every fin of every set (a set is one geometry at one station; sets are different parts) and returns the smallest valid domain: **zero incidence**: `Sector { n, phi0 }` (the width `pi / n` between adjacent mirror planes of the dihedral group `D_n`: `n` is the largest rotation order every set is invariant under, mirror planes through a fin or half way between two fins common to all sets), `Periodic { n }` (rotation symmetry and no mirror: one period `2 pi / n` whose end planes are the same plane), `Full` (nothing symmetric); **incidence in the plane `phi_inc`**: `Half { phi0 }` when that plane is a mirror plane of every set, else `Full`. Tests: `N = 1..8` evenly spaced fins are `Sector { n: N }`, four fins "+" and "x" and three fins give the half domain in their mirror planes (and `Full` at 20 degrees to four fins), four fins and three fins on different stations share only the pitch plane (`Sector { n: 1 }`), four fins and eight fins give `Sector { n: 4 }`, a pair of four-fin rings 10 degrees apart is `Periodic { n: 4 }`, three fins at arbitrary angles `Full`, two fins at +-30 degrees keep the mirror at 0. **What the mesher still needs for several sets:** the `k` layers become the union of all fin azimuths, each layer owned by its set (`Part::Fin(set)`), with per-layer node-angle and half-thickness arrays (today `alpha` / `half_w` are one array per node), one `FinPlan` (i, j ranges, remap knots, taper columns) per set on its own station (the segment search of item 23 per set; sets whose columns overlap share the rows above the surface and need the radial remaps of both: the rows between the tips need a merged `j` fan), the gap cells beside a layer of another set at the columns where that set's fin is absent are plain fluid cells of nominal width; the stack of a gap is sized by the neighbouring fins present at that column. **What `Solver3` needs for a periodic seam (`Periodic`, `Full`)**: the azimuthal lines have no end ghost (mirror or axis image): the implicit `k` factor needs cyclic block-tridiagonal solves along `k` (or the seam coupling lagged in the explicit ghost, slower to converge and not unconditionally stable at the CFL of the other factors), the face and gradient stencils across the seam (`k = nk - 1` to `k = 0`: two cells either side, the viscous cell gradients), and the axis ghost maps a cell to the cell half a period away (`n` fold: `nk / 2` for `Full`, even `nk`); the GPU kernels the same. No half or sector shortcut exists for an unsymmetric body at incidence: it is the full 2 pi domain, twice the half domain at incidence in cells.

26. **Engine wiring of the viscous tier (S4 CPU).** A wall-resolved tier is a RANS run: `Case3::with_tier(Coarse | Fine | Lift)` sets `Case3.viscous = Some(Viscous { re_c, sutherland, SST })` from the tier's `WallFlow` (`sutherland` = 110.4 K / T_inf, `SUTHERLAND_SEA_LEVEL`) and `Numerics3::viscous(mach, level)`: the 2D `Numerics::viscous` (van Albada below Mach 0.85, minmod above, the low-Mach flux correction and, below Mach 1, the low-Mach preconditioning of the implicit left-hand side, `cfl_max` 50 and 20 from Mach 1, `DtMode::Sum`, `sub_iters` 3, 200 first-order iterations) with the **block** lines and the **characteristic outflow** (`Numerics3.pressure_outflow = false`: `RansOpts3::default()` has the static-pressure outflow of the plates, wrong for a body, whose boundary layer it would force). `Case3::euler_equations()` keeps the grid and drops the viscous terms (the Euler fin gates on the stack grids). `Case3::wedge_of(&Case)` carries a viscous 2D case's parameters and numerics (`Numerics3::from_2d`). `build_solver` / `solver_on` / the grid cache (`solver_with_axis`: the axis map is cached with the grid, `Solver3::with_axis`) call `Solver3::set_viscous_with(Some(viscous), RansOpts3 { pressure_outflow, ks: grid.wall_ks() when any is non-zero })`. **Friction in the class forces:** `WallForces.shear` per wall class from `Solver3::wall_tractions()` (force per area the fluid exerts on the wall, the flux the mean-flow equations see) times the face area, `[Fx, Fz, Mz]`, the class from a (family, first node) -> `Wall` map of `wall_faces`; `forces_from`, `breakdown_from` and so `lift_forces` (the `ca` that `StopRule3` monitors) include it; `DragParts.friction: FrictionParts` (nose, aft per segment, base, fin sides, edges, tips, total), `DragParts.total` = pressure + friction (`pressure_total()`, `body_friction()`, `fin_friction()`); `drag_parts_on(case, &Solver3)`, `wall_forces_on`, `wall_shear`, `breakdown_of` take the solver (`drag_parts(case, g, u, p_inf)` and the GPU's device reduction stay pressure only: `shear` empty); `run3::y_plus_max(&Solver3)` (streamwise `j` / `k` walls only: the base and blunt edges are `i` faces) and `run3::base_cp`, in `PointResult3.{y_plus_max, cp_base}` (NaN from the GPU runner). `tests/fin_gates/common.rs`: `engine_is_viscous(case)` = the case is viscous and the backend is the CPU; `Solved.fin_friction` is `DragParts::fin_friction()`. **Line solve:** every finned `Case3` (`Case3::new` and so `sector`, `base_half`) and every wedge takes the **block** lines on both backends: the diagonal solve (the GPU default of `Numerics3::implicit_for`) blows up on a finned half domain at incidence (the example rocket on the base-wake grid at Mach 3, alpha 6: pressure collapse behind the blunt fin trailing edge from iteration ~110 on the GPU, `gpu3d`'s trace; the same case with the block lines is stable on CPU and GPU: residual 7e-3, pmin 0.078 after 400 iterations; ISSUES #24 on the CPU). **The CPU wedge runner** runs the RANS fixture cases through `Case3::wedge_of` (`BOWSHOCK_CPU_ONLY=<id>` runs one case of a group, for parallel processes).

27. **First RANS runs on the CPU through `Case3` (measured, `tests/wedge_gates.rs` CPU runner and `tests/viscous_fins.rs`).** *G-V3* (G3 ogive-cylinder, Mach 0.5, coarse, the 2 degree wedge, 2150 iterations against the 2D 4000): `cd_total` 0.209153 vs 0.213002 (-1.81 %, band 2 %), `cd_friction` 0.090085 vs 0.090032 (+0.06 %, 3 %), `cd_base` 0.118686 vs 0.122572 (-3.17 %, 4 %), `y+max` 0.649 (< 3): PASS against both the 2D CPU and the 2D GPU reference. *G-V4 example table (finless wedge, `cd_solver_total` of the fixture, all ten points through `Case3::wedge_of`, CPU f64; band 2 %, 3 % from Mach 0.9 to 1.1): all PASS:* M 0.10 +1.50 % (0.363835 vs 0.358435; the parts differ: nose -0.0079 vs +0.0024, base 0.1237 vs 0.1100), M 0.50 -0.05 %, M 0.80 -0.17 %, M 0.90 -0.25 %, M 1.00 -0.34 %, M 1.10 -0.26 %, M 1.30 -0.26 %, M 1.70 -0.28 %, M 2.30 -0.26 %, M 3.00 -0.16 %; the wedge needs 1850 to 7000 iterations (2D: 1200 to 3000), the friction agrees to 0.1 to 1 % at every point except M 0.10 (+0.8 %), the base to 0.4 to 1 % except M 0.10 (+12 %: the 2 degree wedge's low-Mach wake). *First RANS runs with fins* (ANF body and fins, Mach 2, `Re_c` 7.0e5, 300 iterations each, 8 threads): finite, positive `rho`, `p`, `k`, `omega` throughout (smallest `k` 9e-12, the free-stream floor), residual falling 1.1 orders (sector) and 1.5 orders (half domain) from iteration 20 in 300 iterations (not converged; the friction was still settling: body friction 0.51 at iteration 50, 0.075 at 300), friction of the **sector on the `Coarse` tier** (191 x 124 x 25 cells, fin stack): body 0.0747 against the flat-plate estimate 0.0720 (**1.04**), fins 0.0302 against 0.0318 (**0.95**); of the **half domain at 2 degrees on the `Lift` tier** (191 x 80 x 32): body 0.0712 (0.99), fins 0.0306 (0.96), CA 0.4382, CN 0.3483, x_cp 7.50 cal. Where the largest residual sat: the nose-tip cell on the axis (sector: all 300 iterations, `x` = -0.015 to 0.015, `r` < 0.003; half domain: from iteration 250), at iterations 100-200 of the half domain the fin leading-edge / root junction (`x` = 8.995, `r` 0.50 to 0.55, theta 45 degrees: the leading edge of the fin) and the trailing edge / base corner (`x` = 9.995 to 10.015, `r` 0.5); no instability, no negative `k` / `omega`, nothing at the fin tip. **`y+` by class** (first cell of the streamwise walls): `Coarse` tier body 2.2, fin sides 25.0 (designed 15), fin tips **5345**; `Lift` tier body 51 (designed 60), fin sides **4198**, fin tips **4799**. The tips are not resolved in either tier (the cell above a tip is the far-field fan of the radial grid, 0.1 d), and **the `Lift` tier's fin sides are not at `y+` 60**: its uniform azimuthal cells (`32 << level` over the half domain, 5.6 degrees) put the first cell of a fin side at 0.05 to 0.15 d, thousands of wall units, which is far outside the log layer the automatic wall treatment is calibrated for (the friction still came out at the flat-plate value, within 5 %); a fin stack at `y+` 60 for the lift tier (the fin stack code takes any `y+`) would give the intended wall spacing at the cost of the uniform azimuth. `y+max` as the engine reports it (`run3::y_plus_max`) is over all streamwise walls including the tips; `y_plus_max_of(s, class)` gives it by class.

28. **Mean-stationary stop rule (`StopRule3`, `Numerics3::{mean_window, mean_from, mean_tol}`).** The subsonic base wake of the Euler half domain does not settle: `app-integrate` measured on the GPU (finless example body, iterations 3000 to 7000 sampled every 10) at Mach 0.5, 2 degrees CA peak to peak 57 % of its mean at a period of 222 iterations (CN 120 % at 108), Mach 0.5 / 6 degrees CA 51 % / T 133, Mach 0.8 / 2 degrees CA 43 % / T 182 (CN 33 % / T 56), Mach 0.8 / 6 degrees CA 25 % / T 53, Mach 0.95 / 2 degrees CA 12 %, the controls Mach 1.1 and 1.3 2 to 3 %; the window means drifted by 0.1 to 8 % (CA, Mach 0.5, 2 degrees: -7.9 %) over 4000 iterations. The 50-iteration samples of the range rule alias periods of 50 iterations and never see a range below 0.2 % of an oscillation of 12 to 120 %, so those points ran to the cap. The rule: the runner feeds the forces `[CA, CN, CN x_cp]` every `MEAN_EVERY` = 10 iterations from `mean_from` (`StopRule3::wants_forces` / `observe`, the CPU runner does it in `run3_on`; the GPU runner has to read the forces back every 10 iterations, not every 50 `SYNC` chunk, or the periods of 50 alias again); at each sample the means over the last `mean_window` iterations and over the window before it are compared for CA and, at incidence (a half domain with `alpha` non-zero), CN and the moment about the nose: `|m1 - m0| <= mean_tol |m| + 1e-4` for all of them, both windows after `mean_from`, 8 samples each (`window_means`). When they agree and neither the residual rule nor the range rule fired, the run ends `StopReason::MeanStationary` and **the reported CA, CN and `x_cp` are the means of the second window** (`x_cp = <CN x_cp> / <CN>`), `PointResult3.window: Option<WindowMean { end, length, ca, cn, x_cp, oscillation }>` has the window means whenever a full window was sampled, also for a `MaxIterations` run, and `oscillation` (largest relative half range of the three over the window) is the size of the oscillation for an unsteady-wake warning. Defaults: Euler (`Numerics3::auto`) window 1500 from 1000 (the earliest stop is iteration 4000), `mean_tol` 1e-2; viscous window 2000 from 2000, `mean_tol` 5e-3 (unmeasured). **Replay of `app-integrate`'s raw GPU histories** (`/tmp/wake-csv/M*.csv`, every 10 iterations from 3010 to 7000, so the earliest stop is 4510): window 1500, tol 1e-2 stops at iteration 5090 (Mach 0.5, 2 degrees: CA 0.2394 against the means of 0.2409 / 0.2267 over 3000-5000 / 5000-7000, a mean that drifts by 7.9 %: nothing stops it earlier honestly, `oscillation` 3.0 is CN and the report says so), 4840 (Mach 0.5, 6 degrees: CA 0.2503 vs 0.2510 over 3000-7000, CN 0.2155 vs 0.2152), 6140 (Mach 0.8, 2 degrees: CA 0.1877 vs 0.1890, CN 0.0766 vs 0.0764), 4620 (Mach 0.8, 6 degrees: 0.2325 vs 0.2324), 4640 (Mach 0.95: 0.1733 vs 0.1734), 4580 (Mach 1.1 / 6 degrees and 1.3 / 2 degrees: 0.3486 / 0.3486 vs 0.3485), all inside the 8000 cap; tol 5e-3 misses Mach 0.5 / 2 degrees and Mach 0.8 / 2 degrees inside 7000, a window of 1000 stops at 5390 / 4150 with CA +0.8 / +1.9 % off the long mean, a window of 2000 stops later (6530 at Mach 0.5, 2 degrees); **CPU f64 histories** of the finless example body on the coarser nk = 12 half domain at 2 degrees, Euler (every 10 iterations to 4000; CA swings 0.16 to 0.29 about 0.236 at Mach 0.5, 0.15 to 0.25 about 0.209 at Mach 0.8): window 1500 / tol 1e-2 fires at iteration 4000 at Mach 0.8 (CA 0.2092 against the 2500-4000 mean of 0.2092, CN 0.1170) and not by 4000 at Mach 0.5 (the 1000-2500 and 2500-4000 means 0.2424 and 0.2361 differ by 2.6 %); window 1000 fires at 3470 / 3030 with CA 0.2381 / 0.2108; a window of 1000 is too short for the 222-iteration period (the mean of a window that is not a whole number of cycles is off by `A / (pi N)` of the amplitude, 2 % for 4.5 cycles of 28 %). Synthetic histories of the measured amplitudes and periods (CA at 222 and 108, CN at 108 on a start-up transient of 600 iterations): stop at iteration 4990 with CA within 0.7 %, CN within 0.1 %, `x_cp` within 0.01 cal of the true means; with a drift of 4 % per 1000 iterations no stop (the means of consecutive windows differ by 6 %).

29. **Diameter steps (S13, `body_grid::Segment::step`, `BaseBody::with_step`).** A step of the radius by `dr` is a segment `width` long with slope `dr / width`, a chamfer; a slope of at least `STEP_SLOPE` = 1 (45 degrees; boattails and flares stay far below) makes `Segment::is_step()` true, and `sting_grid` / `base_grid` give such a segment exactly one cell (never the 4-cell minimum of a cone, never clustered). `width` should be the axial size of the cells next to the step (a chamfer much narrower than its neighbours is a jump of cell size that the solver sees: the wall rows follow the chamfer, the radial lines stay vertical, so the cells above the step are sheared parallelograms, positive volumes at every `j` as the tests check, forward and backward, sting and base-wake grids, wedge and half domain, levels 0 and 1). Fins refuse a root that crosses a step (`Error::Unsupported` naming the segment and its `x` range); fins on the tube behind a step build. The coefficients refer to the largest cross-section as before (`nose_to_largest_area` takes the largest station). **Not done:** the gate against Hoerner / NASA step data (a forward-facing step is a ring of area `pi (r2^2 - r1^2)` at the local pitot pressure, a backward step a base): the wedge runs of the stepped bodies were not made (the CPU was busy with the G-J and Arcas runs); the cell-skew limit of the plan ("reject steps taller than x") is the caller's: a step of 0.15 d over 0.1 d (56 degrees) builds at both levels.

30. **After the phys3d merge (`920da8c`): G-V3, G-J, Arcas fins-off on the CPU through `Case3::wedge_of` (f64, Block lines, the 2D grids and numerics, medium for the Arcas and TP-2005 cases).** *G-V3* g3-cpu with the plain i-wall state: `cd_total` 0.213277 vs 0.213002 (+0.13 %), `cd_friction` 0.090042 vs 0.090032 (+0.01 %), `cd_base` 0.122855 vs 0.122572 (+0.23 %), `y+max` 0.649, 2750 iterations (2D 4000). *G-J* (`pb / p_inf`, band 0.02, jet through `Case::motor` -> `Case3.jet` -> `Solver3::set_jet`): M 2.0 Me 1.69 0.912273 vs 0.912295, M 2.0 Me 1.97 0.825915 vs 0.825311, M 2.0 Me 2.53 0.680483 vs 0.680268, M 2.5 Me 1.69 0.748046 vs 0.748001, M 2.5 Me 1.97 0.683742 vs 0.683670, M 2.5 Me 2.53 0.587671 vs 0.587628: all PASS, within 0.001 of the 2D solver and the last five (14 000 iterations in 2D): M 1.65 Me 1.69 1.054720 vs 1.052957, Me 1.97 0.972000 vs 0.974093, Me 2.33 0.840019 vs 0.837859, Me 2.53 0.782455 vs 0.780554, and M 2.0 Me 2.33 0.719840 vs 0.719958: **all eleven TP-2005 cases PASS, within 0.0022**. *G-V4 Arcas fins-off* (`cd_solver_total` against the 2D solver on the same medium grid, band 2 %): M 0.60 0.206310 vs 0.206253, 0.80 0.206809 vs 0.206760, 1.20 0.387960 vs 0.387883, 1.50 0.352823 vs 0.352739, 1.80 0.323705 vs 0.323623, 2.30 0.283666 vs 0.283594, 2.96 0.240850 vs 0.240810, and the transonic ones (band 3 %) M 0.90 0.236927 vs 0.236896, M 0.95 0.278734 vs 0.278702, M 1.00 0.390896 vs 0.390683: **all ten Arcas fins-off points PASS** to 0.01 to 0.06 % (friction to 0.02 %, base to 0.2 %), 1750 to 2700 iterations (2D 4000). *Lift tier with the fin stack* (ANF, double-wedge fins, Mach 2, 300 iterations, 191 x 80 x 78 cells): friction body 0.0712 (0.99 of the flat plate), fins 0.0315 (0.99), residual -2.2 orders, `y+` body 51.6, fin sides **106** (designed 60: the stack grows from the root chord, not from the local `Re`), tips 4754 (not resolved, excluded from `y_plus_max`), CA 0.4778, CN 0.3804, `x_cp` 7.59 cal; 1834 s on a loaded machine (2.4 times the cells of the uniform azimuth).
