they run on the wedge after the flip); `bowshock-gpu-cases`: `src/{cone,ogive,run}.rs` (512) and `tests/{explicit,implicit,implicit_lowmach,loops,runs,viscous}.rs` (1187) and `tests/common/mod.rs` (52) are the 2D GPU runners and their GPU-vs-CPU tests: deleted, replaced by `euler3d.rs`, `s2.rs` and gpu3d's viscous 3D GPU-vs-CPU test; `src/wedge3.rs` stays (its `Case` / `Wake` use: `Analysis::wedge_cases`); `bowshock-validate`: `src/bin/{cone_gpu,ogive_gpu,subsonic_gpu,transonic_gpu}.rs` (1639) delete (the GPU wedge runner `GpuRunner3` with `--wedge` replaces them), `src/bin/{cone,ogive}.rs` become wedge-only (`run_cone_wedge`, `run_ogive_wedge` stay, the 2D runners go: `bowshock-cases::cone::{build_solver, run_cone}`, the ogive twins, which need a plain `Meridian` state instead of the 2D `Solver` for the post-processing), `src/bin/transonic.rs` and `src/bin/subsonic.rs` **are wedge-only now** (8a16940: sweep, Stoney, Czarnecki-Monta, Fox TM X-2439, Euler vs RANS and the low-Mach study run through `Case3` + `bowshock_cases::wedge::run_wedge_point`; `--scheme explicit`, `--drop`, `--cd-tol`, `--outflow` are gone), `examples/{bench,bench_viscous,cfl_scan,eval_loop,luma_issues,perfcheck}.rs` (984: `bench3` replaces `bench` / `bench_viscous`; `cfl_scan`, `eval_loop`, `luma_issues`, `perfcheck` are 2D-solver instruments: dropped, the Luma issues they reproduce are in ISSUES.md), `examples/ref2d.rs` (929, the generator of `ref2d.json`: it needs the 2D solver; delete it and say in the fixture header that it was generated at the commit before the deletion), `bowshock-cases`: the 2D tests of `subsonic.rs` / `transonic.rs` / `cone.rs` / `ogive.rs` that exercise the 2D solver (scheme-independence, solid-block first residual, far-field preservation, minmod 200 x 100: an estimate of 450 lines) go with it; the data modules (`stoney`, `czarnecki_monta`, `fox_tm_x2439`, `tangent_cone_cd`) stay; `examples/{animate,diag}.rs` (363) use the 2D `Solver`: dropped. *(e) app (`crates/bowshock`, app-integrate):* `analyze/cfd/fields.rs::from_2d`, `analyze/cfd/run.rs` (`on_field`, `field::SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL`), `analyze/flow_view/{prepared,axi}.rs` (`Field`, `Wake`), `analyze/solve_demo.rs::paint`, and the six `field::GAMMA` sites -> `field3::GAMMA`. *(f) docs that still describe the 2D solver:* `crates/bowshock-cfd/README.md` (34 references in 1298 lines: the solver chapters, G1-G3 and the cone / ogive / transonic / subsonic tables), `HANDOFF.md`, `ISSUES.md` (4), `crates/bowshock-validate/README.md`, `ROADMAP.md` (3), `crates/bowshock/src/ARCHITECTURE.md` (2), `guide/src/{sweeps,results,motor}.md` (7): each says what ran on the 2D solver; rewrite them to the wedge numbers (this series' gate lines) with the 2D tables kept as frozen reference data, `SOLVER_REV` 7. *Line count of the final commit* (whole files counted, the partial ones estimated): 6 804 (cfd whole) + 850 (cfd partial) + 1 471 (core whole) + 2 000 (core partial, tests, analysis legacy route) + 1 751 (gpu-cases) + 3 700 (validate: 1 639 GPU bins, 984 examples, 929 `ref2d`, the 2D branches of four bins) + 800 (cases: tests and examples) = **about 17 400 lines removed**, plus the rewritten docs.

32. **Cell budget and the Draft tier (measured: `tests/grid_census.rs`, `tests/draft_tier.rs`; CPU f64, example body: ogive 3 d nose + 12 d tube, `Re_c` 1.756e6, smooth walls).** *Where the cells go* (default `Coarse` sector grids, all numbers at grid level 0): finless wedge of the example body 156 x 140 x 1 = **21 840** cells (15 576 fluid): solid body block 4 752 (21.8 %) + dead cells upstream of the tip 1 512 (6.9 %), fluid upstream of the tip within 2 L 7.8 %, along the body 31.0 %, in the wake within 2 L 20.4 %, **beyond 2 body lengths 12.1 %** (only 12 of 156 columns and 9 of 140 rows: the far field is cheap); rows: `n_gap` 54 + `nj_out` 86, the 54 gap rows (wall-resolved first cell 6e-6 d at the base corner, growth 1.2) are solid under the whole body. Example rocket, 4 fins, **sector 191 x 140 x 31 = 828 940** (fluid 69.3 %, solid body block 24.8 %, dead 5.7 %, fin boxes 0.2 %; fin stack 31 azimuthal cells), **half domain `Lift` tier 191 x 94 x 98 = 1.76 M** (fluid 73.0 %). G-F1 case (ANF body, 4 thin fins): sector 196 x 128 x 27 = **677 376** (the number `phys3d` reported; DESIGN3D planned 333-443 k; fluid 68.7 %, solid body 25.5 %, dead 5.6 %), half domain `Lift` 196 x 84 x 82 = 1.35 M. ANF default fins sector 191 x 128 x 27 = 660 096. So **28-31 % of every dense array is never updated** (solid body block + dead cells), the fin boxes are 0.1-0.3 %. *Levers on the finless wedge* (cells, one lever alone): domain 5 L / 3 L instead of 10 L / 5 L 21 216 (-2.9 %; the geometric rows absorb the distance); axial counts x 0.6 (n_up 28, n_nose 48, n_seg 40, n_wake 40 -> 18 / 30 / 24 / 24, tip cell 0.05 d) 13 440 (-38 %); body wall functions (first cell at y+ 60, the `Lift` spacing 3.7e-4 d: rows 140 -> 94) 14 664 (-33 %); gap rows from 0.01 d at the base corner instead of the wall spacing (gap 54 -> 14 rows, so the solid block 21.8 % -> 10 %) 15 600 (-29 %); all four = **`Tier::Draft` 96 x 76 = 7 296 cells (-67 %)**; with the fin stack at growth 1.35 / 1.6 and 20 cells over the root chord: example sector **125 x 76 x 21 = 199 500 (-76 %)**, half domain 125 x 76 x 82 = 779 000 (-56 %). *Accuracy of the cut* (finless example wedge, CD total): **Mach 2.0** `Coarse` 0.35788 (3 200 iterations, 450 s, 141 ms per iteration) against `Draft` 0.36012 (**+0.6 %**, 1 400 iterations, 104 s, 74 ms), `Draft` without the coarse gap rows 0.35565 (-0.6 %), `Draft` with the wall spacing sized for the case's own Mach number (y+ 30 / 60) 0.35496 / 0.35499 (-0.8 %), friction 0.0977 vs 0.0970 (+0.8 %), `Lift` (full axial grid, y+ 48) 0.36345 (+1.6 %). **Mach 0.5** `Coarse` 0.27020 (5 250 iterations CdStationary, 880 s, friction 0.15546, base 0.11324) against `Draft` 0.25629 (**-5.1 %**, MeanStationary after 6 000 iterations: the coarse gap rows let the wake go unsteady, base 0.0970), `Draft` with the wall spacing sized for Mach 3 (y+ 5 at Mach 0.5) and fine gap rows 0.28089 (+3.9 %, 1 500 iterations, 108 s), the same with the full 10 L / 5 L domain and the full 40 wake columns 0.27646 (**+2.3 %**, 10 976 cells, 144 s), full domain alone 0.26508 (-1.9 %, MeanStationary), full wake alone 0.25486 (-5.7 %), `Lift` 0.29711 (+10 %, MeanStationary); friction is within 1.5 % in every variant (0.1575 vs 0.1555 at y+ 5 to 40). **So the cut is within 1 % at Mach 2, and within 2-4 % at Mach 0.5 only when the wake stays steady; the subsonic base wake of this body is at the edge of shedding, and the coarse gap rows / coarse wake push it over (the mean of the cycle is 5 % low): `Draft` is a supersonic / transonic tier, and below Mach 0.8 it needs the fine gap rows and the full domain (about 11 000 cells, -50 %).** The 3 % target is met at Mach 2 (0.6 %) and at Mach 0.5 with the safe variant (2.3 %), not with the default levers at Mach 0.5. Time: `Draft` iterates at 66-74 ms against 141-168 ms per iteration, and needs 1 400 against 3 200 iterations at Mach 2: 104 s against 450 s (4.3 x). *The solid body block:* the GPU computes the dense array; 28-31 % of its cells (`Coarse`) and 13-15 % (`Draft`) are solid or dead. Meshing only the fluid is a multi-block change, not a mesher switch: the fluid is the union of A = rows `j >= j_off` over every column and B = the wake columns `i >= i_end` below `j_off` (the gap), two dense rectangles that touch along `j = j_off` for `i >= i_end`; every `j` stencil, every `j` line (the columns of the wake cross A and B), the ghost layers, the axis map, the wall-face lists, the force integration, the viewer export (`Grid3::cell` is a global index everywhere) and every GPU kernel (the solid mask, `SolidBox`) would see two arrays with halo exchange; the saving is the solid share (28-31 % of the cost at best, 20-25 % after the halos and the extra launches), the effort about two weeks for the CPU and GPU solvers together. **The cheap part is already taken by the gap lever** (rows below the body surface 54 -> 14 at the Draft spacing, solid share 21.8 % -> 10 %, no solver change). *Grid sequencing mesher part (S9):* `Grid3Params::coarsened()` halves every axial and radial count (the azimuth with its fin stack is kept) and `engine/sequence3.rs` has `prolong` (the parent's state to its four children) and `restrict` (the volume-weighted mean); the test shows that for the finless wedges (Coarse, Draft, Euler) every fluid child has a fluid parent (the body surface, base plane and axis fall on even indices) and `restrict(prolong(u)) = u` to round-off: the grid at 78 x 70 (Coarse) / 48 x 38 (Draft) is the first sequence level. **The finned grids do not pair** (191 x 140 -> 104 x 70: the fin fans ahead of the leading edge and behind the trailing edge have a free length, `plan_fins`, 25-27 columns that are not halved): pairing needs an even fan length and an even chord count (about one day in `plan_fins`: round `m` up to even and re-solve the growth); until then a finned run can be started two levels down only on its finless wedge (the body / base flow), not on the sector. *What is not done:* the restriction / prolongation inside a run loop (start the fine run from the prolonged state with the first-order phase kept), the coarse-level iteration counts (the finless Coarse wedge needs 3 200-5 250 iterations at 141-168 ms: a start two levels down would pay for them at a sixteenth of the cells).

33. **G-F4 (Arcas fins on / off, GPU Coarse tier RANS): the missing supersonic fin increment is the blunt trailing edge of the real fins, not the grid, the reference or the solver.** The probe (`tests/arcas_probe.rs`; the Euler CPU sector, Arcas body 18.2 d, four fins of the modelled planform, C_A without the base on the body cross-section, 400-600 iterations) for the modelled `DoubleWedge` section (sharp leading and trailing edge) and for a flat-back section (wedge leading edge over 40 % of the chord, constant thickness to a blunt trailing edge, `Custom { [0,0], [0.4,1], [1,1] }`, the same 0.123 in maximum thickness): fins off 0.1706 / 0.1424 / 0.1135 (Mach 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.3); fins on `DoubleWedge` 0.2179 / 0.1754 / 0.1337 (pressure increment **+0.0473 / +0.0330 / +0.0202**, of which fin sides +0.0569 / +0.0405 / +0.0254, the body's aft pressure falls by 0.011 / 0.009 / 0.007); fins on flat-back 0.2936 / 0.2310 / 0.1692 (increment **+0.1230 / +0.0886 / +0.0557**, the trailing-edge faces +0.1288 / +0.0915 / +0.0564). *Flat-plate friction of the four fins* (facade model, Niskanen 3.78-3.81, mean chord 2.77 in, Re/m 9.84e6, wetted area 46.65 in^2 = 11.73 body cross-sections): **0.0467 / 0.0440 / 0.0393**. `gpu3d`'s viscous increments are exactly Euler `DoubleWedge` plus friction: Mach 1.5 0.0926 = 0.0473 + 0.0467 (0.0940), Mach 1.8 0.0770 = 0.0330 + 0.0440 (0.0770): the RANS fin friction is the flat-plate friction, so friction is not what is missing; the missing part is pressure drag that a sharp-trailing-edge fin does not have. With the flat-back section the increment is Euler + friction = **0.170 / 0.133 / 0.095** against the data **0.165 / 0.116 / 0.091** (+3 % / +15 % / +4 %), and the fins-on value `gpu3d`'s fins-off RANS + that increment = 0.3243 + 0.170 = **0.494** (data 0.505, -2.2 %) at Mach 1.5, 0.289 + 0.133 = 0.422 (0.438, -3.7 %) at Mach 1.8, 0.248 + 0.095 = 0.343 (0.349, -1.7 %) at Mach 2.3: inside the 15 % band everywhere and inside 4 %. **Verdict: physics / geometry of the model, not the grid** (the increments of `DoubleWedge` are what the solver gives for that section and the friction is the flat-plate value; chordwise resolution, the sharp-edge floor, the tip and the fin y+ do not change a sum that equals Euler + flat plate), **not the reference** (the data are `C_A,corr`, the axial force with the base-annulus force removed, which keeps the fin trailing-edge faces: `drag_parts.total - base - base friction` does the same), and **not a solver error**; what is wrong is the fin: D-4014 Fig. 1(b) is read in `bowshock-reference::arcas` as a mean thickness 0.123 in of a section the model draws as a double wedge, but the data say the section has a blunt trailing edge (a wedge or round leading edge and a flat back), whose base drag is +0.076 / +0.056 / +0.036 of C_A at those Mach numbers. The transonic INFO points (8-23 % low) are the same missing term (the blunt-edge base drag grows towards Mach 1). The residual: fins off is 4-5 % low in the supersonic points (Mach 1.5 0.3243 vs 0.340, Mach 2.3 0.248 vs 0.258): the carborundum transition strips of the model (roughness of the nose) are not modelled and the body friction is smooth. *No band changes.* To close it: give `arcas_fin_spec` a section with a flat back (`FinProfile::Custom` as above, or a `Wedge` profile with a blunt trailing edge in the spec) and re-run G-F4 on the GPU; the Mach 1.8 increment would then be 0.133 against 0.116 (+15 %, inside the 30 % band); the shape (leading-edge wedge length) is a reading of the figure and should be looked at in the report.

34. **G-F4, the source read: the fin section is a double wedge (the flat-back inference of item 33 is refuted).** The reports were fetched from NTRS (`/tmp/d4013.pdf`, `/tmp/d4014.pdf`, scanned: read as images): NASA TN D-4014 p. 4: "The model consisted of a cylindrical centerbody, a boattailed afterbody, and **trapezoidal double-wedge fins**. The afterbody boattail ended with a reflexed lip"; Fig. 1(b): fin section 0.150 in thick at the root, 0.096 in at the tip, wedge half angle 2.72 degrees (so the section is sharp at both edges: t/c = 2 tan 2.72 = 0.095 normal to the 15 degree line), fin anchor tabs 0.220 x 2.165 x 0.035 in; both reports: carborundum strips on the nose and 1/2 in aft of each fin's leading edge; D-4013 p. 4: the model had a 1 in diameter sting 11.5 in aft of the base, and axial force was not corrected to free-stream conditions at the base except for the summary data at roll 0, alpha 0; D-4014 Fig. 4: the chamber axial-force coefficient at alpha 0 is 0.02-0.04 (fins off) and 0.03-0.04 (fins on, 0 degree cant), sign not stated. So **`arcas_fin_spec` keeps the `DoubleWedge` section** (its doc comment quotes the source) and the flat-back probe of item 33 stays a probe: it shows what a blunt trailing edge would do, not what the model has. What the double wedge plus flat-plate friction misses at Mach 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.3 (0.075 / 0.050 / 0.040 of C_A against the data increments 0.165 / 0.116 / 0.091) is **not explained**; the candidates, none tested: the fin anchors at the roots (forward-facing steps of 0.035 in: a stagnation estimate 0.01 of C_A), the strips on the fins (roughness drag a few per cent of the fin friction), the 1 in sting and the base flow it changes (the fins-on and fins-off base pressures differ and the chamber correction differs with it: Fig. 4 gives 0.01 more with fins), the sign of the chamber correction (a flip moves fins on and fins off by 0.06 each, the increment by 0.02), the spanwise thickness taper (the mean-t^2 is the same), the reading uncertainty of the figures (+-0.012). The gate stays as it is; the G-F4 fins-on and increment lines at Mach 1.5 and 1.8 fail by -17 % / -16 % on the GPU Coarse tier and are reported as such.

35. **Cost study on the CPU (phys3d, 2026-11-18): `sub_iters`, the wedge stop rule, frozen per-step work, a j-block hybrid.** Harness: `tests/viscous3.rs::cost_point` (`POINT` = `tab-<mach>` the RANS-table body, ogive 3 d + tube 12 d, ISA 120 m, roughness 6e-5 m, grid sized for the point's Mach, or `g3-<mach>`; `SUB`, `CFL`, `ITERS`), the wedge with the 2D numerics through `build3`, level 0; it runs the engine's `StopRule3` and, on the same history, `run::stop_reason` of the 2D facade (`drag_rel_tol` 2e-3), and prints `HIST iteration CD CA residual` every 50 iterations. Iterations to the 3D rule (5e-4 over 1000) / to the 2D rule (2e-3 over 500, 2 orders of drop), `C_D` at the 3D stop, ms per iteration (2 threads, loaded machine):

    | point | sub 1 | sub 2 | sub 3 | 2D fixture |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | tab M 0.1 | 2050 / 1350, 0.35959, 88 ms | ~1950 / ~1300, 0.36424, 111 | ~1950 / ~1250, 0.36378, 130 | 1200, 0.35844 |
    | tab M 0.9 | 3900 / 1850, 0.35628, 100 | 3900 / 1850, 0.35626, 126 | 3900 / 1800, 0.35625, 149 | 1850 |
    | tab M 2.3 | 3400 / 1650, 0.37541, 94 | 3400 / 1650, 0.37552, 118 | 3350 / 1650, 0.37552, 142 | 1650 |
    | G3 M 0.5 | 2900 / 2050, 0.21328, 97 | 2800 / 1950, 0.21328, 116 | 2750 / 1900, 0.21328, 138 | |
    | G3 M 1.5 | 2850 / 1800, 0.37937, 93 | 2850 / 1800, 0.37927, 113 | 2850 / 1800, 0.37936, 135 | |

    (The `~` entries are read from the history offline: the 2D rule's residual-drop condition never held for sub 2 and 3 at Mach 0.1, whose residual stalls at 4.1e-2 against 2.9e-3 for sub 1; that is also why their `C_D` sits 1.5 % above the 2D value, item 27's +1.50 % (nose -0.0079 vs +0.0024), while sub 1 is 0.3 % above it.) **(1) `sub_iters`:** iterations equal within 5 % and `C_D` within 0.03 % (Mach 0.1 aside) at 0.66 to 0.71 of the time per iteration, so the **wedge** takes 1 (`Numerics3::from_2d`). **The finned grids keep 3** (`Numerics3::viscous`): the small finned sector (short body, 4 fins, 324k cells, Mach 0.5, `cfl_max` 50, `fin_sector_runs`) with 1 went non-finite at iteration 370 (residual 80, 260 at 300, 1e48), 170 iterations after the CFL ramp reached 50, with 3 it held 80 over iterations 200 to 300; at Mach 2 (`cfl_max` 20) 1 and 3 agree to 450 iterations (590 to 670 ms per iteration against 830 to 940). **(2) Why 39 100 iterations against 20 950:** the stop rule; `from_2d` took the 3D viscous rule and ignored the case's `drag_rel_tol`. The 3D rule fires at 2050 / 3900 / 3400 and the 2D rule on the same history at 1350 / 1850 / 1650 for Mach 0.1 / 0.9 / 2.3 (fixture 1200 / 1850 / 1650), `C_D` 0.0 to 0.24 % apart; `from_2d` now carries `drag_rel_tol` into `rel_tol`, `window` and the minimum iterations (500). **(3) Frozen per-step work** (temporary `Cadence` knobs, not kept; wedge, sub 1, Mach 0.9 and 2.3): the diffusive spectral radii of the faces every 4th iteration, or the wall state every 4th: the same iterations and `C_D`, no time beyond the load noise (92 vs 100 ms, 91 vs 94 ms); both together non-finite at iteration 250 at Mach 0.9; the SST update every 2nd iteration (advancing with the skipped time): the same iterations, 4 to 9 % less time, every 4th non-finite at 250 at Mach 2.3 (fine at 0.9); the block factors of an earlier iteration: every 2nd fine at Mach 0.9 (the same iterations, 7 % less time) and non-finite at 250 at Mach 2.3, every 4th non-finite at 250 and 200. The preconditioner blocks are assembled once per step and shared by the sub-iterations: no knob. Nothing worth the start-up risk on the CPU. **(4) Block along `j`, diagonal along `i` (and `k`)** (temporary `ImplicitParams::j_block`, not kept: the diagonal factors followed by the block `j` line, `P` applied before it, the defect pass of `DiagonalCorrected`): wedge Mach 0.9 / 2.3 the same iterations (3900 / 3450) and `C_D` within 0.08 %, but 161 to 168 ms per iteration against 100 / 94 for the block scheme with 1 (both factor sets are assembled and the defect needs the block operator); on the sector at Mach 0.5 its residual left the block runs' at iteration 250 (314 against 80) and reached 1750 with a density of 0.74 at 300. No gain.

36. **Re-verification through `Analysis::wedge_cases` after the phys3d merge (CPU f64, merged build 6d2f11a; the wedge now also carries the roughness of the analysis on the body).** G-V3 g3-cpu `cd_total` 0.213278 vs 0.213002 (+0.13 %), friction 0.090043 vs 0.090032 (+0.01 %), base 0.122853 vs 0.122572 (+0.23 %), PASS. G-V4 table-rans (smooth walls of the fixture's 6e-5 m roughness now applied in 3D): M 0.10 0.359591 vs 0.358435 (+0.32 %, 2050 iterations), M 0.90 0.356282 vs 0.357142 (-0.24 %, 3900), M 2.30 0.375416 vs 0.376383 (-0.26 %, 3400), PASS. Arcas fins-off M 0.60 0.206313 vs 0.206253, M 1.00 0.390907 vs 0.390683, PASS. G-J M 2.5 Me 2.53 pb/pinf 0.587610 vs 0.587628, PASS. G-V4 table-euler M 0.90 0.173815 vs 0.174802 (-0.56 %, 850 iterations against 13 500 in 2D), PASS. **Two NonFinite with `sub_iters` 1 on a supersonic medium-level wedge** (Arcas fins-off M 2.30: 250 iterations; TP-2005 M 1.65 Me 1.69): with `sub_iters` 3 Arcas M 2.30 is 0.283666 vs 0.283594 (PASS, as before); the rule is now `sub_iters` 1 only on a wedge below Mach 1 (`Case3::with_tier`, `Numerics3::from_2d` of phys3d). **`table-euler-M0.50`:** the 2D reference stopped on the iteration cap (10 000, a phase of a cycling wake) and the wedge stops on the mean-stationary rule at 4 010 iterations with a window mean CA 0.1088 (oscillation 0.47); the runner with `BOWSHOCK_MEAN_WINDOW=0` runs to the cap and its last sample is 0.130622 against 0.133290 (-2.0 %): like with like agrees to the band, a mean against a sample does not. The gate now reports an inviscid case whose 2D stop is `MaxIterations`, or whose wedge stop is the mean rule, as INFO (not gated), as G-W1 does. The last state's parts of a mean-stopped run are one phase, not the mean (`PointResult3.window` has the means).

37. **The Arcas supersonic fin increment: an attempt to explain it (grid, data, test article, second data set).** *Is it the grid?* No. Euler sector at Mach 1.5 (`tests/arcas_study.rs`, C_A without base, fins off 0.1706): default 0.2179 (increment +0.0473), 64 instead of 32 cells over the root chord 0.2203 (+0.0497), 48 radial cells over the span (tip resolved) 0.2187 (+0.0481), both 0.2214 (+0.0508): at most +0.0035 (7 % of the increment). RANS `Draft` sector on the CPU (y+ 15 on the fins, 20 chordwise cells, 126 x 64 x 14): fins off 0.3230, fins on 0.4182, **increment 0.0952** (fin pressure +0.0540 = sides +0.0527 + edges +0.0013, body aft -0.0120, nose +0.0020, fin friction 0.0491, body friction +0.0022); 40 chordwise cells (150 x 64 x 14): fins on 0.4263, increment 0.1033 (fin sides +0.0584): chordwise resolution moves the increment by 0.008; `gpu3d`'s Coarse GPU run: increment 0.0926. The data increment is 0.165. *The section:* 2.72 degree wedge, so at the first chordwise column (0.014 d from the leading edge, `0.3 x` the mean cell) the section half thickness is 5.1e-4 d against a sharp-edge floor of 5.5e-4 d (2 % of t/2): the floor acts on the first column only, the leading-edge face it leaves is the `edges` term (+0.0014 at Mach 1.5, 0.0013 in RANS), and the first fin-stack cell is 6e-4 d high, so the leading-edge half thickness is about one cell height; the thickness is not meshed by cells (the fin layer between two gap stacks is a solid whose side faces are the node surfaces), it is resolved by the columns: 16 per half chord at the default. The streamwise wedge slope is 0.046 at the root and at the tip (0.150 in over 1.63 in, 0.096 over 1.05 in: the ridge is the 15 degree line, the leading edge the 30 degree one, so the leading-edge-to-ridge distance shrinks to the tip as the thickness does); the model's constant 0.123 in over the tapering chord gives the same `integral c tau^2 dy` to 1.4 %. *The data in its own terms* (D-4014 Fig. 5(a), 5(b), 4, read again from the scans at alpha = 0: raw C_A fins off 0.355 / 0.345 / 0.295 / 0.245, fins on 0.54 / 0.47 / 0.385 / 0.31 at Mach 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.3 / 2.96; the repo's corrected values 0.340 / 0.322 / 0.258 / 0.210 and 0.505 / 0.438 / 0.349 / 0.273 are those minus the chamber term 0.015-0.025 (off) and 0.035 (on): the reading holds to the stated 0.012): data increment **0.165 / 0.116 / 0.091 / 0.063**; Ackeret wave drag of the four double-wedge fins (strip theory, `fin_wave`'s formula with the local `t/c` = 0.0444, planform 5.83 in^2 per fin, 11.7 wetted fin areas per body area): **0.0413 / 0.0309 / 0.0223 / 0.0166**; fully turbulent flat-plate friction of both sides (Niskanen 3.78-3.81, `Re_c` 6.9e5 on the mean chord): **0.0466 / 0.0439 / 0.0393 / 0.0339**; anchors (tab 0.220 x 0.035 in with a 6 degree ramp of 0.330 in and chamfered ends, Fig. 1(b): Ackeret `Cp` 0.19 on 0.0308 in^2 of frontal area, plus about 1.6 in^2 of wetted tab): **about 0.003**; sum **0.091 / 0.078 / 0.065 / 0.054**, **missing 0.074 / 0.038 / 0.026 / 0.009**; the 3D increment is Euler fins + body interference (+0.0473 / +0.0330 / +0.0202, the aft body falls by 0.011 / 0.009 / 0.007) + fin friction (0.0467 / 0.0440 / 0.0393): 0.094 / 0.077 / 0.060: **the 3D solver reproduces wave + friction + anchors (+3 % / -1 % / -8 %), and the data exceed the 3D increment by a factor 1.8 / 1.5 / 1.5**. The missing part is 0.45 / 0.33 / 0.29 / 0.14 of the data increment and falls faster than the wave drag (`1/beta`: factor 2.5 from Mach 1.5 to 2.96; the missing part: 8). Candidates, with what is known: the strips on the fins (1/16 in wide, No. 60 grit, 1/2 in behind the leading edge: the grains stand 0.01 in in a laminar layer of about 0.007 in at 0.5 in; a frontal-area estimate gives 0.005-0.012), the open fin slots of the boattail (D-4013 p. 4: plugged without fins; with fins the slots connect the external flow to the base chamber, which the chamber correction of Fig. 4 does not remove: it is 0.01 larger with fins), the chamber correction itself (sign inferred; a flip moves fins on and off by 0.06 each, the increment by 0.02), the transition strip on the nose (in both). **No component computable from the reports closes more than about 0.02 of the 0.074 at Mach 1.5; the remainder sits in test-article details the model does not have (slot venting, strips) or in the reduction (Fig. 4), not in the 3D solver or its grid.** *Second data set:* item 38 (the Finner numbers first written here, "no shortfall", were wrong: the Euler-sector fin sides they used were under-resolved).

38. **The Army-Navy Finner's fin section (G-F5 +20-27 % high) and the 1.832 point.** *The numbers.* The viscous Lift tier (phys3d, full body, half domain, GPU f32, 8-column trailing-edge taper) gave CD0 = 0.7453 / 0.5900 / 0.5244 / 0.4668 at Mach 1.832 / 2.375 / 2.718 / 3.147 against the table 0.585 / 0.484 / 0.435 / 0.373: +27 / +22 / +21 / +25 %, the two roll phases within 0.4 %. The sector agrees with the half domain where both exist: Mach 1.832, assumed section, Coarse sector (670 k cells, 6 841 it) 0.7321, Fine sector (968 k cells, 12 051 it) 0.7537, Draft sector (156 k cells) 0.7077 / Lift half domain 0.7453; Mach 3.147 Draft sector 0.4509 / Lift 0.4668. Parts at Mach 1.832 (Coarse sector, `C_A` without base 0.5514): nose 0.1111, fin sides +0.2617, fin edges +0.0772 (the blunt trailing edge face and the leading-edge floor), friction body 0.0734 and fins 0.0280, base 0.1807; fins off 0.3595 (nose 0.1101, friction 0.0754, base 0.1740). *Not the data and not the solver at Mach 1.832 alone: the section.* `Case3::anf_fins` carried a section the source does not give, a wedge over the first 0.1 chord (21.8 degrees per side, the shock of which is detached at Mach 1.832, its limit is 20.7 degrees): the fin sides alone are +0.16 .. +0.26 at 1.832 and +0.16 at 3.147 (the frontal area of the sides is fixed by the thickness, 0.08 x span x 2 x 4 fins / (pi/4) = 0.41 body areas, so the side force is 0.41 `Cp(theta)`: 0.13 at 21.8 degrees against 0.03 at the 2.3 degrees of a full-chord wedge at Mach 3.147). *What the sources say:* Dupuis, DRDC TM 2002-136 p. 18 (read second hand, from the optical reading in `nrdptel/fusionspace-eridanus` docs/research/basic-finner-transonic-tables.md, section 2): four "1 cal x 1 cal" wedge fins, 0.08 caliber thick at the trailing edge, leading-edge and nose radius 0.004 caliber; the same note, section 5: BRL Report 711 (1949) p. 29 "wedge fins, 16 % thick" (0.1245 in of 0.786 in); Abiza et al. (3ds.com, p. 3): "four 1x1-caliber fins with sharp leading edges and thicknesses of 0.08 calibers at the trailing edge"; Shelton et al. (NASA 20180006293): leading-edge fillet r/d 0.005, trailing edge 0.1 d. So the section is a **wedge over the full chord** (half angle 2.3 degrees per side) with a blunt trailing edge of 0.08 cal and a leading edge of radius 0.004 cal, i.e. half thickness 0.004 = 10 % of t/2. *Euler sector, CPU* (`STUDY_LE=0.999`, `STUDY_Z0`; the pressure drag including base; the friction added from the Draft RANS runs: body 0.076 / 0.066 / 0.060 / 0.055, fins 0.028 / 0.026 / 0.024 / 0.022): full-chord wedge, sharp leading edge (floor 2 % of t/2): total 0.4658 / 0.3505 / 0.3051 / 0.2610 (fin sides +0.017 / +0.012 / +0.011 / +0.009, edges +0.125 / +0.076 / +0.062 / +0.051), CD0 0.570 / 0.443 / 0.389 / 0.338 = -3 / -8 / -11 / -9 % of the table; a leading edge of half thickness 0.25 t/2 (2.5 times the radius): 0.6374 / 0.4893 / 0.4301 / 0.3826, the leading-edge face adding 0.176 / 0.148 / 0.133 / 0.123; scaled to the sourced 0.1 t/2 (x0.4, and about 0.6 of a flat face for a round one) it adds about 0.04 / 0.035 / 0.032 / 0.03: CD0 about 0.61 / 0.48 / 0.42 / 0.37 = +4 / -1 / -3 / -1 % of the table. The old Euler numbers of item 37 (CD0 0.6137 ... 0.2981 for the 0.1-chord bevel, "no shortfall") were pressure totals with fin sides that the 8-cell azimuthal Euler sector cannot resolve (0.05 against 0.16 on the stack grids): disregard that paragraph. *The change:* `Case3::anf_fins` now carries the sourced section, `[[0, 0.1], [0.5, 0.55], [1, 1]]`, and the mesher's floor of a blunt edge is `BLUNT_EDGE_FLOOR` = 10 % of t/2 (it was 25 %: it inflated a 0.1 t/2 edge 2.5 times; `half_thickness` test adjusted). Consequences: `G-L1` (CN_alpha +8 % high with the 0.1-chord bevel, 13 % band) and `G-F5` have to be re-run with it (phys3d / the GPU jobs `solver3d-anf-le2`); *RANS with the sourced section* (Coarse sector, 670 k cells, GPU f32, 4 800-6 530 iterations, `STUDY_LE=0.999`): total CD0 (base included) with the sourced leading edge (`Z0 = 0.1`) **0.5137 / 0.4185 / 0.3761 / 0.3308** at Mach 1.832 / 2.375 / 2.718 / 3.147 against the table 0.585 / 0.484 / 0.435 / 0.373: **-12.2 / -13.5 / -13.5 / -11.3 %**; with a sharp leading edge (floor 2 % of t/2) 0.4966 / 0.4038 / 0.3617 / 0.3153 (-15 / -17 / -17 / -15 %). Parts at 1.832 / 3.147 (`Z0 = 0.1`): nose 0.1111 / 0.0879, fin sides +0.0145 / +0.0081, fin edges (trailing-edge face, leading-edge face) +0.1106 / +0.0615, friction body 0.0744 / 0.0527 and fins 0.0270 / 0.0182, base 0.1761 / 0.1025. The leading-edge radius is worth +0.017 / +0.015 (CD0, 3 %); the Euler estimate above (-3..+4 %) was optimistic by about 12 points because RANS has less base suction and trailing-edge face drag than Euler (Mach 1.832, sharp edge: base 0.177 against 0.215, fin edges 0.083 against 0.125). So **the 1.832 point is not an outlier any more: with the sourced section the four points sit uniformly 11-14 % below the table** (with the assumed bevel they sat 20-27 % above). What the section does not contain: the 2.0 degree cant of the tested fins (DRDC TM 2002-136 p. 18: "all fins were canted 2.0 degrees"; a lift-induced drag of about `sum(fin area) / Sref x 4 / beta x delta^2` = 0.016 / 0.008 at Mach 1.832 / 3.147, 3 and 2 % of CD0), the tunnel / range Reynolds numbers (the case runs the sea-level atmosphere at the table's Mach number) and the unknown provenance of the table itself (not in the repository). The G-F5 lines above Mach 1 print `FAIL KNOWN DESIGN3D-L-item-38`. Device memory (the third item of the same turn): process peak of the Coarse sector (670 410 cells) in f32 **2 922 MiB** (4.4 kB per cell; the persistent arrays of gpu3d's ledger are 412 MiB / 638 B per cell, the rest, 2.4 GiB, is the transient buffers of the compiled step that Luma keeps one set of per recorded plan), df32 4 347 MiB; Draft sector (156 k cells) 810 / 1 179 MiB; Euler sector (116 k) 323 / 389 MiB; the finless Coarse wedge (20 k) 175 / 201 MiB (the desktop's 2 327 MiB not counted). The 5.2-5.6 GiB seen with `bench3 --mem` come from several recorded plans in one process (each holds ~2.4 GiB): the product path (one `GpuRunner3`, f32, one compiled loop) is 2.9 GiB; the 4 GB promise holds in f32, df32 breaks it. The cheapest reduction is Luma's (reuse of dead intermediates inside one recording, about -2 GiB), then a runner dropped before the next is built (a second live plan costs +2.4 GiB).

39. **G-S: the sector is a valid shortcut for zero-incidence drag (viscous, GPU, 1 % band).** The ANF with the sourced section, Coarse stack grids (sector 670 k cells; the half domain of the same body and fins, `Between` for N = 4, `InPlane` for N = 3), RANS, GPU f32, `cfl_max` 10, Mach 0.8 and 2: total CD0 sector / half domain / difference: N = 4, Mach 0.8 **0.51055 / 0.51470 / -0.81 %** (9 940 / 8 370 it, both mean stationary); N = 4, Mach 2 **0.48204 / 0.48590 / -0.79 %** (6 940 / 6 350 it); N = 3, Mach 0.8 **0.44217 / 0.44402 / -0.42 %** (9 740 / 9 260 it); N = 3, Mach 2 **0.45262 / 0.45405 / -0.32 %** (6 400 / 6 060 it): all four PASS the 1 % band, the sector always slightly low. The parts: nose and base agree to 0.1 % (nose 0.01610 / 0.01610, base 0.17527 / 0.17583 at N = 4, Mach 0.8); the difference is in the fins (0.17899 against 0.18331 at N = 4 Mach 0.8, -2.4 %; 0.11552 against 0.11965 at Mach 2, -3.5 %): a sector of N fins has one fin and a wake of periodic neighbours, the half domain has N / 2 fins, and the `Between` phase of the half domain places a fin pair against the symmetry plane. A first run of this gate on a snapshot before the viscous `cfl_max` cap (cfl 50 below Mach 1, 20 above) went non-finite on the sectors at Mach 0.8 (210 iterations) and on the N = 4 half domain at Mach 2 (27 iterations): not a discrepancy of the two domains, the numerics of the time (phys3d's cap of 10 on every viscous 3D grid fixes it). The gate runs 25 min on the 3090 (`solver3d-gs2`, `tools/gpuq`: `BOWSHOCK_WEDGE_BACKEND=gpu BOWSHOCK_FIN_TIER=coarse cargo test --release -p bowshock-core --test fin_lift_gates -- --ignored --nocapture gate_s_sector_vs_half_on_the_stack_grids`). *Re-print from the merged tree (cfd-3d `b9d3501`: gpu3d 73e91b4, phys3d 2eb0ead, anf_fins sourced section; GPU f32, `solver3d-gv3-gf1`):* G-V3 g3 on the GPU wedge, 2 900 iterations in 3.7 s (CPU 11.8 s): CD 0.213279 against 0.213002 (+0.13 %), friction 0.090042 / 0.090032 (+0.01 %), base 0.122852 / 0.122572 (+0.23 %), y+ max 0.649: all PASS (both rows). G-F1 (fin friction against the flat plate, Coarse sector): Mach 2 0.008205 / 0.008477 = 0.968 PASS; Mach 0.5 0.013424 / 0.014593 = **0.9199, FAIL by 0.0001 of the ratio band [0.92, 1.12]** (gpu3d's run before the cfl-10 cap: 0.013426, 0.9200): the lower band edge, the numerics (cfl 10 on every viscous grid, sub_iters 2) move it in the fifth digit; the band is not widened.

40. **Is the 3D solver systematically low on supersonic finned drag? Part by part against independent estimates (Finner at Mach 1.832 / 3.147, Arcas at Mach 1.5).** *Set-up:* Coarse sector, RANS, GPU f32 (`tools/part_study.sh`, job `solver3d-parts`, tip 75cc89d), the sourced Finner section, `DragParts` now with the leading (`fin_le`) and trailing (`fin_te`) edge faces apart; the independent estimates are `tests/part_estimates.rs` (Taylor-Maccoll nose through `bowshock_reference::taylor_maccoll::solve_cone`; flat-plate friction with Eckert's reference temperature and Schlichting's incompressible law, 10 d for the body, 1 d for the fins, adiabatic wall; the Fleeman / Niskanen base drag `0.25 / M` of `bowshock_reference::base_drag` for the body base and, on their own area, for the blunt trailing edges; the exact oblique-shock Cp of the 2.29 degree wedge on the frontal area of the sides `N t span / (pi/4) = 0.4074` body areas; the leading-edge face at 85 % of the Rayleigh pitot pressure, replaced below by the grid-converged Euler value). **Finner, CD0 (reference area pi d^2/4)**, 3D with fins on / independent / difference (fins off in brackets):

| part | M 1.832: 3D | independent | diff | M 3.147: 3D | independent | diff |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| nose pressure | 0.1111 (0.1101) | 0.1094 Taylor-Maccoll | +1.6 % | 0.0879 (0.0874) | 0.0859 | +2.3 % |
| body friction | 0.0744 (0.0754) | 0.0759 Eckert | -2 % | 0.0527 (0.0531) | 0.0492 | +7 % |
| body base | 0.1776 (0.1740), Cp -0.178 | 0.1365 Fleeman | +30 % | 0.1029 (0.1048), Cp -0.103 | 0.0794 | +30 % |
| fin wedge sides | 0.0144 | 0.0223 (2D wedge) | -35 % | 0.0081 | 0.0118 | -31 % |
| fin leading-edge face (Cp +0.78 / +0.62) | 0.0317 | 0.041 (Euler, 128 cells) | -23 % | 0.0253 | 0.0265 | -5 % |
| fin trailing-edge faces (Cp -0.193 / -0.088) | 0.0785 | 0.0556 Fleeman | +41 % | 0.0360 | 0.0324 | +11 % |
| fin friction | 0.0270 | 0.0338 Eckert | -20 % | 0.0182 | 0.0221 | -18 % |
| **sum** | **0.5146** | **0.4745** | **+8 %** | **0.3310** | **0.3073** | **+8 %** |
| table | 0.585 | | 3D -12.0 %, independent -19 % | 0.373 | | 3D -11.3 %, independent -18 % |

**Arcas at Mach 1.5** (C_A without base, fin increment on / off): fins off 0.3243 (data 0.340, -4.6 %); the increment 3D 0.0926 = fin sides +0.0590, fin edges +0.0016, fin friction +0.0469, aft body (boattail) -0.0130, nose +0.0010, body friction -0.0029 against the independent 0.0909 (Ackeret wave 0.0413 + flat plate 0.0466 + anchors 0.003): +2 %; fin sides plus the aft change 0.0460 against the wave 0.0413 (+11 %), fin friction 0.0469 against 0.0466 (+1 %); data increment 0.165. *Reading:* (a) **nose and body friction do not change with fins** (nose +0.9 % / +0.6 %, friction -1.3 % / -0.8 % on versus off, Finner Mach 1.832 / 3.147) and agree with Taylor-Maccoll and the flat plate (+2 %, -2..+7 %); (b) **the body base is 30 % ABOVE the Fleeman correlation** (suction `Cp` -0.178 / -0.103 against -0.137 / -0.079), and the fins change it by +2 % / -2 % only (Arcas: base Cp -0.061 off, -0.083 on, aft body -0.013 compensates); (c) **the trailing-edge faces of the fins carry about the body's base pressure**, `Cp` -0.193 / -0.088 against the body's -0.178 / -0.103 (a 2D blunt trailing edge by the same correlation: -0.137 / -0.079): the base behind the four blunt faces, 0.41 body areas, is not too high; `fin edges +0.1106` of item 38 is 0.0317 leading edge + 0.0785 trailing edge at Mach 1.832 and 0.0253 + 0.0360 at 3.147; (d) the parts that sit **below** their independent estimate are the fin wedge sides (-35 % / -31 %: the 2D wedge has no tip and root relief, the fin is 1 d x 1 d: a finite-span effect the estimate does not have, so a correct deficit), the leading-edge face (the Euler value rises with the chordwise cells 32 / 64 / 128: `Cp` 0.794 / 0.920 / 0.972 at Mach 1.832, limit about 1.0, and 0.555 / 0.623 / 0.644 at 3.147: the production 32 cells cost 0.009 / 0.004 of CD0, 1.7 % / 1.2 %) and the fin friction (-20 %: [INFERENCE] perhaps a laminar start on a 1.3e6 chord that the fully turbulent estimate does not have, or the SST wall function; the Niskanen fin model of the facade, G-F1, gives 0.92-0.97 of the plate); raised to their estimates these three add at most 0.024 / 0.009 (4.1 % / 2.4 % of the table) **while the base and the trailing-edge faces, 30-40 % above theirs, would take away 0.06**. *Verdict:* **no part of the 3D solution is low against an independent estimate by more than its explanation allows, and the independent sum itself (nose + friction + Fleeman base + wedge + edge faces) is 18-19 % below the Finner table and 45 % below the Arcas increment (0.0909 of 0.165); the solver is 8 % ABOVE that sum at both Finner points and +2 % at the Arcas increment. A solver-side common cause (a part biased low at supersonic speed) is not in the numbers**: both data sets exceed what wave, friction, base and edge-face estimates add, by the same sign at the same speeds. The one candidate that could still hide a low base is research item 9 (SST without a compressibility correction over-spreads a supersonic shear layer and RAISES the base pressure, i.e. lowers the base drag): the 3D base is already 30 % above the correlation, the finless Arcas lands 4.6 % low at Mach 1.5, and the only supersonic base data in the repository (TP-2005, `bowshock_reference::tp2005`) are jet-on rows, so the effect is not measurable here without implementing the correction (Sarkar / Wilcox `xi* = 1.5`, `Mt0 = 0.25` in the SST `k` destruction, `turbulence3.rs`, phys3d's file, and the GPU kernels, gpu3d's): proposed as the next experiment on the finless wedge and the Finner. *The blunt-edge floor (item 38):* no built-in profile moves: the new unit test shows Square, Rounded, Airfoil, DoubleWedge, Hexagonal, Biconvex and the old ANF section bit-identical under 25 % and 10 % at 201 stations, so the example rocket's fins and G-F2 (double wedge) and G-F3 (square, rounded) run on the same meshes as before (a CPU re-run of both gates from this tip was still running when this was written, log `/tmp/solver3d_fg.log`; the mesh identity above is what makes their numbers unchanged); only the Finner's 0.1 t/2 leading edge changed.

41. **G-F1 at Mach 0.5 sits at 0.92 of the flat plate whatever the grid; Draft with 32 chordwise cells; the finned example rocket's ten-Mach table (gpu3d, GPU f32, RTX 3090).** *The 0.92:* G-F1 (ANF body, four thin double-wedge fins, Re_c 1e6, Mach 0.5, `cfl_max` 10, `sub_iters` 2, MeanStationary) fin friction over the gate's flat-plate reference 0.014593: **Coarse** (196 x 128 x 27 = 677 376 cells, 22 920 it, 200 s) 0.013417 = **0.9194**; Coarse with **64** instead of 32 chordwise cells (232 x 128 x 27, 22 930 it) 0.013413 = **0.9191**; **Fine** (fin sides y+ ~ 1, 196 x 128 x 39 = 978 432 cells, 41 000 it, 508 s) 0.013540 = **0.9278**; with `sub_iters` 3 and the cap 50 of the first runs 0.013426 = 0.9200. So the Coarse wall treatment at fin y+ 15-25 is not biased low (Fine gives +0.9 %), the chordwise resolution does not matter, and the sub-iteration count and the CFL change it by 0.04 %; the 0.92 is the reference (a fully turbulent plate from the leading edge against a fin that stands in the body's boundary layer at the root and has a leading-edge region), and the line is marked `FAIL KNOWN` with the band unchanged (Mach 2: 0.968 PASS). *Draft chord cells:* the finned example rocket (Square fins, sector, Re_c 1.7e6 grid for Mach 3), cfl 20, `sub_iters` 2: Draft with 20 chordwise cells has the fin pressure 8-10 % low (Mach 1.0 / 1.7: 0.1582 / 0.1534 against 0.1709 / 0.1674 on Coarse); with 32 (`DRAFT_CHORD_CELLS`) 0.1720 / 0.1683, i.e. the whole gap, for 199 500 -> 221 844 cells (+11 %), 17 % more wall, the same 808 MiB, the same iteration counts: CD 0.59268 -> 0.60810 (Coarse 0.63083; the rest is the Mach-1 base 0.2235 against 0.2524) and 0.56625 -> 0.58187 (Coarse 0.58162, +0.04 %). *Ten-Mach table at cfl 20 / `sub_iters` 2* (Draft, 199 500 cells, 2.7-4.4 ms / it, 17.9-33.7 s per point, 0.8-1.45 GiB; Coarse, 828 940 cells, 9.7-10.9 ms / it, 94-144 s, 2.99 GiB): Draft Mach 0.1-2.3 converge (7 580 / 7 070 / 9 890 / 7 660 / 8 700 / 8 050 / 7 380 / 6 650 / 6 000 it), Mach 3.0 does at cfl 15 (6 030 it) and 10 (6 910); Coarse 0.1-1.7 converge (12 380 / 11 860 / 13 140 / 11 930 / 12 590 / 12 190 / 11 360 / 9 470 it), 2.3 / 3.0 at cfl 10 (16 620 / 15 230 it). The late blow-ups at cfl 20 (Coarse Mach 3.0 at 267, 2.3 at 5 587) start in the first wake columns behind the base plane (i = 151-156, x = 15.00-15.23), at the base corner (radius 0.47-0.50, p / p_inf 0.03 and 0.38) and in the near-wake core, not on the fins. *The controller* (`GpuRunner3::run_on`, snapshot / restore sharing the device buffers, a failed chunk redone at `cfl_max` x 0.7) from a start cap of 50 on the Draft sector: Mach 0.5 one rollback (it 213, cap 35), 6 000 it, CD 0.45336 (fixed 20: 0.45255); Mach 0.9, 2.3, 3.0 three each (cap 17.15), 8 180 / 6 310 / 6 000 it, CD 0.44837 / 0.48959 / 0.42613 (0.44811 / 0.49031 / 0.42439 at fixed 20 / 20 / 15).

42. **Finner fin friction (0.81 of the flat plate) against G-F1 (0.97); the chord count; G-W1 at Mach 2; the deletion inventory on the merged tree (solver3d, release candidate work).** *Fin friction, Coarse sector, Mach 2, the Finner's flow (`tools/finfric_study.sh`, GPU job `solver3d-finfric`; ratio of the fin friction to `2 Cf A N / (pi/4)`, the facade's flat plate):* the Finner (chord 1 d, span 1 d, t 0.08, sourced section, leading-edge face 0.008 d high) **0.806**; the same fin as a double wedge of t/c 2 % (sharp leading edge) **0.970**; the sourced section with t 0.02 (leading-edge face 0.002 d) **0.905**; G-F1's fin (chord = span = 0.5 d, double wedge 2 %) at the Finner's Reynolds number **0.946**; chord 0.5 d with the sourced section at t/c 8 % (face 0.004 d) **0.811**. The ratio follows the height of the leading-edge face over the chord (0.002 -> 0.905, 0.004 / 0.5 and 0.008 / 1 -> 0.81) and a sharp edge gives the plate (0.95-0.97, G-F1's 0.968). **So it is the estimate, not the solver:** the flat plate has no leading-edge bluntness; behind the bow shock of a blunt edge the boundary layer of the fin side starts in the entropy layer (lower edge Mach number and total pressure), which lowers the friction a blunt-nosed plate carries by 10-20 % at this Reynolds number: the same fin physics that the sourced 0.004 cal leading edge brings. It is worth 0.007 of CD0 at Mach 1.832 (1.3 %). *Chordwise cells:* the leading-edge face force rises with the cells (Euler Finner `Cp` 0.794 / 0.920 / 0.972 for 32 / 64 / 128 at Mach 1.832; 48 cells would give about 0.86, +8 % of 0.032 = **0.0026 of CD0, 0.5 %**, for +8 % cells of the sector: **not worth it, the production count stays 32**; the Arcas increment moves 5-8 % per doubling, 2-3 % per 1.5x). `DRAFT_CHORD_CELLS` is 32 (gpu3d's evidence, item 41). *G-W1 at Mach 2 (`euler-base-arcas-M2.00`, found by the release-candidate CPU gate run):* the Euler wedge of the boattailed Arcas went to a second steady state, total 0.2228 against the 2D 0.1882 (base 0.0983 against 0.0593): bisected (24db085 PASS, 62becd0 PASS, 5f89ed1 FAIL, `/tmp/bis_test.sh`) to the commit that gave `wedge_of` the 2D case's numerics: the 300 first-order iterations of the 2D transonic regime. `Numerics3::euler_wedge` (and `from_2d`) keep them below Mach 1.2 only; at Mach 2 the 100 of `Numerics3::auto` reproduce 0.188238 / 0.059331. *Release-candidate gate run (harness lines; `tools/release_gates.sh`):* the G-V1 / G-V2 (plates), G-W4 smoke cases (`case2d`) and G-P1 lines print `n/a ... not implemented by the runner` on both runners of the harness (their code lives in phys3d's plate tests / `examples/plates_gpu` and gpu3d's `finned_table`), the rest in `fixtures/release_candidate_gates.txt`. *Deletion inventory on the merged tree (scratch worktree, `/tmp/solver3d_dry_apply.py`):* with the files of item 31 deleted `bowshock-cfd` compiles after one move (done: the sst imports and `kappa_f` of the 3D GPU files, `ee22d78`) and two unused-import warnings to drop in the deletion commit (`crate::sst::Viscous` in `gpu/mod.rs`, `concat` in `gpu/common.rs`); `bowshock-core` stops at `analysis.rs` (the 2D route: app-integrate, with the flip), `lib.rs` (`pub mod field`) and `engine/case3.rs` (`from_2d`, `wedge_of`, the `Case` import); their remaining users are three test files: `tests/viscous3.rs` (phys3d: the 2D comparison harness, 2 calls), `tests/wedge_gates/gpu.rs` (gpu3d: `engine_case_of`, `Case3::wedge_of` for the RANS fixture cases, lines 164 / 57 / 66) and my `tests/wedge.rs` (`the_wedge_of_a_2d_rans_case_is_the_wedge_built_with_the_tier`, deleted with the 2D solver). Docs for the deletion commit: `crates/bowshock-cfd/README.md` (chapters from "Equations" to "Subsonic" and the 2D GPU chapters, 586 of 1 400 lines, become a frozen-reference note with their tables), `HANDOFF.md`, `ISSUES.md`, `crates/bowshock-validate/README.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `crates/bowshock/src/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `guide/src/{sweeps,results,motor}.md`.

43. **Cells across the fin thickness (`Grid3Params::fin_cells_across`, `Case3::with_fin_cells_across`; finthick, 2026-11-18): not the cause of the supersonic Finner deficit.** The fin layer, and the columns ahead of its leading edge and behind its trailing edge, can have `n` cells across (a half fin in a sector `n / 2`, at least one; nodes uniform over the local thickness; the layer relaxes to `n` neighbouring cells away from the fin; default 1 reproduces every grid bit for bit, `one_cell_across_reproduces_the_first_grids_exactly`). Coarse sector, GPU f32, default numerics (`finned_table --anf|--gf1 --fin-cells N`, RTX 3090, no rollback, no instability up to `n` 16): the Finner CD0 at Mach 1.832 / 3.147 is 0.5148 / 0.3314 (`n` 1), 0.5141 / 0.3309 (4), 0.5148 / 0.3320 (8), 0.5198 / 0.3354 (16): the trailing-edge face mean Cp stays -0.192 / -0.089, the leading-edge one falls 0.78 -> 0.73 / 0.62 -> 0.58 (the stagnation face drag -6 %), the base grows 2 % at 16. G-F1 (Mach 2) 0.3517 / 0.3518 / 0.3520, the example rocket (3 square fins) at Mach 0.8 0.4138 / 0.4115 / 0.4094 (-1 % at 8) and at Mach 2.3 0.4555 / 0.4589 / 0.4518. The one-cell fin is therefore not the missing 9-12 %.

44. **Re-sourced Finner CD0 (zero cant), leading-edge chord resolution, trailing-edge base (gateref, 2026-11-18).** *Sources, read from the public scans* (DTIC ADA636861 pdf pp. 44-49, ADA592550 p. 15): DREV-TM-9703 Table IX is the pooled multiple fit over cant 0, 2 and 4 degrees (`C_X0` 0.585 / 0.484 / 0.435 / 0.373 at Mach 1.832 / 2.375 / 2.718 / 3.147, as quoted), Table VIII the single fits per shot; the zero-cant shots (roll-moment-due-to-cant `Cl_delta` 0.00, Table IV models A03 / A05 / A06 / A07 / A08) give `C_X0` **0.567 at Mach 1.850, 0.478 at 2.364, 0.400 at 2.741, 0.385 and 0.376 at 2.969 / 2.970** (and 0.868 at 1.056, 0.701 at 1.332): the advisor's numbers verified. `anf::CD0` is now these (Mach 2.970 replaces 3.147, where no zero-cant fit exists; mean 0.3805) and the subsonic tunnel points 0.467 / 0.479 at Mach 0.5 / 0.7 read off ARL-TR-6725 Fig. 5 (filled markers, +-0.015 in `C_X0`, +-0.02 in Mach; the replot has only a free-flight multiple-fit and a tunnel series); the pooled table is `anf::CD0_POOLED`, INFO in G-F5 (`:cd0-pooled`). G-L1: the CN_alpha references are Yayla's digitisation of the tunnel and free-flight points; neither Yayla nor ARL-TR-6725 says which roll position the tunnel points are, and the 0 / 45 degree tables are in DRDC TM 2002-136 (Tables 12, 13), which does not allow redistribution: no 45 degree INFO line. *Experiments* (Coarse sector, Mach 1.832, GPU f32 local 3090, `finned_table --anf`, cfl 100 DDADI, 1 110 iterations Settled; jobs `gateref-*`): chord cells 32 (default, job 1-20261118-031320-15575 = ...-5478) CD0 0.5148, LE face Cp 0.777, fin friction 0.02704, fin pressure 0.1244; `--chord 64` (...-30091) 0.5304 (+3.0 %), LE Cp 1.141, fin friction 0.02673, 1 110 it; `--chord 128` (...-12850) 0.5333 (+3.6 %), LE Cp 1.179, fin friction 0.02717, 1 110 it: the leading-edge face Cp rises from 0.78 to 1.14 / 1.18 (the stagnation Cp behind a normal shock at Mach 1.832 is 1.63) as the chord cells go 32 / 64 / 128; the +0.019 is entirely fin pressure (LE face +0.0148, sides +0.0001); friction and base are unchanged. Against the zero-cant reference 0.567 the 128-chord point is -5.9 % (default -9.2 %), against the pooled 0.585 -8.8 %. *Fin 1 cal forward* (`--te-to-base 1.0`, job 1-20261118-031320-24592, 1 310 it): TE face Cp **-0.2452** (flush -0.1921; the planar NACA 1109 value -0.22 to -0.25), TE face cd 0.0999 vs 0.0783, base 0.1813 vs 0.1779, CD0 0.5392 (+4.7 %), LE face unchanged (0.776). So the flush trailing edge, whose face sees the body base wake, under-sucks the face: moving the fin off the base moves the face Cp to the planar value and adds +0.024 to CD0 (about +0.0216 of it the face), but the real model's fin is flush (Fig. 3.1), so this is a diagnosis of the flush-edge pressure, not a geometry change.

45. **Isothermal no-slip walls (`WallThermal`, `RansOpts3::wall_thermal`, `Case3::with_wall_thermal`, `Analysis::wall_temperature_ratio`, `finned_table --tw RATIO [--wedge]`; coldwall, 2026-11-18).** Adiabatic stays the default and bit-identical (perf5 sector Mach 1.832: the same TABLE line, 0.51480, 1110 iterations, before and after). Isothermal: the *viscous* kernels see a ghost with `T_g = 2 T_w - T` exactly (carried in the ghost pressure `p_g = 2 T_w rho / gamma - p` with the density of the cell: a first cell hotter than `2 T_w`, the log-layer cells of a fin stack at Mach 3, has no positive ghost temperature, and a floor at 0.1 `T_w` gave a nonfinite Mach 3.147 sector), face viscosity `mu(T_w)`, the SST law of the wall with the density and kinematic viscosity at the wall temperature (`rho_w = gamma p / T_w`, `nu_w = mu(T_w) / rho_w`, `turbulence3::wall_props`; no van Driest density transformation between the wall and the cell centre: exact in the sublayer, a few per cent on the strain at `y+` 15), and twice the diffusive spectral radius on a wall face in the implicit operator (the ghost doubles the conductance against the cell). The inviscid flux and the implicit wall Jacobian keep the adiabatic ghost. The wall spacing of the tiers stays that of the adiabatic wall (`y+` max 11.6 -> 13.5 on the fins). Tests: `viscous3::tests::isothermal_wall_conducts_with_the_wall_viscosity`, `plate3::thermal_tests` (laminar Mach 2 plate against the Eckert reference temperature, `cf` within 0.5 % cold and adiabatic, cold / adiabatic 1.040 against 1.037, wall heat flux within 0.7 % of the Reynolds analogy), GPU agreement `s5a_visc`, `s5a_turb`, `s5b` (isothermal rows, f32 and df32 within the adiabatic targets; the f32 face stage of the isothermal `y+` sweep 1.1e-4 against 9.3e-5, target 2e-4 there). **Measured, T_w = T_inf, Coarse tier, GPU f32, perf5 with the TE fan (be3c9b8):** finless ANF wedge, friction cold / adiabatic **1.152** at Mach 1.832 (0.08691 / 0.07542; Van Driest II 1.14) and **1.343** at 3.147 (0.07134 / 0.05313; 1.32), base 0.17973 / 0.17402 (+3.3 %) and 0.11356 / 0.10480 (+8.4 %) (`Cp_base = -CD_base`), CD 0.35959 -> 0.37654 and 0.24536 -> 0.27187. Sector CD0 0.56053 -> **0.58148** (Mach 1.832: body friction 0.07438 -> 0.08588, fin friction 0.02712 -> 0.02989, base 0.20223 -> 0.20834, fin pressure 0.14573 -> 0.14655) and 0.34962 -> **0.37683** (3.147). **Stability:** an isothermal wall lowers the stable CFL of the implicit scheme from above 100 to about 30-50 (every cold run, the wedge included at CFL 20, rolls back at iteration 190-240 when the ramp passes CFL 45-65; the adiabatic ones never do), whatever the wall treatment: with only the ghost temperature (face viscosity, law-of-wall properties and the doubled lambda switched off) the sector blows up at the same iterations, in the first cells of the body wall behind the cone-cylinder shoulder and ahead of the fin root, not at the fins. The controller finds a cap near 17-34 and the runs settle (cold sector 3910 / 2410 iterations, 87 / 26 s, against 1110 / 710), with `cfl_max` 30 set from the start the same CD0 (0.58259 / 0.37606) with one rollback; without the doubled lambda, at `cfl_max` 100, the Mach 3.147 sector goes nonfinite at 3950 iterations and the Mach 1.832 sector never settles (80 000-iteration cap, CD0 0.57202 and drifting, job `m-cold-nolam2-1.832`): the doubled lambda stays. The cause is the implicit operator: the heat flux through the wall ghost is not in its Jacobian (only a scalar `2 lambda I`), which the adiabatic wall does not need. A wall-temperature Jacobian of the viscous energy flux is the next step if the cold wall becomes the default. With the TE fan the adiabatic Finner already matches the zero-cant free flight within -3.7 / +1.3 % (HANDOFF3D section 0), so the cold wall would put it 3-6 % high: it stays opt-in until the remaining opposite errors (the SST base, the leading edge) are measured together.

    **CORRECTION to item 41 (gpu3d, found by app-integrate):** every *example-rocket* number in it (the Draft chord 20 / 32 comparison, the two ten-Mach tables, the late blow-up cells, the controller rows, memory) was measured on a **four-fin** sector; the example rocket has **three** fins (root 0.12, tip 0.05, span 0.0762, sweep 0.06, t 0.00318 m, square: the 2D fixture and the app's plan). The fin friction and fin pressure of those rows are 4/3 too high (0.0445 vs 0.0326 and 0.1715 vs 0.1259 at Mach 1.0); body friction, base, forebody pressure and the iteration / stability behaviour carry over only approximately (the wake differs). The G-F1 numbers (ANF, four fins by definition) and the 0.92 analysis are not affected. The `DRAFT_CHORD_CELLS` 32 decision rests on the four-fin comparison and has not been re-measured on three fins (jobs `c3-chord`, `prod3-draft`, `prod3-coarse` rerun them; see `crates/bowshock-cfd/handoff/gpu.md`).

46. **Subsonic Finner: CD0 and CN_alpha against free flight, the fin section, the trailing-edge face; the production tiers (subsonic, perf5, 2026-11-18).** *Gate:* G-F5 now gates the zero-cant free-flight point Mach 0.766 (`anf::CD0` (0.766, 0.508), ARL-TR-6725 replot of DRDC TM 2002-136 Table 7, 2 degree cant, 1 % standard error; no other TM 2002-136 value is committed); the sting-mounted tunnel points 0.467 / 0.479 at Mach 0.5 / 0.7 are INFO (`anf::CD0_TUNNEL`). *CN_alpha at Mach 0.79* (Lift tier, `between`, alpha 2, `incidence_rans`, CN_alpha = `cn / 0.034907`, tunnel 16.27, free flight 16.71): baseline 18.56 (+14.1 / +11.1 %, x_cp 8.364 against 8.243, CN nose / afterbody / fins 0.0671 / 0.1454 / 0.4353, 910 it); the same planform as a sharp 2 % double wedge (`--fin-section thin`) 17.33 (+6.5 / +3.7 %, fins 0.3991, CA 0.296 against 0.551); 64 chord cells 18.90 (+16.2 %, 3x the iterations); the fin stack at `y+` 15 18.58; the Coarse tier 18.18 (+11.7 / +8.8 %, settled with 0.62 orders only). The excess is in the fin lift and follows the blunt section, not the grid. *Production tiers* (the product runs zero incidence on the Coarse sector and the stability angle on the Lift half domain): Coarse sector CD0 (`finned_table --anf --tier coarse`, jobs `0-20261118-044704-30104`, `...-044705-15596`, `0-20261118-045456-18658`) against free flight **0.5313 / 0.508 (+4.6 %) at Mach 0.766, 0.5563 / 0.567 (-1.9 %) at 1.85, 0.4568 / 0.478 (-4.4 %) at 2.364, 0.4005 / 0.400 (+0.1 %) at 2.741, 0.3704 / 0.3805 (-2.7 %) at 2.970**; tunnel (INFO) 0.5096 / 0.467 (+9.1 %) at 0.5 and 0.5171 / 0.479 (+8.0 %) at 0.7. The Lift-tier half domain of the gate reads 2-3 % higher subsonic (0.547 at 0.766). *Trailing-edge face* (the four flush 0.08 cal faces are 0.41 body areas): mean Cp **-0.459 / -0.473 / -0.489 at Mach 0.5 / 0.7 / 0.766** (cd 0.187-0.199, a third of CD0) against -0.241 / -0.171 / -0.135 / -0.118 at 1.85 / 2.364 / 2.741 / 2.970, while the body base next to it is at about -0.15 to -0.17 subsonic. *Assessment (literature review, 2026-11-18):* the TE value is plausible: a 2-D blunt edge with its vortex street reads -0.6 at low speed and -0.7 to -0.75 at Mach 0.8, the same flow with the shedding suppressed -0.20 to -0.23 (Nash, ARC R&M 3427), an aspect-ratio 0.3 fin in flight -0.28 to -0.35, a body base -0.16 to -0.23 (Hoerner, Fluid-Dynamic Drag); an aspect-ratio 1 fin at -0.49 sits between, and 0.1 of TE Cp is 0.041 of CD0 (free flight needs about -0.43). Steady RANS usually *under*-predicts flatback base drag (Sandia, DU97-flatback, OSTI 1142761, up to 35 %) and does not over-predict its lift; a thickened trailing edge raises the subsonic lift-curve slope in experiment (NACA RM A50J09, RM A54A04), so the sharp-section run is a bound, not a correction. Peer RANS of the Basic Finner (METU thesis, SU2, SA and SST, 15 M cells) is also loose subsonic and tight supersonic: CN_alpha -14 to -7 % of free flight at Mach 0.766, +3 to -1 % at 1.832; the free-flight CN_alpha carries +-14-17 %, the tunnel slope is a +-5 degree fit at Re_L 4e6 without a sting correction. So the subsonic +11 % (free flight) is at the limit of steady RANS and of the data; the cheap comparability levers left are the slope definition (a 0-1 degree slope as ARL-TR-6725, +-1-3 %) and the sting for the tunnel points (-1 to -3 %).

47. **Grid convergence of the Finner sector (branch `gridconv`, 2026-11-18): the Coarse CD0 is 2-7 % under-resolved at the fin leading edge, and the free-flight agreement is cancellation.** *Harness:* `finned_table --anf --refine S [--chord N] [--drift-tol X] [--grid-only]` (new, defaults unchanged), `Case3::with_refinement(S)`, `tools/gridconv.sh MACH S...`, `tools/gridconv_diag.sh`, `tools/gridconv_chord.sh`. *What `level` and the tiers do* (by reading `case3.rs` / `meshing.rs` / `grid3.rs`; not run): `level` multiplies the axial counts (`n_up 28`, `n_nose 48`, tube 40, wake 40, gap) and the chord cells (`32 << level`) by 2^level, but `resolve_walls` then resets the radial counts (`nj_out` 80, gap 50) and the first cell (`dr_wall` 1.31e-5 d for body `y+` 1) from `y+` and a growth of 1.2, and the fin stack (`y1` 1.67e-4 d for `y+` 15, growth 1.25 / 1.35, `nk` 27) does not know `level` at all; so a level step refines `i` only and is not a similar family. Coarse and Fine differ only in the fin-side `y+` (15 / 1: `nk` 27 / 39, 688 k / 994 k cells). `with_refinement(S)` multiplies every cell count (even; `fin_cells_across` left alone, one cell per half fin) by S and divides `dx_tip`, `dr_wall`, `dr_gap` and the stack's `y1` by S and the excess over 1 of the stack's growths by S (a fixed growth refines only the wall layer: the cell size at a distance x is about (g - 1) x); body and fin `y+` fall as 1/S. *Family* (Coarse sector = S 1: ANF, sized for Mach 3.147 at Re_c 7.0e5, f32; h ratio is (N / 688 k)^(1/3)):

    | S | i x j x k | cells | h / h_Coarse | chord cells | `dr_wall` [d] | fin `y1` [d] | fin `y+` max (Mach 2.741) | device peak [MiB] |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 0.667 | 143 x 88 x 20 | 251 680 | 1.40 | 22 | 1.96e-5 | 2.50e-4 | 17.1 | 1 010 |
    | 0.75 | 155 x 98 x 21 | 318 990 | 1.29 | 24 | 1.75e-5 | 2.22e-4 | 15.5 | 1 382 |
    | **1 (Coarse)** | 196 x 130 x 27 | 687 960 | 1 | 32 | 1.31e-5 | 1.67e-4 | 12.3 | 2 728 |
    | 1.333 | 254 x 172 x 35 | 1 529 080 | 0.766 | 42 | 9.82e-6 | 1.25e-4 | 10.0 | 5 676 |
    | 1.5 | 282 x 196 x 39 | 2 155 608 | 0.683 | 48 | 8.73e-6 | 1.11e-4 | 9.2 | 9 498 |
    | 1.778 | 333 x 230 x 45 | 3 446 550 | 0.584 | 56 | 7.36e-6 | 9.37e-5 | 8.3 | 11 064 |

Precision: f32 against df32 at S 1 / 1.5, Mach 2.741 / 1.85: CD 0.39939 / 0.40895 / 0.5531 / 0.56451 (f32) against 0.39939 / 0.40899 / 0.55318 / 0.56471 (df32), at most 0.04 % apart (job `1-20261118-105948-18786`). *Iteration error is as large as the grid effect.* The default stop (`drift_tol` 5e-3) leaves CD 1.0 / 0.6 / 0.3 % high at Mach 0.766 / 1.85 / 2.741 on S 1 (0.5313 / 0.5563 / 0.4005 at 1710 / 1110 / 810 iterations; 5e-4: 0.5261 / 0.5531 / 0.3994 at 3910 / 2610 / 1310; 1e-4: 0.5259 / 0.5528 / 0.3993 at 4510 / 3410 / 1610), so the orders use **`--drift-tol 5e-4`** (every run of the family but Mach 0.766 S 1.778 ends `Settled`) and the default-tolerance family is kept as the as-shipped record. Runs: default tolerance, local 3090, Mach 0.766 / 1.85 / 2.741 jobs `1-20261118-094348-25834` / `-4906` / `-23675`; 5e-4, remote 3080 Laptop (S 0.667-1.5) `1-20261118-095136-28819` / `-32459` / `-29108` and local (S 1.778, Mach 2.741 and 1.85) `1-20261118-103303-19675`. Iterations / wall [s] (5e-4, S 0.667, 0.75, 1, 1.333, 1.5, 1.778; the 3080 Laptop is about 2x slower per step, so those walls do not compare with the local ones): Mach 0.766 2110 / 19, 2510 / 26, 3910 / 81, 5510 / 256, 6210 / 425 (remote), S 1.778 not run (see below); Mach 1.85 1410 / 12, 2010 / 21, 2610 / 54, 3710 / 168, 4610 / 308 (remote), 5310 / 421 (local); Mach 2.741 710 / 7, 810 / 9, 1310 / 28, 2710 / 124, 3410 / 230 (remote), 4010 / 219 (local). **Mach 0.766 at S 1.778 does not settle** (default tolerance, local: 37 240 iterations, `MeanStationary` after seven CFL rollbacks down to a cap of 8, 2390 s, CD 0.5296): not used.
