    | base | 0.1427 / 0.1401 / 0.1369 / 0.1350 / 0.1343 / 0.1344 | 3.9 | 0.1340 | 2.7 % |
    | fin pressure | 0.0825 / 0.0842 / 0.0909 / 0.1001 / 0.1042 / 0.1103 | none (x1.10 per step, i.e. ~ h^-0.33) | | not converged |
    | TE face Cp | -0.133 / -0.133 / -0.135 / -0.140 / -0.140 / -0.142 | 2.73 | -0.144 | 8.2 % |
    | LE face Cp | 0.472 / 0.508 / 0.655 / 0.829 / 0.929 / 1.056 | none | | not converged |

    The same CD at the shipped tolerance, S 0.667 ... 1.778: Mach 0.766 0.5423 / 0.5382 / 0.5313 / 0.5293 / 0.5278 / 0.5296 (last unsettled); 1.85 0.5481 / 0.5481 / 0.5563 / 0.5655 / 0.5685 / 0.5746; 2.741 0.3973 / 0.3965 / 0.4005 / 0.4084 / 0.4116 / 0.4174. *Reading:* the **forebody pressure** (p 2.5-3.0, GCI <= 0.7 %), the **base** (p 1.75 / 3.9, GCI 2.7-3.3 %; Mach 1.85 non-monotone at +-0.5 %), the **body friction** (p 1.1-1.6, Coarse 2-3 % low, about 0.4-0.5 % of CD) and the **TE face Cp** (p 2.7-6.6; GCI 1-8 %) are discretisation-converged to a few per cent. The **fin pressure** and the **LE face Cp** are not: at Mach 1.85 / 2.741 they rise by 5-10 % per refinement step without an asymptote (Mach 2.741 LE face Cp 0.47 ... 1.06; FINFACES gives the LE face an area of 0.0040 d^2 over the 1 d span, about 0.004 d tall, and the clustered chord cell at the leading edge is 0.3 c / nf = 0.0094 d on Coarse, 0.0054 d on S 1.778); at Mach 0.766 the fin pressure is flat within +-1.5 % over S 0.667-1.5 (non-monotone) but the LE face Cp still rises 0.63 -> 0.93. **Fin friction** keeps falling (Coarse 2-7 % above the finest grid; the Fine tier at S 1 is a further -3.1 / -4.3 / -4.7 %: 0.0394 / 0.0258 / 0.0196, job `1-20261118-102040-4418`; that job's Fine tier at Mach 1.85 ended `MeanStationary` with the base 14 % higher, CD 0.578, and its 4 cells across the fin thickness change CD by -0.2 / -0.3 / -0.3 %).

    *Chord resolution is the lever* (S 1, 5e-4): 32 / 64 / 96 / 128 chord cells give CD 0.3994 / 0.4270 / 0.4317 / 0.4294 at Mach 2.741 (fin pressure 0.0909 / 0.1172 / 0.1216 / 0.1191, LE face Cp 0.655 / 1.157 / 1.201 / 1.110; 306 x 130 x 27 cells at 128), 0.5531 / 0.5820 / 0.5874 / 0.5905 at 1.85, 0.5261 / 0.5403 / 0.5436 / 0.5449 at 0.766 (jobs `1-20261118-111205-16709` local, `1-20261118-105931-28262` remote; the remote job's Mach 1.85 / 2.741 repeats were killed as duplicates of the local ones). From 64 cells the leading edge is resolved (cell 1.2 face heights) and CD is flat within +-0.5 % up to 128; 64 cells settle at all three Mach numbers in 1410 / 2810 / 3610 iterations (the Coarse grid: 1310 / 2610 / 3910), but with 96 or 128 cells the steady solve stops on `MeanStationary` (CD of the window mean) at Mach 0.766 and 1.85 (128 cells: 14 820 iterations and six CFL rollbacks at 0.766, 10 670 at 1.85). A second family with the chord fixed at 96 on every grid (S 0.75 / 1 / 1.333 / 1.5 / 1.778, `--chord 96`, jobs local `1-20261118-112027-19554` (2.741), `-30080` (1.85), remote `1-20261118-112028-19962` (0.766: S 0.75, 1, 1.333 only; its S 1.5 was still running when this was written)) then converges monotonically: CD at Mach 2.741 0.4416 / 0.4317 / 0.4273 / 0.4252 / 0.4248 (S 0.75-1.333 `Settled`, 1.5 and 1.778 `MeanStationary`), at Mach 1.85 0.5986 / 0.5874 / 0.5848 / 0.5811 / 0.5805 (S 0.75 `Settled`, the rest `MeanStationary`), at Mach 0.766 0.5694 / 0.5436 / 0.5376 (S 0.75 `Settled`, the rest `MeanStationary`, 19 890 iterations and six rollbacks at 1.333). Extrapolation from S 1 / 1.333 / 1.778: CD 0.4217 (p 2.5) at Mach 2.741; forebody pressure p 2.9-3.5, body friction p 1.25-1.5; fin pressure (0.118 / 0.169) and TE face Cp (-0.150 / -0.270) change by 0.0006 / 0.0019 and 0.0007 / 0.0036 between the finest two grids at Mach 2.741 / 1.85, but not on an asymptote (p < 0 by the formula: the window means of an unsettled flow carry +-0.3 %).

    *Coarse CD0 error split* (percentage points of the free-flight value `anf::CD0`: 0.508 / 0.567 / 0.400; the grid-converged value is the part-wise estimate from the chord-96 family, extrapolated where p is 0.5-4, else its finest grid, +-4 / 1.4 / 1 % of CD0 as the sum of the finest-step differences):

    | | Mach 0.766 | Mach 1.85 | Mach 2.741 |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | Coarse CD0 as shipped (default stop) | 0.5313, **+4.6 %** | 0.5563, **-1.9 %** | 0.4005, **+0.1 %** |
    | stop rule (default - 5e-4) | +1.0 | +0.6 | +0.3 |
    | discretisation (Coarse 5e-4 - converged) | **-2.0** | **-5.0** | **-6.6** |
    | of which fin pressure | -2.8 | -4.4 | -6.8 |
    | forebody p / fin friction / base / body friction | +1.05 / +0.59 / -0.30 / -0.52 | +0.08 / +0.23 / -0.54 / -0.42 | +0.12 / +0.21 / +0.35 / -0.50 |
    | converged CD0 (parts) against free flight | 0.5362, **+5.6 %** | 0.5817, **+2.6 %** | 0.4258, **+6.5 %** |

    *Conclusions.* (1) The Coarse sector CD0 is **not grid-converged**: its leading-edge face is under-resolved (32 chord cells, a 0.0094 d cell against a face about 0.004 d tall) and the missing fin pressure (-2.8 / -4.4 / -6.8 % of CD0, 0.0143 / 0.0249 / 0.0271 in CD) outweighs every other discretisation error; the other four parts together move it by +0.8 / -0.7 / +0.2 % (body friction -0.4 to -0.5 and base -0.5 to +0.35 against fin friction +0.2 to +0.6 and forebody pressure +0.1 to +1.05). (2) The grid-converged model over-predicts free flight at all three Mach numbers (**+5.6 / +2.6 / +6.5 %**, uncertainty +-1-4 %), so the Coarse agreement at Mach 2.741 (+0.1 %) and the small -1.9 % at 1.85 are **cancellation of a -5 to -7 % discretisation error against a +2.6 to +6.5 % model error**; Mach 0.766 (+4.6 % shipped) is +1.0 stop rule, -2.0 discretisation, +5.6 model. Tuning the model (fin section, the leading-edge bluntness, the wall treatment) to match the Coarse CD0 would tune it to the grid; the model error to attack is the converged one. (3) The default stop rule is itself worth 0.3-1.0 %. (4) The cheap production fix is 64 chord cells on the Coarse grid (+20 % cells, the same iterations, `Settled` at all three Mach numbers): CD0 0.4270 / 0.5820 / 0.5403 is +0.3 / +0.05 / +0.8 % of the converged parts-sum above; the alternative is to keep Coarse and state that its CD0 is 2-7 % low of the converged model. Not done: a family past 3.4 M cells (memory), df32 on the finest grids, the leading-edge face height as a free parameter (item 44).

48. **What fills the transient device memory of the 3D GPU step (memprof, 2026-11-18; measurement and analysis only, no solver change).** *Tools:* `tools/memprof/run.sh` builds an example against a copy of the pinned Luma rev (`7fafed6`, cargo checkout) patched by `tools/memprof/luma-memstats.patch` (env `LUMA_MEMSTATS=<file>`: at the end of every recording the live interval, logical bytes, size class and writer kernel of every buffer, before and after `Recording::share_transients`, plus `Context::debug_mem` / `debug_reclaim` for the pager's resident bytes and the idle pool); `tools/memprof/mem_items.rs` (chains of runners, `--reclaim`, `--run-on N`, `--snaps N`); `tools/memprof/analyze.py LOG REC CELLS` makes the table below. *Case:* `finned_table --anf --tier coarse` sector, **687 960 cells** (196 x 130 x 27), f32, DDADI, `sub_iters` 2 (what `with_tier` sets; sub 3 in brackets), 103 launches (108), 541 (568) intermediate buffers per step; the G-F1 sector of `mem_stages` (708 480 cells) has the same structure but its i solves allocate scratch too (6 buffers at sub 2, 9 at sub 3): before sharing 3 287 B/cell at sub 2, kept 1 504 B/cell.

*Where Luma stands (code, `7fafed6`).* Luma **does** reuse buffers by liveness, but only at the end of the recording: `Context::retire` (`record.rs:252`) keeps every buffer released while tracing out of the pool, `share_transients` (`record.rs:347`) then packs the fresh dead ones whose live intervals do not overlap into one buffer **per (size class, usage)** (`record.rs:379`), and gives the rest to the pool. So a step costs the **sum of all its intermediates while it is traced** (compile peak) and, once recorded, about the **peak live set** (kept by the plan). Every buffer is rounded up to the next power of two (`pool::size_class`, `alloc_buffer` `context/mod.rs:745`; persistent arrays too: the 217 persistent buffers one step binds are 215 MB as classes against 143 MB of data). The pool (`pool.rs`, keyed by (class, usage)) keeps up to **40 % of the device** idle (`cap = budget / 2`, budget 80 %, `context/mod.rs:520`), has no public trim, and the idle bytes are in no Luma counter but in `nvidia-smi`; they are given back only when an allocation fails (`relieving`). Over the budget a recording aborts (`evict_one` -> `abort_recording`, `pager.rs:415`: the step then runs eagerly every iteration, DESIGN3D 54x) [code reading, not provoked].

| item (ANF sector, sub_iters 2) | buffers | compile peak (class B/cell, logical) | kept after sharing | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| block assembly `FL`, `FR`: 25 + 25 face arrays per direction (`implicit3.rs:804`, one `Fused[50]` launch per direction) | 300 | **928** (620) | 279 buffers, **812** | (a) live from assembly to the last sweep, also (c): stored so that every solve reads them | `T` lines, cmds 26-66 |
| line-solve scratch of the parts Thomas solve (25 f32 per cell, j and k lines; `linalg.rs:500` `scratch.take` per launch) | 4 (6 at sub 3) | **780** (400) | 1, 195 | (b) one buffer per solve while tracing, merged to one by sharing; (a) one live at a time | 4 x 134 MB class for 68.8 MB logical (power-of-two 1.95x) |
| SST: gradients, blends, face data, cell terms, solves, update (`turbulence3.rs`) | 97 | 378 + 61 + 128 = **567** (376) | 0 (reuse the earlier buffers) | (b) dead after the stage, retained until the end of the trace | cmds 79-101 |
| sweep vectors (x, y, dU, defect r, corr, per sub-iteration) | 60 (35) | 213 (140) | 91 | (a) | cmds 57-68 |
| inviscid: primitives with ghosts, face states, fluxes | 33 | 201 (137) | 201 | (a) until the residual, then reused | cmds 0-8 |
| viscous: wall state, gradients, face fluxes, lambdas, lhs primitives | 33 | 153 (101) | 104 | (a)/(b) | cmds 9-25 |
| DDADI diagonal `D` (25 cell arrays, `dd_diagonal3`, `implicit3.rs:977`) | 25 | 152 (100) | 134 | (a) (c): stored | cmds 32-56 |
| **sum** | **541** | **2995** (1875 logical) | **1549** | | `SUM` of REC 2: before 2 060.7 MB, after 1 066.0 MB, peak live 1 027.6 MB class / 663.8 MB logical |

*Reading the sum:* of the 2995 B/cell compile peak only **1494 B/cell (50 %) is live at once** (peak of the live set, class bytes); the other 1501 B/cell is (b), dead but held until the recording ends (three of four scratch buffers 585, the SST arrays 567 and the rest). After the recording Luma keeps 1549 B/cell, 3.7 % above that peak: the interval packing is near optimal, so **in steady state there is no significant liveness loss**; what is lost is the size-class rounding (class 1494 vs 965 logical at the peak, 1.55x) and that classes never share (cell, face, 128 MiB scratch). The process figure adds the persistent arrays and loop state, **621 B/cell** (407 MiB: Luma's resident bytes after the compile minus the plan's; the `memory_report` ledger says 638, it counts an array twice where two structs hold the same `Array`), the CUDA context (256 MiB on the 3090, 160 on the 3080L) and the idle pool: compile peak **3616 B/cell** (2 344 MiB over the context; measured 2600 MiB total, `smi`), steady **2170 B/cell**.

*`sub_iters` (job `1-20261118-190725-25323`, ANF sector, 3090, `--reclaim` between runs).* sub 1 / 2 / 3 / 4: before sharing 1 708 / 2 061 / 2 434 / 2 807 MB, kept 1 024 / 1 066 / 1 087 / 1 108 MB, process peak over the context 1 974 / 2 344 / 2 700 / 3 058 MiB, steady over the context 1 386 / 1 460 / 1 480 / 1 502 MiB. Each sub-iteration costs **+373 MB (542 B/cell) at compile** (two 134 MB scratch buffers for its j and k solves, the defect and correction vectors) and **+21 MB (30 B/cell) steady**: the scaling is a compile-time effect of (b), not residency. The G-F1 numbers agree (708 480 cells: sub 2 / 3 before sharing 2 329 / 2 837 MB, kept 1 066 / 1 087 MB; jobs `1-20261118-185511-17841` (3080L) / `-24115` (3090)).

*Second plans, kept solvers, the pool (c).* A second recorded plan in a process costs another **1 066 MB kept** (not 2.4 GiB: that number was the compile peak): `run_replay` after `run_monitored` +1 168 MiB (`mem_stages` of the first jobs); the product path keeps one (the limiter switch replaces it; the loop is compiled twice in `mem_items` because `run_monitored(1)` then `(50)` re-traces, product chunks are 50). Two ANF/G-F1 sector solvers kept alive add up exactly: resident 1 424 + 1 424 = 2 848 MiB (job `1-20261118-190240-26983`, 3080L, sub 2): a kept solver is 2.1 KB/cell. The **8.9 GiB peak of the full solve is mostly the pool**, not live data: chain wedge (19 968 cells), sector, sector, half (1 350 048 cells, `Tier::Lift`, 2 degrees), half, sector in one process (job `1-20261118-190725-20493`, 3090, nothing reclaimed): `smi` 7 208 MiB, of which resident 2 841 (the half's plan and arrays), **idle pool 4 071 MiB** (6 912 MiB idle with nothing resident after the last drop); the pool is keyed by size class, so the sector's 4 MiB classes cannot serve the half's 8 MiB classes and every new grid shape adds its own set up to the 40 % cap. Live need of that chain at its worst (the half's compile, sub 2): about 4 740 MiB = 814 MiB persistent (resident 2 841 minus the plan's 2 125 MB) + 3 924 MiB before sharing (3 048 B/cell; kept 2 125 MB = 1 574 B/cell, peak live 1 295 MB logical = 960 B/cell); 5 360 MiB measured over the context at sub 3 (job `1-20261118-190725-12494`, 3080L). After `debug_reclaim` the same process is 196-294 MiB: nothing leaks. Wedge-seed solvers are dropped on return (`Gpu3::seed`) and are not the issue; the pool is. **Snapshots (c):** one `GpuSolver3::snapshot` after each chunk pins one state set, 8 arrays x 4 MiB class = **32 MiB (49 B/cell)** each (job `1-20261118-191515-26913`: 1 476 -> 1 636 MiB over 8 kept snapshots); the controller keeps the last two plus every one below the cap, the ramp 1 -> 100 at x1.02 is 233 iterations = 5 chunks: up to 7 sets = 224 MiB = **340 B/cell** during a run (`run_on(800)` peak 2 632 MiB against 2 504 for the compile alone, job `1-20261118-191025-31696`). The Stepper holds init, probes, ping, pong (4 state sets, about 190 B/cell class).

*Cost of recomputing (job `1-20261118-191515-2622`, 3080L, G-F1 sector, no DDADI, stage times each alone):* block assembly 5.20 ms of a 19.2 ms step (27 %), a line solve 1.1-1.4 ms: re-assembling `FL`/`FR` in every solve (4-6 solves) is out; recomputing only the two flux Jacobians `A(l)`, `A(r)` (cheap, analytic in the 5 face-state variables and the normal) and caching only the dissipation matrix `|A|` saves half.

*Ranked reduction options* (B/cell of class bytes, effect on compile peak / steady; cells = budget 80 % = 19.2 GiB (3090) / 12.8 GiB (3080L) over compile peak; today **3616 B/cell: 5.7 M / 3.8 M cells at compile, 2170 steady: 9.5 M / 6.3 M**; sub 3: 4159, 5.0 M / 3.3 M):

| # | change | compile B/cell | steady B/cell | max cells 3090 / 3080L (compile) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2 | Luma: reuse a dead buffer during the recording (a buffer whose last recorded use precedes an allocation, which `share_transients` already proves safe), compile peak = peak live | -1446 (sub 3: -1958) | 0 | 9.5 M / 6.3 M |
| L3 | Luma: exact-size offset arena for the plan's transients (no power-of-two class, no per-class groups), with L2 | -584 more | -584 | 13.0 M / 8.7 M; with exact persistent sizing (est. -200) 14.9 M / 10.0 M [INFERENCE] |
| O2 | ours: cache only `dis` (25 per face and direction) in `factor3`, fuse `A(l)`, `A(r)` into the consumers (`implicit3.rs:804`; to time: kernels read 25 instead of 50 face values) | -457 | -454 | 6.5 M / 4.4 M |
| L1 | Luma: line-solve scratch from a plan-level workspace (one per plan, not per `launch_parts`) | -585 (sub 3: -975) | 0 | 6.8 M / 4.5 M |
| O6 | ours: CFL snapshots to host (or one device snapshot plus the ramp's on the host) | 0 | -340 during the run | steady 11.3 M / 7.5 M |
| L4 | Luma: `Context::trim()` / pool cap by live bytes / a public memory counter; ours: call it when a runner is dropped or the grid shape changes | 0 | 0 | removes the idle 2-7 GiB of a multi-grid process (the "8.9 GiB") |
| L5 | Luma: Stepper with 2 state sets instead of 4 | -100 | -100 | +3 % |
| O2 + L2 + L3 | | 1081 | 1081 | **19 M / 12.7 M** |

The sub-iteration plan is already one plan; separate plans per sub-iteration would add `FL`/`FR` (928) to the persistent set: no gain. Splitting the SST update into its own plan would take 567 B/cell off the compile peak but the compiled loop is one step closure.

*Residency floor for slab tiling* [INFERENCE from the table, not built]: persistent geometry, wall data and state 620 (exact sizes about 420); state sets old/new 64; what crosses slabs between sweeps (b, x, y, dU, r, corr, dt, residual, primitives with ghosts, SST fields) about 210; one slab of transients incl. halos (peak live logical 960 B/cell of the domain, of which the 210 above are already counted: 750 x (1 + 4 / thickness) / slabs, e.g. 8 slabs of 8 planes 140): **about 0.9-1.1 KB/cell, 3.3-4x today: 19-23 M cells (3090), 12.5-15 M (3080L)** at the 80 % budget, about 0.45 KB if geometry is streamed from the host. DDADI lines: a slab perpendicular to a direction cuts that direction's lines (the sector is 196 axial x 130 radial x 27 azimuthal). Three exact ways: (i) re-tile per sweep with line-aligned bundles (tile = complete i-lines for the i sweep, etc.; x, y, dU stay full-domain; the face matrices of a tile, with a halo of one cell, are re-assembled per sweep: assembly 27 % of the step per pass, 2-3 passes per sub-iteration: +50-100 % step time unless only `dis` is recomputed), (ii) stream Thomas across slabs along the cut direction (forward elimination carries 25 + 5 f32 per line, back substitution needs the modified upper blocks: 100 B/cell for the one direction at a time, or a second forward pass), (iii) block-Jacobi at slab boundaries (changes the solver, defect correction absorbs part). Luma side: the trace is shape-specific but kernels are not, and views carry runtime start slots, so equal slabs can share one plan with the buffers substituted per slab (the `Stepper` swap), which needs L2/L3 to make the per-slab compile small.

*Draft Luma issue* (not filed): "Recorded plans cost the sum of their intermediates at record time; allocations round to powers of two; the idle pool is class-keyed with no trim", with the five measurements above and `tools/memprof` as the repro (the diagnostic patch is 135 lines). Jobs: `1-20261118-185511-24115`, `-17841`, `1-20261118-190239-7339`, `-9713`, `1-20261118-190240-26983`, `1-20261118-190725-25323`, `-20493`, `-12494`, `1-20261118-191025-31696`, `1-20261118-191515-26913`, `-2622`.

49. **Luma 231c986 (pin bump), trim, a device-memory guard, CFL snapshots on the host (lumabump, 2026-11-19).** *Changes:* (1) `Cargo.toml` pins Luma `231c986` (v0.5.0 branch: in-recording buffer reuse, exact-size plan sharing, `Context::memory_stats`, `Context::trim`). (2) `LazySolver3::run_monitored` calls `Context::trim` before it traces a replacement loop (the old plan is given back first) and after every trace (the trace's spare buffers, 1.1 GiB idle per 688 k cells, no longer sit in the pool); `GpuRunner3::new` trims when the grid shape or precision differs from the last solver built in the process (a table rebuilds a solver of the same shape per node and keeps its pool). (3) `GpuRunner3::new` refuses a grid the device cannot hold: `bowshock_cfd::gpu::device_bytes3(cells, precision, sub_iters, rans)` (300 MiB + 4100 B/cell, x1.45 double-float, x0.86 Euler, +100 B per `sub_iters` above 3) against the device, 5/4 of `memory_stats().budget_bytes` (Luma's 80 % default) less 5 %, less `resident_bytes` held by solvers still alive: `Error::Gpu("grid of 4823000 cells needs ~24.3 GiB, device budget 18.9 GiB")` instead of a failed allocation; `BOWSHOCK_GPU_MEM_GUARD=off` skips it (the measurements below). Unit tests: `the_device_estimate_bounds_the_measured_peaks` (cfd), `the_memory_guard_compares_the_estimate_with_the_free_budget` (core). (4) `GpuSolver3::snapshot` downloads the 11 loop-state arrays to host memory (raw `S` elements; `restore` uploads them: bit for bit, test `a_host_snapshot_restores_the_implicit_run_exactly`, f32 and df32); a snapshot is 0 device bytes (the controller kept up to 7 x 49 B/cell of device arrays); the download time is counted in `PointTiming::download_s`. No device snapshots are kept, the "last two on the device" fallback was not needed. (5) `tools/memprof/mem_items.rs` (now `crates/bowshock-validate/examples/mem_items.rs`, `--ext`, `--euler`) and `tools/memprof/run.sh` use `memory_stats`/`trim` and a 5 ms process sampler (`nvidia-smi -lms 5`; the old 40-100 ms polls missed the 20 ms end-of-recording spike); `tools/gpuq/gpumem` prints `fast_peak_mib` from `-lms 10`. The 7fafed6 patch and `analyze.py` are gone (branch `memprof`, `2c0e356`).

*Measured* (process peak of `nvidia-smi`, MiB incl. the 256 MiB CUDA context; ANF sector 688 k cells / ANF half domain 1.35 M cells, sub_iters 2, f32, `--run-on 300` = first-order phase plus the second-order loop; steady = after `trim`):

| | 7fafed6 (3080L, `0-20261119-033636-22874`) | 231c986 + host snapshots, no trim around the trace (3080L, `-23619`) | **final** (3080L, `0-20261119-034237-27486`) | **final** (3090, `0-20261119-033636-4418`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sector peak (compile + run) | 2 632 | 3 658 | **2 700** | **2 796** |
| half-domain peak | 5 062 | 6 218 | **4 748** | **4 844** |
| sector / half steady | 1 652 / 3 102 | 1 598 / 2 556 | 1 598 / 2 552 | 1 694 / 2 648 |
| Luma resident, sector | 1 424 | 1 071 | 1 071 | 1 071 |
| full solve (`engine3d_finned_timing`, 28 points, ~39 200 it) device peak | 7 996 (3080L, 517.8 s, `0-20261119-003836-14692`); 8.9 GiB (3090, item 48) | 6 272 (520.3 s, `0-20261119-001807-9117`) | **4 808 (499.4 s)** | **4 084 (254.5 s, `0-20261119-020442-27719`)** |

*Reading it.* (a) **The bump alone does not lower the compile peak**: the trace still holds the sum of the step's intermediates, 2.0 GB before sharing, and the exact-size arena it creates at `end_recording` is allocated while those are alive (first compile +0.1 GiB over 7fafed6); a replaced plan's exact-size buffers are returned to the driver only when the device is idle, so the second compile (first-order to second-order loop) sat on top of the first plan: 3 658 against 2 632 (+39 %), 6 218 against 5 062. Trimming before and after the trace removes that (2 700, 4 748: the old peak or 6 % below it). (b) The plan Luma keeps is smaller (resident 1 424 to 1 071 MiB, -25 %) but the process steady figure moves only -4 % (1 652 to 1 598): about 300 MiB of it is not counted by `resident_bytes`. (c) The "8.9 GiB" of the full solve was the pool of the chain of grid shapes: **4.1 GiB on the 3090 (-54 %), 4.8 GiB on the 3080L (-40 %)**. (d) Peaks per cell, f32 SST sub 2 (`finned_table --anf --refine S --max-iter 260`, `0-20261119-033636-4418`, 3090): S 1 / 1.333 / 1.5 / 1.778 / 2 / 2.25 = 0.69 / 1.53 / 2.16 / 3.45 / 4.82 / 6.80 M cells, peaks 2 760 / 4 870 / 8 668 / 9 962 / 18 554 / 22 644 MiB = 3 871 / 3 164 / 4 091 / 2 953 / 3 979 / 3 451 B per cell: size-class rounding makes it jump, 4.1 KB is the bound. **The 6.8 M-cell grid that failed at the trace on the 3090 (item 47, device full at 19.5 GiB) now runs: 260 iterations, 109 ms/it, 22.6 GiB of 24; S 2.0 18.0 GiB, 76.7 ms/it** (guard off; with the guard on both are refused: 24.3 and 34.2 GiB estimated against 22.5 GiB usable, so the largest grid the guard admits on the 3090 is about 5.1 M cells and on the 3080L (usable 15.2 GiB) about 3.6 M; the measured limit is ~6.8 M and ~4.2 M [INFERENCE for the 3080L]: the guard is deliberately a bound, `BOWSHOCK_GPU_MEM_GUARD=off` for the edge). (e) Settings (`0-20261119-034602-18469`, 3090, sector, peaks less context): `sub_iters` 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 = 2 356 / 2 540 / 2 558 / 2 592 MiB (93 / 100 / 101 / 102 %, was +542 B/cell per sub-iteration at compile in item 48: gone), double-float 3 636 (143 %), Euler 2 166 (85 %); half domain sub 1 / 2 / 3: 4 338 / 4 588 / 4 758 MiB, Euler 3 910.

*Time.* Full solve on the 3090 **254.5 s** (39 200 it, 6.49 ms/it; 257.3 s before, `22ffd8e`) and on the 3080L **499.4 s** (12.74 ms/it; 517.8 s with 7fafed6 on the same code, 520.3 s with the pin and host snapshots but no trim around the trace, `-9117`): the iterating time did not rise (473.5 s against 493.3 s), so the host snapshots and the trims cost nothing measurable (the trims run once per compile; a snapshot is 49 B/cell over PCIe every 50 iterations). Gates (tools/release_gates.sh): wedge 60 PASS / 52 INFO and gpucases (all test binaries ok, including the new snapshot test) on the 3080L (`0-20261119-020451-26016`), identical to the tip's tally (`1-20261118-043225-18931`; the sixth digit of some `C_D` moves, 0.213519 to 0.213522 for G-V3); fins and lift on the 3090 (`0-20261119-035119-21404`): G-F1 1, G-S 4 and 14 G-F4 PASS, 4 G-F4 KNOWN (Arcas, item 37); G-F5 10/10 PASS, G-L1 28 PASS and the same 3 subsonic FAIL (`wt-M0.690-inplane`, `wt-M0.790-between/inplane` `cn_alpha`), the tip's tally (`1-20261118-043225-*`). No regression.

*Luma issue (draft, not filed):* "Recording: the plan's exact-size arena is allocated before the unshared intermediates are released, and the buffers of a dropped plan are returned to the driver late. (1) `share_transients` creates each shared buffer with `ctx.new_buffer` while every pre-sharing transient is still alive, so the peak of a recording is `before sharing + after sharing` (2.0 + 1.0 GB for a 688 k-cell step), not `before` as in 7fafed6 where the shared buffers were existing ones; (2) buffers of no size class are not pooled but released at the next device-idle point, so replacing a compiled loop by a new one (`compile_until_all` twice) holds both plans plus the new trace (3 658 MiB against 2 632 MiB) unless the caller trims in between (`Context::trim()` before and after the trace makes it 2 700). (3) `MemoryStats::resident_bytes` does not count the ~300 MiB of a plan (1 071 MiB resident against 1 368 MiB process over the context after `trim`). (4) The in-recording reuse (`a72ce9e`) did not lower the trace of this step: the step's intermediates are all alive until the residual and the SST update, so the sum is the peak. Repro: `tools/memprof/run.sh --chain anf@2 --reclaim --run-on 300` and `tools/gpuq/gpumem`'s `fast_peak_mib`."

50. **Prescribed transition for the SST model, and what a laminar run does to the Basic Finner CD0 (branch `transition`, 2026-11-18): a plausible transition closes the +3-7 % gap; the location is the parameter, not a model.** *Feature (opt-in, default unchanged):* `Case3::with_transition(x_tr_body_calibers, fin_chord_fraction)` (field `Case3::transition`, `RansOpts3::transition`), `finned_table --x-tr X --fin-tr F`, `tools/transition_sweep.sh MACH X:F...`; module `transition3`. A fixed intermittency `gamma` per cell, 0 laminar / 1 turbulent (`TurbIn::gamma`, `Rans3::gamma`, GPU `TDev3::gamma`; empty = all turbulent, so the default path is untouched). Where `gamma = 0`: **`mu_t = 0` after every update** (also in the initial state and in `set_state_turb`), which is the suppression of the production of both equations (`P = mu_t S^2`, the `omega` source `gamma_SST rho P / mu_t`), so `k`, `omega` are only convected, diffused and destroyed (what a "laminar zone" does in commercial codes and what `gamma P_k` with `gamma = 0` does in Menter's `gamma-Re_theta`; its `max(gamma, 0.1)` destruction factor is not needed, the free-stream decay of `k` is slow); and a wall cell takes the **plain resolved-wall state** (`k_w = 0`, Menter's `omega_w`, `mu_t,w = 0`, speed 0 in `wall_state`, `ks` ignored) and the strain of its velocity gradient instead of the law of the wall (`wall_state_pass`, `TurbIn::law_speed`, GPU `wall_state_pass3` / `wall_law3`): the law of the wall at the fin stack's `y+` 10-17 would put the turbulent shear on a laminar layer. The field is a property of the **nearest wall face** of the cell (`walldist3::WallBvh::nearest`, exact triangle distance as the wall distance): body segment or sting face upstream of `x_tr` (nose diameters from the tip) laminar; fin face upstream of `x_le(j) + fin_frac (x_te(j) - x_le(j))` of its row `j` laminar (`fin_frac` 0 = turbulent from the leading edge, 1 = laminar all over; a swept fin has a swept line); every other part, and cells of no wall, turbulent. *Why this and not a `mu_t` trip or `gamma-Re_theta`:* a Baldwin-Lomax `mu_t` trip with the production left on hides a developed `k` that appears at the trip; with the production off the turbulence grows from the free-stream `k` over a physical transition length (plate, below); a two-equation `gamma-Re_theta` model needs the free-stream turbulence intensity (unknown to a factor 10 in a range) and Re_theta correlations that are not calibrated for supersonic slender bodies, and its output would be a location anyway.

    *Tests.* CPU: `transition3::tests::gamma_follows_the_nearest_wall_station` (box with a body floor and a fin: body trip station, fin chord fraction by row, fully laminar fin, `(0, 0)` = empty field); `engine::case3::tests::transition_is_opt_in_and_reaches_the_solver` (the Coarse ANF sector: default and `with_transition(0, 0)` hand the solver no field, `with_transition(2, 0)` makes the near-wall body cells upstream laminar and downstream turbulent); `plate3::tests::transition_plate_is_laminar_then_turbulent` (`#[ignore]`, 44 s; G2 plate `Re_x = 5e6 x`, `x_tr = 0.2`, `Re_x,tr = 1e6`, `y+` 0.2-0.4): **laminar upstream is Blasius** (`cf` within -1.4 / -0.9 / +2.8 % at `x` 0.06 / 0.12 / 0.18, exactly zero `mu_t` in the layer: 1.19e-3 / 8.43e-4 / 7.16e-4 against 1.21e-3 / 8.51e-4 / 6.97e-4), **turbulent downstream** (`cf` 3.60e-3 at `x = 0.22`, overshoot to 3.64e-3 (+14 % over the fully turbulent run) at 0.25, then 3.03e-3 at 0.5 and 2.683e-3 at `x = 0.96` against 2.618e-3 of the fully turbulent run on the same grid, +2.5 %, the tripped layer being thinner; correlation 2.733e-3), `max mu_t/mu` 178 at 0.97; `cf` is half way between the laminar and the turbulent curve 0.023 behind the trip (0.11 `x_tr`, the transition length of the flight cone below is 0.16 of onset). GPU against CPU: `s5a_turb::turbulence_kernels_match_cpu` has four new rows with the left half of the cells laminar (box with rough cells and `law_strain`, the same on an isothermal wall, the `y+` 0.1-300 sweep smooth and rough; every stage of the update): f32 wall state 5e-7 to 2.1e-6, `k, omega` 1.9e-6 to 2.5e-6, `mu_t` 1.4e-6 to 2.2e-6 (the old rows' band; df32 6e-14 to 1.5e-13 / 1.4e-6 to 2.6e-6 / 1.3e-6 to 4.5e-6: the line solves are f32 in both precisions); `s5b::transition_box_matches_cpu` (30 iterations of the full solver from the free stream, trip at `x = 0.4` through the block's top face, M 0.3, `Re` 2e3 / 2e5, explicit and implicit block): state f32 3e-7 to 8e-7 (target 1e-5 explicit / 1e-3 implicit), `k, omega, mu_t` 4e-7 to 5e-6, df32 8e-12 to 3e-9 / 4e-8 to 2.4e-7 (all inside the existing targets; job `1-20261118-231323-29364`, local 3090). `bowshock-cfd` 121 tests, `bowshock-core` 207 tests and clippy / fmt pass; `bash tools/release_gates.sh gpucases` job `2-20261118-235323-19868`.

    *Expected transition location (sources).* The solver's free-flight Reynolds number is the `--anf` one, `Re_c = rho c d / mu = 1.225 x 340.294 x 0.03 / 1.7894e-5 = 6.99e5` per calibre at every Mach number (sea level, 30 mm model), so the free-stream `Re_x = M x 6.99e5 x (x / d)`: **5.35e5 / 1.29e6 / 1.92e6 per calibre at Mach 0.766 / 1.85 / 2.741**, `Re_L` of the 10-calibre body 5.4e6 / 1.3e7 / 1.9e7, the fin root chord (1 cal) 5.4e5 / 1.3e6 / 1.9e6. (1) **Flight data on the same nose angle:** Fisher and Dougherty, *In-flight transition measurement on a 10 degree cone at Mach numbers from 0.5 to 2.0*, NASA TP-1971 (1982; the AEDC transition cone flown on an F-15 at 1.5-15 km, low-disturbance environment, adiabatic wall; <https://ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/19820018351>): transition Reynolds number on the cone length from the apex **3.5e6 at Mach 0.5 rising to above 9e6 at Mach > 1.6** (82 points, a nearly linear band in Mach number; Tollmien-Schlichting waves identified in the cone microphone spectra; onset-to-end ratio 0.86, so a transition length of 16 % of the onset station). Reading the Mach 0.766 / 1.85 / 2.741 values off it (the last is an extrapolation, [INFERENCE]): `Re_x,tr` about 4.8e6 (3.5-6e6) / above 9e6 / 9e6-1.5e7, i.e. **`x_tr` about 9 (6.5-11) / 7 or more / 4.7-8 calibres**, almost the whole 10-calibre body at Mach 0.766. The Taylor-Maccoll 10-degree cone has edge-to-free-stream unit Reynolds number 1.02 / 1.06 / 1.16 at Mach 1.44 / 1.85 / 2.741 (own calculation, Sutherland viscosity, adiabatic), so the free-stream `Re_x` used here is within 16 % of the edge-based one. (2) **The ANF nose is a cone for 2.84 calibres** and then a cylinder (zero pressure gradient, flat-plate-like): the cone's favourable gradient is not there, a lower `Re_x,tr` (the flat-plate range of 1.5-3e6 at low disturbance, `x_tr` 2.8-5.6 cal at Mach 0.766, 1.2-2.3 at 1.85, 0.8-1.6 at 2.741) is the other bound. Aeroballistic ranges and flight are essentially disturbance-free (Harvey, NASA TM 78635 (1978), on the wind-tunnel noise that makes tunnel `Re_x,tr` lower; Potter, AIAA J. 13(3), 1975, aeroballistic-range cones). (3) **Roughness does not trip a range model:** the critical roughness Reynolds number of distributed roughness is `Re_k = u_k k / nu_k = 250-600` for Mach numbers up to 2 (Braslow, Knox, Horton, as reviewed in Holloway and Morrisette, NASA TN D-3517 (1966)); with the Blasius wall shear `Re_k = 0.332 (k/x)^2 Re_x^1.5`, the height that trips at `x = 1` cal (`Re_x` 5.4e5, Mach 0.766) is `k = 42.5 x / Re_x^0.75 = 64` um (`Re_k` 600; 42 um at 250), 0.002 cal, against 1-5 um of a machined model; a fixed `k` of 5 um has `Re_k` above 600 only within the first 20 `Re_x` of the tip. So natural transition it is, and the locations above stand for a clean model; a painted or rough rocket (`k_s` 30-100 um at `Re'` 2e7 / m, Mach 0.9 at sea level: `Re_Uk = k Re'` 600-2000) is tripped at the tip, which is what the fully turbulent default (and the rough-wall law at `ks > 0`) assumes.

    *Reading.* (1) **The effect is of the size of the gap and of the right sign.** Body transition alone, fins turbulent: CD0 falls by 0.0009 / 0.0032 / 0.0082 / 0.0208 / 0.0350 / 0.0508 at Mach 0.766 for `x_tr` = 1 / 2 / 3 / 5 / 7 / 9 cal, 0.0006 / 0.0030 / 0.0082 / 0.0135 / 0.0172 / 0.0161 at Mach 1.85 and 0.0005 / 0.0031 / 0.0082 / 0.0130 / 0.0194 / 0.0253 at Mach 2.741 (the first three steps are the same at the three Mach numbers, -0.0005...-0.0009 / -0.003 / -0.008: 0.1 / 0.6 / 1.5 % of CD0). The free-flight value is matched at **`x_tr` 6.6 cal (`Re_x,tr` 3.5e6) at Mach 0.766, 5.8 cal (7.5e6) at 1.85 and not below 9 cal (1.7e7: +0.4 %) at 2.741**: at Mach 0.766 exactly Fisher's Mach 0.5 value 3.5e6 (the Mach 0.77 reading 4.8e6 gives -3.6 %), at 1.85 inside the `> 9e6` band (7 cal: -0.4 %; 9 cal: -0.2 %), at 2.741 within the extrapolation (5 / 7 / 9 cal: +3.5 / +1.9 / +0.4 %). With the flight-cone `Re_x,tr` the error against free flight goes from **+6.4 / +2.6 / +6.7 % (mean +5.2) to +0.1...-3.6 / -0.4...-0.2 / +3.5...+0.4 %**, i.e. to about 0 +- 4 %, inside the +-1-4 % grid-convergence uncertainty of item 47. **A physically plausible transition location therefore closes the gap, but it is not the only thing that does** (the fins, below), and the three free-flight CD0 do not fix the location: 3 parameters-worth of freedom (`x_tr` per Mach number, the fins) against 3 numbers. (2) **Why the laminar run is worth less than its friction:** the body friction is 0.1018 / 0.0739 / 0.0583 turbulent (18.8 / 12.7 / 13.7 % of CD0) and falls to 0.0460 / 0.0354 / 0.0279 at `x_tr` = 7 (-0.056 / -0.039 / -0.030), but the thinner layer raises the **base** by +0.011 / +0.020 / +0.010 and the **forebody pressure** by +0.007 / -0.001 / -0.001 (the Mach 0.766 cone sees the displacement thickness of its laminar layer: 0.0137 -> 0.0204; supersonic it does not) and the fin pressure by +0.003 / +0.003 / +0.002, so 63 / 45 / 64 % of the friction reduction reaches CD0 at 7 cal (the base at Mach 1.85 grows from 0.1996 to 0.2376 as `x_tr` goes 0 -> 9 cal and CD0 turns round at 7 cal: -0.0172 at 7, -0.0161 at 9). Friction as the only change would over-state the effect by a factor 1.6-2.2. (3) **The fins:** their friction is 0.0388 / 0.0266 / 0.0198 (7.2 / 4.6 / 4.6 % of CD0); `fin_frac` 0.3 removes 0.0095 / 0.0048 / 0.0033 of it (-1.3 / -0.6 / -0.6 % of CD0 net, 0.0068 / 0.0037 / 0.0026) and independent of the body (the same at every `x_tr`); **fully laminar fins** (`Re_c` 5.4e5 / 1.3e6 / 1.9e6: laminar by the flight-cone criterion and borderline at Mach 2.741 by the flat-plate one, but the leading edge is blunt (0.004 cal radius) and the junction has a horseshoe vortex, neither modelled as a trip here) take **-0.0868 / -0.0305 / -0.0164 (-16 / -5.2 / -3.8 % of CD0)** at Mach 0.766 / 1.85 / 2.741: much more than their friction (0.027 / 0.017 / 0.012) because the fin pressure (-0.045 / -0.013 / -0.0045: the trailing-edge face's base suction) and the base (-0.014 / -0.001 / 0) follow the state of the boundary layer that leaves the blunt trailing edge. Those runs are the unsteady ones (`MeanStationary` after 6000-6700 iterations, CFL rolled back to 49 / 24, oscillation of CA over the last window 1.2-4.3 % at Mach 0.766, 0.14-0.23 % at 1.85; `CdStationary` at 5100 with `x_tr` 9 at 1.85): their values carry +-1 % at least, and they are not credible as a prediction (a 4-fin sector on a steady solver). With laminar fins the model **under**-predicts free flight at Mach 0.766 and 1.85 (-10.7 / -2.7 % with a turbulent body, -11.9 ... -17.9 / -4.4 ... -5.7 % with `x_tr` 3-9 cal) and reaches it only at Mach 2.741 (+2.7 % with a turbulent body, +0.6 / -0.6 / -2.2 / -3.6 % at `x_tr` 3 / 5 / 7 / 9): free flight does not support laminar fins together with the flight-cone body transition. (4) **Tuning warning:** the free-flight CD0 is a 1 % curve fit of 2 degree cant data (item 46), there is one body and three Mach numbers, and the grid-converged gap itself carries +-1-4 %: the sweep shows the size and the direction of the transition effect, not a calibration of `Re_x,tr`.

    *Recommendation: neither a default prescribed transition nor a gamma-Re_theta model now; keep the fully turbulent default, ship the opt-in, and let the application choose a Reynolds-number criterion only for smooth walls.* (a) The fully turbulent default is right for what the application is built for: painted, rail-buttoned rockets at `Re'` 2.3e7 M / m (sea level), where the roughness criterion above trips the layer at the tip (and `ks > 0` already takes the rough law of the wall), and it is the conservative (higher-drag) side for a clean model: +5 % of CD0 for a clean small model at `Re_L` 5e6-2e7, the size of the quoted model error. (b) For a clean smooth wall (`ks` = 0 or `k Re' < 250`, the case the free-flight comparison is) the application can turn the opt-in on with `x_tr = Re_x,tr / (M Re_c)` calibres, `Re_x,tr` about 3.5e6 below Mach 0.8 rising linearly to about 9e6 at Mach 1.6 and held (the flight cone of Fisher and Dougherty; [INFERENCE] above Mach 2), fins turbulent; that closes the Finner gap to 0 +- 4 %, but it rests on one cone data set and three CD0 numbers, so do it only after a second free-flight data set (other Finner Mach numbers, the 10-calibre body at other Reynolds numbers) confirms the criterion; it is a 1-parameter, 0-extra-cost model and `finned_table --x-tr` reproduces it today. (c) A **gamma-Re_theta** (or k-omega-gamma) model is **not** worth it for this purpose: it adds 1-2 transport equations (three more line solves, more memory) to a memory-bound step, needs the free-stream turbulence intensity of a range or of the atmosphere (not known to a factor 10: `Re_theta,t` of the Langtry-Menter correlation is 1136 at `Tu` 0.1 % and 584 at 1 %), has no validation for supersonic slender bodies in this solver, and what the application needs from it is one number, the location, which the cone flight data give at a given Mach number with +-40 % anyway; the effect of the location (+-0.015 in CD0 at Mach 0.766 for +-2 cal around `x_tr` 7) is larger than what the model would add. (d) What is **not** supported by the data: laminar fins on top of the flight-cone body transition (below free flight by 3-18 %, unsteady). Not done: a CPU run of the transition on a sector (the GPU-CPU agreement is the kernel and box tests above), the 4-fin sector with a time-accurate laminar-fin run, free flight of the other Finner data, the rough-wall trip (`Re_k`) as a field, a smooth `gamma` ramp (the sharp trip's overshoot of 14 % at 0.25 `x_tr` on the plate is not smoothed).

51. **G-L1 / G-F5 like-for-like with the Basic Finner tunnel: CN_alpha slope definition, Reynolds number, sting (compar, 2026-11-19).** *Harness:* `incidence_rans --alphas a,b,c` (one cold run, then warm-started angles, one INCIDENCE line each), `--re-l X` (`re_c = X / (10 M)`: `Re_L` of the 10-calibre body), `--sting R` (`BaseBody::with_sting(R/2)`, sting over base diameter; the half domain on the base-wake grid supports it, fins and all). `tools/compar_job.sh VARIANT MACH` runs both roll phases at alpha 0, 1, 2, 5 (limit 6000 it). Variants: A = sea-level free-flight Re (`Re_L` 5.5e6 at M 0.79), B = `Re_L` 4e6, C = `Re_L` 4e6 + sting 0.63 (19 mm on the 30 mm base). The 4e6 and 0.63 are from the assignment; neither the `anf` module nor items 44 / 46 state a tunnel Re. *CN_alpha per rad (between / inplane), Lift tier:*

(Subsonic CN(1)/CN(5) per angle differ by < 1 % between 1 and 2 degrees; CN(5)/5 is 1-5 % higher (in-plane +4 %, between +1 %); CN at 0 degrees is 1e-4 or less. Free-flight reference 16.73 at M 0.80.) *Findings.* (1) Slope definition moves CN_alpha by at most +-1.5 % (0-1 deg vs secant) and +0.5..+2 % (LSQ over 0..5; +6 % at M 2.48 where CN(5)/5 is +3.5 %): the +13 / +14 % subsonic gap to the tunnel is **not** a definition artefact (ARL-TR-6725's 0-1 deg slope = our 1-degree secant). (2) Re_L 5.5e6 -> 4e6: CN_alpha -0.2..-0.8 %, CD0 +0.003..+0.026 (friction). (3) Sting 0.63: CN_alpha -0.8 % (subsonic), 0.0 supersonic (x_cp 8.36 -> 8.35 / 8.39); CD0 **-12 %** subsonic (0.567 -> 0.497 at M 0.79; base drag the annulus only) and -12 % at M 2.48 (0.468 -> 0.412): the sting-mounted CD0 now meets the tunnel points (G-F5 `:cd0-tunnel` INFO, job 0-20261119-032350-31325 remote/any: M 0.5 0.4597 / 0.4561 vs 0.467 (-1.6 / -2.3 %), M 0.7 0.4750 / 0.4746 vs 0.479 (-0.8 / -0.9 %); before, no sting and `Re_c` sea level: 0.536 at M 0.69, +12 %). *Gate change* (`fin_lift_gates.rs`): the tunnel points (`anf::WIND_TUNNEL` in G-L1, `anf::CD0_TUNNEL` in G-F5) are run on the tunnel setup (`anf_half(.., tunnel = true)`: `Re_L` 4e6, sting 0.63 base diameters); the free-flight points are unchanged; CN_alpha stays the 2-degree secant (the definition is within the data's +-3 % of the fit and a 3-angle gate would triple the cost); bands unchanged (13 %, 0.25 cal). *G-L1 rerun (jobs 0-20261119-032350-10635 subsonic, -11829 supersonic, GPU, Lift tier):* CN_alpha tunnel M 0.69 17.45 (inplane; +11.2 %) PASS, M 0.79 18.35 / 18.45 (+12.8 % PASS / +13.4 % **FAIL**, band 13 %), M 1.728 / 1.99 / 2.48 pass (12.85 / 11.30 / 9.65 against 11.92 / 10.60 / 9.34), x_cp all PASS (8.30 / 8.35 against 8.21 / 8.24 cal). Before this change both M 0.79 phases failed (+14 %). Free-flight points not rerun (inputs unchanged). *Remaining* subsonic overshoot (+11..13 % tunnel, +11 % free flight) is therefore the solver's (fin / body lift at Mach 0.7-0.8, item 46) and not the comparison's.

52. **G-S at 64 chord cells, Mach 0.8: the -4 % was a grid difference in the leading edge and a physical one in the trailing-edge wake (gs64, 2026-11-19).** *Harness:* `examples/gs_diag.rs` (`run <sector|half> N M [--chord C] [--across A] [--iters N] [--drift-tol X]`: the stop rule under control, the parts, the CA history, the drag of the leading edge / trailing edge / sides per fin and the trailing-edge Cp on the two sides of each fin; `grid N [chord] [across]`: the two grids compared node by node on the CPU), `tools/gs_diag_job.sh`. With `COARSE_CHORD_CELLS` 64 the gate read N = 4 sector 0.558920 / half 0.582002 (-4.0 %), N = 3 0.478382 / 0.492739 (-2.9 %) at Mach 0.8 (Mach 2 passed). Three causes were tested; the numbers are GPU f32, Coarse tier, level 0, jobs in brackets.
    (a) *Stop rule: not the cause, but a 0.3-1 % noise.* Both domains run 16 000 iterations with every rule off (`--iters`; 20261119-053334-32442 local N = 4, 20261119-053334-27089 remote N = 3). Sector N = 4: 0.558920 at the default stop (1810 it, `Settled`), **0.555686** after 16 000 (CA flat to 0.04 % over the last 2 000 it: the sector is steady and the default rule leaves 0.6 % on the table; N = 3: 0.478382 at 1610 it, 0.476757 converged, 0.3 %). Half domain N = 4: 0.582002 at the default stop (1910 it), and after 16 000 it **a limit cycle**, CA 0.5724-0.5773 over the last 2 000 it (mean 0.5746); N = 3: 0.492739 at 1010 it, mean 0.4860 over the last 2 000 it (0.4844-0.4875). So the half domain's value at a stop is one phase of an oscillation of 0.3-1 % and the sector's a settled value 0.3-0.6 % high, but the converged difference is still +3.4 % / +1.9 %. The stop rule is not changed.
    (b) *Grid: one real difference, the cells across the fin.* The grids are the same where they should be: `gs_diag grid 4` finds the same 64 chord cells (first / last cell 0.00469 d, largest 0.02625 d), the same meridian nodes (max |dx| 0), the same azimuthal stacks (the half domain's between-fin gap is two sector gaps; the cell angles 0.030 deg at the fin to 10.8 deg at mid-gap are equal) and the same cell counts per fin. The difference is in the fin itself: `fin_cells_across` 1 gives the sector's *half* fin one cell (`max(n / 2, 1)`) and the half domain's *whole* fin one cell, so the sector resolves the leading-edge and trailing-edge faces with two cells across (the mirror image) and the half domain with one; the nodes then differ by 4.8e-5 d in radius (the polygon scale of a different cell width). Effect: the leading-edge face drag at N = 4, chord 64 is 0.042156 (sector) against **0.044056** (half domain, +4.5 %), at chord 32 0.032226 against 0.035508 (+10 %); the same drag at **2 cells across in both** is 0.042354 / 0.042341 (-0.03 %; N = 3 0.031735 / 0.031743) and the node comparison is exact (max |dr| 2.8e-14, 728 754 sector nodes matched by azimuth). The sector's own grid is unchanged by the 2 (its half fin owns `n / 2` = 1 cell either way) and its LE drag moves 0.5 %. The gate now takes 2 cells across in both domains on the stack tiers (the Euler tier replicates cell counts with `nk` and keeps 1).
    (c) *Physics: the half domain's free fins have an asymmetric, oscillating wake behind the blunt trailing edge, the sector's fin plane is a symmetry plane.* With the cells equalised the leading edge, the sides, the nose and the friction agree to 0.05-0.5 % and the whole difference is the trailing-edge face and the base: N = 4 half domain (two whole fins) trailing-edge drag **0.23697** per 4 fins against the sector's **0.21334** (+11 %, both fins alike; the base 0.17865 against 0.17472), N = 3: the half fin on the `theta = 0` plane of the in-plane half domain has 0.15849 against 0.15664 (**+1.2 %**: a symmetry plane there too) and the whole fin at 120 degrees 0.16967 (**+8.3 %**), whose trailing-edge face Cp is asymmetric about the fin plane (-0.539 below, -0.571 above; the half fin cannot be; 20261119-060337-28813 remote N = 4, 20261119-060425-12927 local N = 3, 5 000 it). The half domain keeps a CA oscillation of 0.3-1 % for 16 000 iterations; the sector's is 0.02 %. At 32 chord cells the trailing-edge cells are twice as long (0.0094 d against the 0.08 d of the fin's trailing edge) and the trailing-edge drags agree (the asymmetry is not resolved there, [INFERENCE] damped by the coarser cells): sector 0.545289 / half 0.548498 (+0.6 %), trailing-edge drag 0.207829 / 0.207284, base 0.178035 / 0.179075 (20261119-053334-28412): that is why the gate passed at 32 and did not at 64. Mach 2, the wake supersonic and steady: with 2 cells across the two domains agree to **0.003 %** (N = 4 0.554482 / 0.554464, N = 3 0.511148 / 0.511137, 1010 / 910 it each).
    *Result.* G-S Mach 0.8 N = 4 / 3 with 2 cells across (default stop): sector 0.559531 / 0.478874, half 0.585351 / 0.489936 (+4.6 % / +2.3 %), which is a physical difference of the domains (an unsteady, asymmetric trailing-edge wake against a constrained steady one), not a grid or a convergence one; [INFERENCE] the symmetric sector is the less physical of the two for a blunt-trailing-edge fin at Mach 0.8 and under-predicts the trailing-edge suction of the free fins (not tested against data: the sector tables, item 46, are the numbers the free-flight comparison used). Gate run (20261119-061607-23537 remote Mach 0.8, 20261119-061607-19346 local Mach 2, `gate_s_sector_vs_half_on_the_stack_grids`, `BOWSHOCK_FIN_TIER=coarse`, GPU): Mach 2 both PASS, Mach 0.8 both `FAIL KNOWN`. The 1 % band stays; the subsonic G-S lines are `FAIL KNOWN` (`check_known`, as the subsonic G-F1) and the supersonic ones gate. For the product: the sector tables (CD0) are the steady-wake solution; the incidence half domain (1 cell across, Lift / Coarse) resolves the leading edge about 4 % too high in the leading-edge face (0.3 % of CD) and, with 64 chord cells, may show the asymmetric wake at Mach < 1 (not run at incidence). `fin_cells_across` 2 is the half-domain setting that matches the sector.

Request: a `trim`-like call that releases the recorded intermediates of a compiled loop that the replay never reads, or size-class slack reporting in `memory_stats` (resident 825 k = 688 k cells: classes cost up to 2x)."

54. **SST compressibility (dilatation-dissipation) corrections: opt-in, inert at the production grid, a real but small base-pressure effect where the shear layer is resolved; default unchanged (compcorr, 2026-11-19).** *Feature (opt-in):* `sst::Compressibility {Off, Sarkar, Wilcox, Zeman, WilcoxShear}`, `RansOpts3::compressibility`, `Case3::with_compressibility`, `finned_table --comp off|sarkar|wilcox|zeman|wilcox-shear`, `tools/comp_sweep.sh [--wedge] MACH COMP...`, `examples/base_flow.rs` (Herrin-Dutton cylinder base flow, CPU `--cpu` or GPU). All variants scale the destruction terms with the Wilcox pair `beta*_c = beta* (1 + xi F(M_t))`, `beta_c = beta - beta* xi F` (floored at 0), `M_t^2 = 2k/a^2` (`CellIn::a2 = gamma p / rho`; CPU `Sst::terms`, GPU `Sst3::terms`, expression for expression): Sarkar `xi 1, F = M_t^2`; Wilcox `xi 3/2, F = (M_t^2 - 1/16) H`; Zeman (Rumsey form) `xi 3/4, M_t0 0.2, Lambda 0.66`; WilcoxShear = Wilcox with `M_t^2 (1 - F1)` (Brown's "sstc2", wall layer masked). `Off` builds no extra operation (bit-identical). Tests: `turbulence3::tests::compressibility_corrections_change_the_destruction_above_their_threshold` (PASS, CPU), s5a_turb.rs: 6 new CPU-vs-GPU cases (Sarkar / Wilcox / Zeman / WilcoxShear on the box, Wilcox / WilcoxShear on the sector, `M_t` 0.1-1.5 over the cells so every branch acts; job `3-20261119-130817-8966`, **PASS on the remote 3080 Laptop, job `0-20261119-172844-24898`: `turbulence_kernels_match_cpu` and `turbulence_update_launches_and_time`, 2 of 2**), `plate3` `compressibility_corrections_on_the_supersonic_turbulent_plate` (`--ignored`, CPU, 253 s). CPU `-p bowshock-cfd -p bowshock-core` nextest 332 PASS, clippy `-D warnings` and rustfmt clean (before the last two example edits, which compile).

    *Literature.* Herrin & Dutton (AIAA J. 32(1), 1994): `M` 2.46, `D` 63.5 mm, `Re_D` 3.3e6, `delta*` 0.96 / `theta` 0.26 mm ahead of the base, mean base `Cp_b = -0.102` (flat, +3.9 % at the rim), rear stagnation point `x/R` 2.65, maximum reverse velocity 0.27 `U` at `x/R` 1.5. Standard RANS under-predicts the shear-layer turbulence production balance, gives a short recirculation and a base pressure that is too low; models with compressibility corrections raise the mean base pressure towards the data but make the base profile wavy (Simon et al., AIAA J. 2007; Garbaruk et al., DESIDER ch. 11 for SA with Spalart's CC; Forsythe, Hoffmann, Squires, J. Fluids Eng. 124(4), 2002: the CC changes the base pressure by 5-10 % depending on the grid; Das & De 2016). Wilcox (Turbulence Modeling for CFD, 3rd ed., 5.4.4) `xi* 3/2, M_t0 1/4` is the form used for SST by Brown (2002, `sstc1`; `sstc2` = `1 - F1` masked) and Kedia & Sinha 2006 (Fire II afterbody: larger bubble, higher base heating); Rumsey (NASA/TM-2009-215705): Sarkar / Zeman lower attached skin friction and the separation size, Wilcox's threshold limits that, adiabatic walls up to Mach 10 change little, cold walls a lot. Chosen: **Wilcox** (thresholded, BL-safe) with the masked variant for completeness; Sarkar and Zeman kept as the comparison. Not implemented: SST-RC (Spalart-Shur / Smirnov-Menter; needs the Lagrangian derivative of the strain tensor, a second gradient pass on the GPU, and targets swirl / curvature, not mixing-layer growth) and the Mc-based production reductions (SA-specific calibrations).

    *Attached boundary layers (CPU, `plate3`, G2 grid, `Re_x` 5e6 x, adiabatic):* cf(0.97) against the plain SST: Mach 2 Sarkar **-6.9 %**, Wilcox / Zeman / WilcoxShear **0.00 %**; Mach 3 Sarkar **-12.3 %**, Wilcox 0.00 %, Zeman -0.02 %, WilcoxShear 0.00 % (`M_t` stays under 1/4 in a flat-plate layer). Sarkar hurts attached layers as the literature says; the thresholded forms do not. Mach 0.2 G2 / subsonic cases are unchanged by construction (`M_t` tiny; `Off` is bit-identical).

    *Herrin-Dutton base flow* (`base_flow`, wedge, Coarse tier sized for `Re_c = Re_D / M`, sutherland 110.4/131, 7 degree cone nose + cylinder; no sting; the approach layer is set by the cylinder length; CPU f64, the GPU f32 run of `1-20261119-132345-2311` (remote 3080) reproduces CPU `Cp_b` to 0.0001: -0.1837 vs -0.1838, 2.9 s):

    | grid / approach layer | Off | Sarkar | Wilcox | Zeman | WilcoxShear | measured |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Coarse, len 0.5 D (theta 0.17 mm): `Cp_b` | -0.1838 | -0.1872 | -0.1838 | | | -0.102 |
    | Coarse, len 2.4 D (theta 0.22 mm) | -0.1615 | -0.1643 | -0.1611 | -0.1612 | -0.1612 | |
    | Coarse, len 4 D (theta 0.28 mm) | -0.1490 | | -0.1491 | | | |
    | Coarse, len 7 D (theta 0.39 mm) | -0.1386 | | | | | |
    | refine 2 (82 k cells), len 4 D (theta 0.32 mm): `Cp_b` | -0.1402 | -0.1339 | **-0.1344** | -0.1355 | -0.1336 | |
    | same, rear stagnation `x/R` (measured 2.65) | 2.33 | 2.68 | **2.63** | 2.53 | 2.64 | |
    | same, `CD` | 0.23877 | 0.22819 | 0.23292 | 0.23404 | 0.23214 | |
    | refine 2, len 3 D: `Cp_b` / `x/R` | -0.1440 / 2.32 | | -0.1378 / 2.62 | | | |
    | max `M_t^2` (Off) in the field | 0.062 (len 0.5), 0.12 (2.4), 0.14 (4), 0.29 (refine 2) | | | | | |

    Findings: (1) the base pressure of the plain SST is **-0.14 to -0.18 against -0.102** (+35 to +80 %), the recirculation 1.6-2.3 R against 2.65, and depends first on the incoming layer thickness (theta 0.17 -> 0.39 mm: -0.184 -> -0.139) and on the shear-layer grid (refine 2: -0.149 -> -0.140), not on the correction. (2) At the **Coarse** grid the corrections are inert on this case: the shear-layer `M_t^2` of the SST solution peaks at 0.06-0.14 (threshold 0.0625): `Cp_b` changes by 0.0000-0.0004. (3) Where the shear layer is resolved (refine 2, `M_t^2` up to 0.29, 770 cells above threshold) Wilcox raises `Cp_b` by +0.006 (base drag -4.2 %, `CD` -2.4 %) and lengthens the bubble from 2.33 to **2.63 R (measured 2.65)**; the maximum reverse velocity worsens (0.49 against 0.27). It closes only 15 % of the base-pressure error: the rest is the layer thickness and grid. refine 3 (185 k cells, `--len 4`, theta 0.32 mm): Off `Cp_b` -0.1394, `x/R` 2.37, `CD` 0.23809; Wilcox **-0.1297** (+0.0097, base drag -6.9 %, `CD` -4.1 %), `x/R` 2.78, 1500 cells above threshold; so the Wilcox effect grows with the shear-layer resolution (Coarse 0.000, refine 2 +0.006, refine 3 +0.010) and has not saturated; the plain model barely moves with the grid (-0.1402 -> -0.1394).

    *Finner (GPU f32, Coarse tier, 64 chord cells, drift tol 5e-4; remote RTX 3080 Laptop, job `1-20261119-140010-4546`):* finless ANF wedge `CD` Off / Wilcox / Sarkar: **M 1.85 0.35746 / 0.35243 (-1.4 %) / 0.34664 (-3.0 %)**, M 2.364 0.30466 / 0.30296 (-0.6 %) / 0.29801 (-2.2 %), M 2.741 0.27279 / 0.27292 (+0.05 %) / 0.26702 (-2.1 %); base part Off / Wilcox: 0.17282 / 0.16780, 0.14169 / 0.13999, 0.12179 / 0.12192. Sarkar's drop is mostly body friction (0.0750 -> 0.0702: the attached-layer damage above). Four-fin sector M 2.364: `CD` 0.48335 / 0.48172 (-0.3 %, base 0.16218 / 0.16233, fin pressure 0.13588 / 0.13410: the difference is the fin trailing-edge face, inside the stop-rule noise: 2610 / 1710 iterations) / Sarkar 0.47522 (-1.7 %, friction). Fin MT2: max 0.29, 33 k of 570 k fluid cells above 1/16. Solve cost: ms/it 24.2 / 24.7 / 24.9 (+2 %). The M 1.85 and 2.741 sector rows (`cc-sweep-sector-L`, local 3090) were still queued at hand-off.

    *Recommendation.* **Do not make a correction the default.** The Wilcox form is safe (attached layers 0.00 %, subsonic unchanged, +2 % per iteration, no iteration-count change beyond the stop-rule noise) but moves the supersonic Finner by -0.3 to -1.4 %, and only where the base shear layer has `M_t > 1/4`; against the +2.6 / +6.7 % grid-converged gap this is a small share at best (the wedge M 2.741 row, the +6.7 % point, moves 0.05 %). The large base-pressure errors of the SST model on a blunt base come from the incoming layer and the shear-layer resolution, which this correction does not fix; the laminar-nose run of item 50 is a larger lever. Sarkar must not be used (cf -7 / -12 % at Mach 2 / 3). Full-solve impact: none measurable (+2 % per iteration only when enabled; the default path is unchanged).

55. **Cross-validation beyond the Basic Finner: transonic Finner CD0, the Arcas, the Lewis sounding-rocket stage, the AEDC research model (validate2, 2026-11-19). The Finner's pattern does not carry over as a constant: the supersonic lift generalises (CN_alpha +0..+13 %, x_cp within 0.3 cal), the subsonic lift over-prediction does (+7..+21 % on the AEDC model), the +3-7 % fully turbulent CD0 does not (the sign and size follow the friction share and the fin).** *Code:* `bowshock_reference::{arcas_stab, yuska, grm}` (sourced data with uncertainty), `tests/fin_gates/rockets.rs` (the articles as `Case3` builders), `tests/rocket_gates.rs` (G-X1..G-X6, `#[ignore]`d, same `GATE`/`INFO`/`FAIL KNOWN DESIGN3D-X-item-55` lines as `fin_lift_gates`), `fin_lift_gates::gate_f5s_anf_cd0_sector`, `anf::CD0` two new rows; `tools/release_gates.sh xlift|xsector` (and `gate_f5s` in `fins`; the `lift` group now names `gate_f5_anf`, because `gate_f5` also matched the sector gate). Tunnel state of each article from its report (Re/m, stagnation temperature where stated: Arcas 322 / 339 K, GRM 310 K and Yuska 300 K assumed, `WallFlow::sutherland` = 110.4 K / T_inf), fully turbulent, no sting.

(a) *Survey* (public sources only; all read from the scans):
- **Picked.** (1) Arcas, NASA TN D-4013 / D-4014 (the real sounding rocket of G-F4: this item adds the `CN_alpha` secant over 0-4 degrees, `x_cp` and the long model's `C_A,corr`; the reports give neither as numbers: figure readings, +-0.9 per rad in `CN_alpha`, +-0.09-0.27 cal in `x_cp`). (2) NASA TN D-3182 (Yuska, Lewis, 1966, `ntrs.nasa.gov/citations/19660004864`): 1/5-scale second stage of a sounding rocket, a 3/4-power nose, 6 in cylinder, four 45 degree swept thick wedge fins (10 degrees included), Mach 1.79-3.47, models A (roll 0 / 45), B (4.2 in shorter), C (fin tip trimmed): `CN_alpha`, `x_cp/d` (Fig. 4), `C_A0` with the base force removed (Fig. 5); full geometry on its Fig. 1. (3) AEDC-TR-75-125 (Gomillion 1976, Martin generalized research model, DTIC ADB010053 / ADB010054, `archive.org/details/DTIC_ADB010054`, approved for public release): B4 (2.5 d tangent ogive, 9.5 d) + F13 (clipped delta, root 1 d, tip 0.5 d, span 0.75 d, modified double wedge 4 degree edges), Mach 0.8-1.3, **tabulated** `CN`, `CLM`, `CA`, `CAB`, `CAF` per alpha (read from rendered pages, the OCR layer has errors: `grm.rs`); `CN_alpha` and `x_cp` from the incremental slopes over 0-4 degrees, `CAF` = `CA - CAB` at alpha 0. This is the public-release stand-in for the **Modified Basic Finner**: the MBF (the Air Force modified finner of Bhagwandin & Sahu, ARL; `hisa.gitlab.io/archive/asc/modifiedFinner`) is a 2.5 d tangent ogive cylinder with four trapezoidal fins, the same family; the MBF data itself (Mach 0.6-2.5) was not found in a public table.
- **Not done.** *SOCBT* (3 d secant ogive, 2 d cylinder, 1 d 7 degree boattail; BRL, Sahu et al. DTIC ADA171462 has CD vs Mach and drag parts against measurement, Kayser BRL-MR-3161 / AD A113520 has pressures only): the engine has no secant ogive (`Nose` = tangent ogive, cone, power, parabolic, Haack, blunted; the ORK importer already warns and substitutes a tangent ogive). Effort about half a day for the nose (a `Nose::Secant { fineness, rho }` with the analytic profile, `NoseShape`, `to_internal`, the tangent-point tests), plus digitising ADA171462's figures (about half a day); the boattail itself (`AftSpec::Transition`) is supported and G-F4 exercises it on the Arcas. *Launch lugs / rail buttons:* the 3D engine builds `Fins3 { count: 1 }`, so a stubby fin is a first approximation of a lug; a proper one is a solid box off the body, needing the half domain (not the sector), a resolved stack at its height (about 0.05 d) and its own wall class: 2-3 days; **no public numeric lug data was found** (DeMar NARAM-37 1995: +29 % at Re 8e5 on a 0.75 in body with an under-sized reference area; Apogee's lug study is CFD). *Canted fins:* the sector stays valid (roll symmetry) but the mesher builds fins as index-aligned radial slabs; a cant needs the fin layer to lean with the axial position (skewed `k` lines in the fin region), roll-moment output and the spin balance: 1-2 weeks; data exist (DREV-TM-9703 `Cl_delta`, Arcas D-4013 2 degree cant at roll 0, Yuska 0.8 degree). *Mesher limits met here:* `plan_fins` refuses a fin outline that reaches behind its own root trailing edge ("a fin may not overhang the base": the Yuska fin's tip trailing edge hangs 2.34 in behind its root's, and the base plane) and a 45 degree leading-edge sweep over a span of 0.825 d folds the cells above the tip (negative cell volume at the tip ramp of 4 rows; 33.7 and 28 degrees build, thickness is not the cause: probe with 0.25-1x thickness identical). Lifting it: tip-ramp rows proportional to the sweep and a trailing-edge rake; about a day with the grid-quality tests. Gates use the area-matched planform (drawing's root chord, tip chord and span, vertical trailing edge on the base plane, 28 degree leading edge).

(b) *Transonic Finner CD0* (the request of Main; `anf::CD0` rows from DREV-TM-9703 Table VIII p. 44, zero-cant single fits, image checked: shot DA95022010 Mach 1.056 0.868 (`Cl_delta` -0.001), DA95022009 Mach 1.332 0.701 (-0.011; the canted shots of the page, 1.058 / 1.116 / 1.254 / 1.377, have 0.29-0.93); the canted 1.058 shot reads the same 0.868; band stays 8 %, the Mach spread of the table (-0.6 in CD per Mach) is the resolution limit: +-0.02 in Mach is +-1.4 %). Measured (identical to six digits on both hosts, f32): **Coarse sector, 64 chord cells (`gate_f5s`)** Mach 1.056 0.884039 (**+1.8 %**, 810 it), 1.332 0.738269 (**+5.3 %**, 1610 it `Settled`; parts: body pressure +0.1348, base +0.2504, fin pressure +0.2369, friction body 0.0848 / fins 0.0314); **Lift half domain (`gate_f5`, 32 chord cells)** 1.056 0.868656 / 0.867268 between / in-plane (**+0.1 / -0.1 %**), 1.332 0.719963 / 0.718463 (**+2.7 / +2.5 %**). Jobs: sector local `1-20261119-125331-13366`, remote `1-20261119-125332-31700`; Lift local `1-20261119-125332-8533`, remote `1-20261119-131019-22911`. All four PASS the 8 % band; the Coarse sector is above the Lift domain by 1.8 / 2.6 %, the sign of the grid convergence of item 47 (more leading-edge cells, more fin pressure). With the product's drag nodes densest at Mach 0.9-1.3, the Finner's CD0 error there is +2..+5 % (sector), the same band as the supersonic +3..+7 %.

(c) *Results* (measured / data - 1; `CN_alpha` per rad on the body cross-section, `x_cp` in cal from the nose tip, GPU f32, jobs below; the Lift tier half domain, fins in the pitch plane unless noted, 2 degrees (Yuska) or the 0-4 degree secant at 4 degrees (Arcas, GRM: the definition of their data) with the 2 degree value in the log):
- **G-X1 Arcas short** (remote `1-20261119-132441-31899`, commit 467a1f6), Mach 0.6 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 0.95 / 1.0 / 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.3 / 2.96: `CN_alpha` -10.6 / -15.9 / +3.7 / -1.0 / -7.6 / +1.0 / +5.3 / +0.4 / +5.3 / +3.3 %; `x_cp` +0.49 / +0.55 / +0.88 / +0.11 / -0.69 / -0.60 / +0.26 / +0.12 / -0.07 / -0.12 cal. Supersonic (1.5-2.96): all within 5.3 % and 0.26 cal. Mach 0.6-0.9 and 1.0-1.2: the data's own `x_cp` is that of a model "almost neutral" at -2..2 degrees and its `CN` is non-linear; the solver's `CN(2)` at Mach 0.6 / 0.8 is 8.1 / 4.8 against 12.2 / 10.3 at 4 degrees (same non-linearity, stronger; Mach 0.8 stopped `MeanStationary` at 4980 it): `CN_alpha` Mach 0.8 and `x_cp` Mach 0.6-1.2 are `FAIL KNOWN`.
- **G-X2 Yuska** (remote `0-20261119-140111-7263`, formerly `1-...`; 2 degrees, 610 it `Settled` each, except the 8 x 6 ft Mach 1.79 / 1.88 at 2700 / 1650 it `CdStationary`): `CN_alpha` model A roll 0, Mach 2.00 / 2.09 / 2.38 / 2.78 / 3.17 / 3.47: +12.0 / +6.6 / +6.3 / +7.7 / +7.1 / +5.6 %; A roll 45 (between) Mach 2.00 / 2.38 / 2.78 / 3.17 / 3.47: +9.3 / -0.7 / -0.1 / +4.5 / +8.9 %; B (Mach 2.00-3.47): +9.7 / +12.7 / +9.2 / +8.4 / +0.7 %; C: +12.9 / +13.0 / +6.3 / +11.8 / +6.1 % (mean of the 21 lines +7.5 %); `x_cp` -0.04 to -0.32 cal, growing with Mach (A roll 0: -0.10 / -0.05 / -0.10 / -0.14 / -0.15 / -0.32). The 8 x 6 ft tunnel's points (INFO): +4 to +13 %, x_cp within 0.07 cal. `FAIL KNOWN`: A's `x_cp` at Mach 3.47 (-0.32 / -0.30 cal, band 0.25), C's `CN_alpha` at Mach 2.38 (+13.0 %, the data point is off its own trend 10.30 / 9.21 / 9.00). The model changes (B 4.2 in shorter, C smaller fin) are followed in sign and size: `x_cp` B - A -0.54 cal at Mach 2 (data -0.58), C - A -0.15 (data -0.20).
- **G-X3 AEDC B4 + F13** (remote `1-20261119-132441-31899`), Mach 0.80 / 0.85 / 0.92 / 0.94 / 0.96 / 0.98 / 1.05 / 1.10 / 1.20 / 1.30: `CN_alpha` +7.5 / +13.3 / +21.4 / +13.4 / +9.2 / +8.7 / +3.4 / +5.0 / +3.5 / +5.5 %; `x_cp` +0.06 / +0.07 / +0.16 / +0.03 / -0.06 / -0.03 / -0.10 / -0.10 / -0.12 / -0.03 cal (all within the 0.25 band). The solver's `CN_alpha` is smooth (10.6-11.9) while the data dips to 9.3 at Mach 0.92 on one run (the report's repeat scatter +-0.3; the third-party note on this data calls the dip unconfirmed): Mach 0.85 / 0.92 / 0.94 `FAIL KNOWN`.
- **G-X4 Arcas long** `C_A,corr` (local `1-20261119-132441-13290`, remote `1-20261119-140111-23404`, identical to five digits): fins on at Mach 0.6 / 0.7 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 0.95 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.2 +7.6 / +7.8 / +6.5 / -4.0 / -13.5 / -17.1 / -16.8 / -12.3 %; fins off +16.8 / +16.5 / +16.0 / +14.8 / +10.4 / +15.9 / +6.7 / -1.3 %; increment (on - off) at Mach 0.6 / 0.8 passes, 0.9-1.2 is 30-50 % of the data's (0.123 / 0.185 / 0.174 / 0.146 / 0.128 against 0.181 / 0.296 / 0.357 / 0.291 / 0.199): the same under-prediction of the double-wedge fin's transonic and supersonic increment as G-F4 (item 37). Fins-off Mach 0.6 / 0.8 `FAIL KNOWN` (body friction 0.235 of the 0.29 [INFERENCE: where the +0.04 is]).
- **G-X5 Yuska `C_A0`** (same jobs as G-X4): **+19 to +34 % at every one of the 16 points** (A Mach 2.00 0.568 against 0.451 ... Mach 3.47 0.355 against 0.266): body pressure 0.04-0.05, body friction 0.06-0.08, fin friction 0.03, **fin pressure 0.20-0.41** of which the fins' trailing-edge faces (flush with the base, 0.9 of the body area) are a share the log prints. The report's base correction does not say whether it covers them, and the fin is a substitute planform: `FAIL KNOWN`, unexplained ([INFERENCE] for both causes); diagnostic (remote `0-20261119-163616-30405`, Mach 2.0 / 3.17): the fins' trailing-edge faces carry +0.26 / +0.12 of model A's CA (leading-edge faces +0.08), so the data lies **between** the force with them (+26 / +26 %) and without them (0.312 against 0.451, -31 %, at Mach 2.0; 0.259 against 0.302, -14 %, at Mach 3.17; model B Mach 3.17 -11 %, within 12 %): the base correction of the report is the open question, the fin planform the second; the 8 x 6 ft Mach 1.79 / 1.88 / 2.08 sector runs ended **non-finite** (1550 / 3649 / 8830 it) and Mach 1.98 took 11 280 it: see (e).
- **G-X6 AEDC `CAF`** (same jobs): Mach 0.80 / 0.85 / 0.92 / 0.94 / 0.96 / 0.98 / 1.05 / 1.10 / 1.20 / 1.30 **+13.6 / +20.4 / +17.1 / +15.6 / +4.6 / +6.3 / -7.1 / -6.5 / -10.3 / +1.9 %** (friction 0.124 of the 0.158 at Mach 0.8). Mach 0.80 / 0.85 `FAIL KNOWN`, 0.92 / 0.94 INFO (repeat scatter 0.02-0.05); the data's transition is "unknown" (no grit documented).

(d) *Does the Finner pattern generalise?* **Lift: yes in the supersonic range, and the subsonic over-prediction too**: `CN_alpha` supersonic +0..+5 % (Arcas, Mach 1.5-2.96), -1..+13 % (Yuska), +3..+9 % (AEDC Mach 0.96-1.3) against the Finner's +3..+10 %; subsonic AEDC +7.5..+21 % (Finner +11 %; the Arcas goes the other way at Mach 0.6 / 0.8, -11 / -16 %, with the non-linearity above). `x_cp` within 0.3 cal wherever the data defines it (AEDC within 0.16, Arcas supersonic 0.26, Yuska 0.32 at Mach 3.47). **Drag: no.** The Finner's +3..+7 % (fully turbulent, adiabatic wall) was a body-friction-and-base number; here the error has both signs and follows the case: friction-dominated long bodies and low Mach are 13-20 % high (Arcas long fins off, AEDC Mach 0.8-0.94: consistent with a tunnel that is not fully turbulent, [INFERENCE], the Finner's prescribed-transition item 50 pointing the same way), the Arcas sharp double-wedge fins are 40-50 % low in their transonic / supersonic increment (as G-F4, a property of that fin and its test article, item 37), the transonic AEDC model is -7..-10 % at Mach 1.05-1.2, and the thick-wedge Yuska fins are +19..+34 %. The Finner CD0 can therefore not be read as the solver's bias; neither the CD0 nor the supersonic lift deserve tuning to it.

(e) *Found on the way.* (1) The Coarse sector at the Yuska 8 x 6 ft state (Re/ft 5e6, grid sized for Mach 3.47) does not converge at Mach 1.79 / 1.88 / 2.08 (non-finite residual with the CFL controller on, identical on both hosts) and needs 11 280 iterations at 1.98, while the 2.0-3.47 points of the lower Reynolds number take 810-4560 (thick fins, t/c 0.17): a robustness defect of the product path on a valid case, not investigated here (candidates: the 4 rows of the fin tip taper over 0.23 d of thickness, the CFL controller watching the residual and not positivity, the open point of the perf5 section). (2) Hosts agree to the printed digit on every repeated case (sector 1.056 / 1.332, Arcas long x 10 Mach, Yuska x 16): the 3080 Laptop's results can stand for the 3090's. (3) `yuska.rs` Table II Mach 2.08 prints a dynamic pressure 5.9 % above the isentropic value (misprint of the report, checked on the scan).

(f) *Usage.* `tools/release_gates.sh xlift` (G-X1..3, Lift) and `xsector` (G-X4..6, Coarse), `fins` now includes `gate_f5s`; `BOWSHOCK_GATE_MACHS` filters Mach lists as before. Times: G-X1+G-X3 1592 s on the 3080 Laptop (20 Machs x 2 angles), G-X2 921 s (26 points, 1 angle), sector gates 1193 s on the 3090 (G-X4 8 Machs x fins on / off, G-X5 20 points, G-X6 10 points).

56. **Diameter steps in the product path; flight-data validation harness (flightval, 2026-11-19) - steps done, the validation table measured for the 3D engine on 2 of 6 rockets.** *Steps:* `AftSpec::Step { aft_diameter }` (new, `bowshock-core::spec`) maps to item 29's one-cell chamfer (`Segment::step`): as tall as the step, at most 0.1 nose diameter wide (so the slope is at least 1), taken from the section after it so the body keeps its length; a step taller than 0.5 nose diameters of radius is `InvalidInput` (`STEP_HEIGHT_MAX`). `cfd/plan.rs` maps a step of the vehicle (radius jump above 0.5 mm) to `Step` under `BOWSHOCK_ENGINE=3d` and still refuses it for the legacy engine (and `Analysis::run` refuses it under `Engine::Legacy`); a fin root across a step stays refused by `mesh_check`, the fin set then goes to the build-up with the mesher's reason. Tests: `spec::tests::a_step_keeps_the_length_and_moves_the_diameters`, `wedge.rs::the_facades_steps_build_for_every_height_and_place` (forward/backward, 0.001-0.25 d, behind the nose / between tubes / before a boattail / at the base, levels 0 and 1, grids build, no negative volume), `analysis.rs::the_legacy_engine_refuses_a_diameter_step`, `plan.rs::a_step_in_diameter_is_solved_by_the_3d_engine`. *GPU evidence (RTX 3090, jobs `0-20261119-140929-21840` stepped, `0-20261119-140930-32591` unstepped control, Mach 0.8 sector, 3 in rocket with a 3.0 in nose behind a 3.126 in airframe and a 3.19 in fin can 1 in ahead of the fins):* the stepped rocket solves and settles (19 150 iterations, `Settled`, 252 s) but so does the unstepped control, the Rabia short-fin-can rocket with a conical shoulder (19 250 iterations, 8 restarts, CFL 100 to 5.8, 276 s): the slowness is **not** the steps. A probe with a 0.5 in fin tip chord (job `0-20261119-144403-14740`) took 27 650 iterations, 337 s, the same 8 restarts (CFL 100 to 5.8). The blunt nose tip is not the cause (Draft, `FLIGHTVAL_BLUNT_IN=0`: still 8 rollbacks); the cause is in item 57b below. The example rocket's points take about 10 s. The full stepped run on both hosts and the remote run were not made (queue). CPU wedge probe (viscous Coarse, Mach 0.8, a sharp cone with 0, 1 and 2 steps) settled in 1 310 iterations without a restart. *Validation harness:* `crates/bowshock/src/analyze/cfd/tests/flights.rs` (`flight_validation`, ignored; `FLIGHTVAL_SOLVE=1 FLIGHTVAL_OUT=out FLIGHTVAL_CASES=a,b FLIGHTVAL_MACHS=...`, `flight_fixtures_build_and_fly` on the CPU) with six rockets in `assets/validation/flights/*.json` (sources and licences in its README: RASAero II comparison table and example files; cancer-descending, byrum, ion-drive, rabia-short-fin-can, torrent, dont-debate-this; A-601 dropped, its Loki P4935 motor is in no catalogue). Fixture fins that overhang the base are moved forward (mesher rule: overhang at most half the root-to-base distance). Vehicle set-up is checked against RASAero's own peak velocities (Mach 0.57 / 0.76 / 0.81 / 0.90 / 1.29 / 3.16 against 0.58 / 0.75 / 0.83 / 0.89 / 1.27 / 3.2). **Measured (CPU, the app's build-up drag through its flight simulator, no CFD table):** apogee error against the measured one +8.8 % (Cancer Descending), +16.4 % (Byrum), +3.9 % (Ion Drive), +7.4 % (Rabia SFC), +18.7 % (Torrent), +15.9 % (Don't Debate This): mean +11.9 %, 3/6 within 10 %; RASAero II on the same six +2.3, -7.9, +7.7, -2.0, +8.2, +10.1 (mean +3.1 %, 5/6 within 10 %). **CFD-table columns (integrate, 2026-11-19; `flight_validation` without `FLIGHTVAL_SOLVE`, CPU, the stored tables flown through the app's flight simulator, `assets/validation/flights/tables/`):** the legacy tables are the remote 3080 Laptop job `0-20261119-201315-25233` (all six, 41-84 s each); the 3D tables are the local 3090 job `0-20261119-201315-12026` (commit d65ac9c, `BOWSHOCK_ENGINE=3d`: `dont-debate-this` 982 s, 10 points + 0 angle points, Mach 2.3 after 2 CFL restarts; `rabia-short-fin-can` 1 000 s, 10 points + 0 angle points: its angle points all fell back). **Four 3D tables do not exist:** `byrum` (remote job, 5 253 s: 1 of 10 drag points converged (Mach 0.1), nine ended `Fallback`, 3 360-27 560 iterations each, so no table: "the solver did not converge at enough Mach numbers", and the test stopped there, so `cancer-descending` and `ion-drive` of that job never ran), `torrent` (local job: drag Mach 0.8 / 0.9 `Fallback` after 22 300 / 4 620 iterations, the other eight settled; the filtered log ends after the last drag point and no table was written, reason not logged), `cancer-descending`, `ion-drive`. They need a rerun of the integrated tree (`gpuq submit --host remote -- env BOWSHOCK_ENGINE=3d FLIGHTVAL_SOLVE=1 FLIGHTVAL_OUT=out FLIGHTVAL_CASES=cancer-descending,ion-drive,byrum,torrent cargo nextest run --release -p bowshock flight_validation --run-ignored only --no-capture`, without the `grep` that hid the failure lines) and are the open work of 57b / 57c (subsonic Byrum and Torrent points that do not settle; Coarse supersonic Rabia). Apogee against the measured one:

    | rocket | motor | measured ft | peak Mach | 3D ft | 3D % | legacy ft | legacy % | build-up ft | build-up % | RASAero ft | RASAero % |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Byrum (LDRS 26) | AeroTech J570 | 5732 | 0.76 | - | - | 6801 | +18.7 | 6671 | +16.4 | 5280 | -7.9 |
    | Cancer Descending | AeroTech M1297 | 6188 | 0.57 | - | - | 6745 | +9.0 | 6736 | +8.8 | 6328 | +2.3 |
    | Don't Debate This | Cesaroni N5800 | 56573 | 3.16 | 60926 | +7.7 | 59355 | +4.9 | 65561 | +15.9 | 62308 | +10.1 |
    | Ion Drive | AeroTech K550 | 8027 | 0.81 | - | - | 8645 | +7.7 | 8343 | +3.9 | 8642 | +7.7 |
    | Rabia, short fin can | Cesaroni L730 | 10584 | 0.90 | 11322 | +7.0 | 11736 | +10.9 | 11365 | +7.4 | 10376 | -2.0 |
    | Torrent | Animal Motor Works M1850 | 12807 | 1.29 | - | - | 16028 | +25.2 | 15197 | +18.7 | 13852 | +8.2 |

    3D engine (2 rockets): mean +7.3 %, both within 10 % (largest 7.7 %); legacy engine (6): mean +12.7 %, 3 of 6 within 10 % (largest 25.2 %); build-up (6): mean +11.9 %, 3 of 6; RASAero II (6): mean +3.1 %, 5 of 6. Two rockets are too few to say the 3D engine beats the build-up (the same two: build-up +15.9 / +7.4 %, legacy +4.9 / +10.9 %); the apogee is above the measured one in every column but RASAero's, which is the sign of a drag that is too low or a thrust or mass that is too generous, not isolated. The 3D tables here are the `d65ac9c` tree's, before the integration (the uncertainty bands, boattail flag and the solved-jet opt-in do not change their drag). [INFERENCE] about the sign.

57. **Ordinary GPUs: the app's grid setting, a timing model, and draft first (draftfirst, 2026-11-19).** *The app does not solve what the timing harness measured.* `cfd::run::shared_grid()` is true under `BOWSHOCK_ENGINE=3d` (every node's grid is sized for the table's highest Mach number, 3), but `engine3d_finned_timing` solved per-point grids (`BOWSHOCK_SHARED_GRID` unset): the 266.6 s (3090) / 544 s (3080 Laptop) are per-point numbers. Through the app path (`solve_sized(.., true)`, `engine3d_draft_first`, 3080 Laptop, job `1-20261119-133546-2381`): **Coarse full solve 923.9 s shared against 544.3 s per-point** (every sector 1 043 840 cells, every angle node 1 434 048; sector iterations 18 100 against 11 300, angle iterations 8 300 against 6 800; per-point cells 469 k-1.04 M and 825 k-1.43 M).
