The harness now defaults to `shared_grid()` and `BOWSHOCK_SHARED_GRID=0|1` overrides both ways. *Shared against per-point, same code, 3080 Laptop (jobs `...-133546-2381` and `0-20261119-042356-18024`):* CD per Mach 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0: shared 0.4965 / 0.5053 / 0.5141 / 0.5268 / 0.6791 / 0.6959 / 0.6832 / 0.6400 / 0.5641 / 0.4708, per-point 0.5402 / 0.5116 / 0.5183 / 0.5262 / 0.6750 / 0.6953 / 0.6833 / 0.6400 / 0.5662 / 0.4708 (**-8.1 / -1.2 / -0.8 / +0.1 / +0.6 / +0.1 / 0 / 0 / -0.4 / 0 %**); CN at 2 degrees (Mach 0.5 ... 3.0) differs by -4.3 / +3.2 / +5.4 / +5.0 / +4.5 / +2.1 / -4.6 / 0 %, x_cp by under 0.01 m. So per-point is not within 0.5 % at Mach 0.1-0.8 (worst Mach 0.1, 8 %) and moves CN by up to 5 %: **the shared grid stays** (it is also the sizing of the validated gates, Mach 3.147) and the app's solve is the slower number. *The 3090, both ways (`engine3d_finned_timing`, `BOWSHOCK_SHARED_GRID=1` / `0`, `gpuclk` + `gpumem`, same tree):* **shared 440.6 s** (50 200 iterations, process peak 5 058 MiB, 1 829 MHz held, 339 W; job `0-20261119-141400-15143`) against **per-point 266.1 s** (38 800 iterations, 4 906 MiB, 1 827 MHz, 335 W; job `1-20261119-141400-12458`): per-point is 1.66x faster, and the app's solve on the 3090 is **440.6 s, over the 300 s target**. Shared on the 3090 against 3080L: 440.6 against 923.9 s, x2.10. CD shared against per-point on the 3090 is the same as on the 3080L to the fifth digit (per-point minus shared, relative to shared: **+8.8 / +1.3 / +0.8 / -0.1 / -0.6 / -0.1 / 0 / 0 / +0.4 / 0 %** at Mach 0.1 ... 3.0; worst Mach 0.1), so the 0.5 % condition fails at Mach 0.1, 0.5 and 0.8 and **the shared grid stays**. *Compile time per new shape: none that matters.* The first chunk of a run (kernel trace and compile plus 50 iterations) is 0.94 s on average over the 18 big runs in the shared run, whose sectors all have one shape, against 0.81 s in the per-point run, where every run is a new shape (sums 16.8 s and 14.5 s of 440.6 / 266.1 s): the shared grid's "kernels compiled once" rationale buys nothing measurable with Luma 231c986 (a state is rebuilt per node either way, `REUSED 0 of 18`). The iteration count is what costs: low-Mach nodes on the Mach-3 wall spacing need 3 400 / 1 400 / 1 700 iterations (Mach 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8) against 1 100 / 700 / 1 300 per-point, at 2.2 times the cells.

*Which sizing is right at low Mach (`finned_table --mach-max M --refine S`, `tools/gpuq/lowmach_sizing.sh`, Coarse tier, example rocket, roughness 60 um, 3090 jobs `0-20261119-151406-2187`, `-153100-32649`, `-153142-5470`, 3080L `-151406-13876`).* CD at refinement 0.75 / 1 / 1.333: **Mach 0.1 sized for Mach 3 (the shared grid) 0.4971 / 0.4944 / 0.4982 (flat, y+ max 3.3 -> 2.95), sized for its own Mach 0.5625 / 0.5436 / 0.5197 (y+ max 47 / 38 / 29: not converged, still falling toward the shared value; the excess is fin pressure drag 0.145 / 0.133 / 0.115 against 0.090-0.092)**; Mach 0.5 shared 0.5017 / 0.4999 / 0.4993, own 0.5116 / 0.5048 (the 1.333 grid does not build: negative cell volume at (153,127,0)); Mach 0.8 shared 0.5097 / 0.5068 / 0.5127, own 0.5121 / 0.5097 / 0.5068 (agree within the 0.6 % scatter). The domain is the same in every variant (`x_up` and `r_far` 149.9, `x_down` 74.9), the wake columns follow the refinement, so the gap is the wall spacing: the **shared grid is the converged one**; a grid sized for the node's own Mach number puts the fin's first cell at a flat-plate y+ of 15 whose leading-edge peak is 2.5x that (y+ max 38-47 at Mach 0.1), where the automatic wall treatment over-predicts the fin pressure. Variants at refinement 1 (3090; cells, iterations, wall s, CD against the shared value of the same run: Mach 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8 shared 0.4944 (1.04 M, 3 010, 44.8 s) / 0.4999 (1.04 M, 4 510, 153 s on the 3080L) / 0.5068 (1.04 M, 2 210, 33.7 s)): **floor 1.0** (walls sized for max(Mach, 1)) 0.4936 (-0.2 %; 835 k, 1 910, 23.1 s) / 0.5006 (+0.1 %; 835 k, 3 040, 42.3 s) / 0.5092 (+0.5 %; 835 k, 1 510, 21.2 s); **floor 1.5** 0.4927 (-0.3 %; 892 k, 2 510, 32.6 s) / 0.4991 (-0.2 %; 892 k, 3 670, 55.9 s) / 0.5065 (-0.1 %; 892 k, 2 110, 29.9 s); **own Mach with the fin first cell x 0.4** (fin y+ 6: `--fin-y1-scale`) 0.4962 (+0.4 %; 557 k, 1 510, 13.7 s) / 0.4994 (-0.1 %; 824 k, 3 040, 44.9 s) / 0.5071 (+0.06 %; 910 k, 2 110, 31.5 s); x 0.2 0.4917 (-0.5 %) / 0.5005 / 0.5088 (+0.4 %). Corrected fin sizing and the floor are both within 0.5 %; the floor is one line and costs 20-50 % less than shared at Mach 0.1-0.8 (the fin correction saves a further 10 s at Mach 0.1 only). Above Mach 1 a node sized for its own Mach agrees with the shared one (per-point minus shared +/-0.6 %, item above).

*The default.* Drag nodes are sized for `max(own Mach, DRAG_SIZING_MACH_FLOOR = 1.0)` (`cfd::sizing_mach`); the angle nodes keep the table's Mach 3 (their CN moves by up to 5 % with the sizing and the data cannot say which is right: not changed). `SOLVER_REV_3D` 1003. **App-path time on the 3090 (`engine3d_finned_timing`, job `0-20261119-153931-27931`): 402.9 s** (44 250 iterations, peak 5 082 MiB, 1 840 MHz) against 440.6 s shared and 266.1 s per-point: **the 300 s target is not met**. Drag CD against the shared run: Mach 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 -0.6 / +0.15 / +0.6 / 0 %, Mach 1.0-3.0 the per-point values (within 0.6 % of shared); the 8 angle nodes keep the Mach-3 sizing (1 434 048 cells, 8 300 iterations at 18.3 ms = about 150 s of the 403 s) and the 18 wedge seeds and per-run costs are 60 s. The remaining lever is the angle nodes' sizing (per-point would give 266 s) and needs a CN decision: CN moves by up to 5 % with it and which is closer to data is open. The 3080L (shared 923.9 s) was not re-run with the floor.

*Angle nodes: which sizing converges (Lift tier half domain at 2 degrees, example rocket, three fins, 60 um, `incidence_rans --body example --sector-fins example3 --mach-max M --refine S`, `tools/gpuq/lift_sizing.sh`, 3090, job `0-20261119-161750-22992`).* CN (nose-area basis) at refinement 0.75 / 1 / 1.333, with y+ max and wall seconds at refinement 1: **Mach 0.5** sized for 3 0.5532 / 0.5141 / 0.4940 (y+ 24 -> 16; 17.6 s: **still falling 7 % then 4 %**), own Mach 0.5510 / 0.5381 / 0.5380 (y+ 100-200; 11.2 s; settles at 0.538 but its first cell is at 100-200 wall units, far above the tier's 60), floor 1.0 0.5677 / 0.5521 / 0.5452 (y+ 87 -> 39; 19.0 s; falling); **Mach 0.8** shared 0.6217 / 0.5752 / 0.5511 (falling), own 0.6491 / 0.5587 / 0.5768, floor 1.0 0.5787 / 0.5828 / 0.5874 (the transonic half domain scatters by about 3 % between grids whatever the sizing). x_cp moves by under 0.2 cal in every variant. So **no sizing holds CN within 1 % under refinement at Mach 0.5-0.8**: the shared grid is not converged (it falls 4 % per refinement step), the own-Mach grid is at y+ 100-200, and the floor is in between; the 15 % per-point saving is not a free lunch and CN is the product's stability margin. **Mach 2.3** (3080L, job `0-20261119-161751-29999`): CN at 0.75 / 1 / 1.333 shared 0.3543 / 0.3345 / 0.3109 (falling 5.6 % then 7 %), own Mach 0.3414 / 0.3493 / 0.3267 (no trend, 4 % scatter; y+ max 89-337 in both): the lift tier on the example rocket is not grid-converged under either sizing in this refinement family, and own against shared at refinement 1 is +4.7 / -2.9 / +4.4 % at Mach 0.5 / 0.8 / 2.3, the size of the refinement noise itself. *Against the data* (G-L1, `BOWSHOCK_GATE_GRID_MACH`, GPU, job `0-20261119-173607-10311`; CN_alpha per rad, between / in-plane roll, data 16.272 / 11.918 / 10.371): Mach 0.79 gate sizing (3.147) 18.347 / 18.452 (+12.8 / +13.4 %), own 18.219 / 18.284 (+12.0 / +12.4 %), floor 1.0 18.295 / 18.372; Mach 1.728 12.894 / 12.846 against own 12.870 / 12.799 (-0.2 / -0.4 %); Mach 2.351 9.984 / 9.980 against own 9.981 / 9.959; x_cp within 0.005 cal everywhere. **On the ANF the sizing moves CN_alpha by under 1 %** (the own-Mach grid is marginally closer to the data at Mach 0.79, and the in-plane Mach 0.79 point passes the 13 % band with it), so the data cannot choose. Decision: **the angle nodes keep the Mach-3 sizing** (no `SOLVER_REV_3D` change beyond 1003, no confirming full run: nothing changed); per-point angle sizing would bring the 3090 solve to about 266 s but moves the example's CN by 3-5 % that no refinement study resolves, so it is a call for the owner of the stability numbers, not a free speed-up.

*Decision (user): the angle nodes are sized for their own Mach number too* (`sizing_mach`: `Lift => mach.min(table_max)`; `SOLVER_REV_3D` and `LIFT_REV_3D` 1004). **Confirming app-path solve on the 3090** (`engine3d_finned_timing`, job `0-20261119-181807-10028`): **314.5 s** (42 950 iterations, process peak 4 732 MiB, 1 840 MHz held, 333 W) against 402.9 s with the Mach-3 angle sizing and 440.6 s fully shared: **the 300 s target is missed by 5 %** (the angle runs are 10.9-18.0 ms per iteration at 0.83-1.43 M cells, 8 700 iterations; fixed costs 37.5 s). CN at 2 degrees at Mach 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.95 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0, own sizing against Mach-3 sizing: 0.5354 / 0.5566 / 0.6441 / 0.6201 / 0.5382 / 0.4184 / 0.3499 / 0.2807 against 0.5124 / 0.5744 / 0.6789 / 0.6514 / 0.5626 / 0.4271 / 0.3338 / 0.2807 (**+4.5 / -3.1 / -5.1 / -4.8 / -4.3 / -2.0 / +4.8 / 0 %**); x_cp 0.9027 / 0.9133 / 0.9268 / 0.9231 / 0.8995 / 0.8331 / 0.7563 / 0.6796 m against 0.9030 / 0.9174 / 0.9289 / 0.9256 / 0.9020 / 0.8365 / 0.7525 / 0.6796 (within 0.004 m). **Uncertainty to carry: the Lift-tier CN of the example rocket is not grid-converged under refinement (4-7 % per refinement step with the Mach-3 sizing, 3-6 % scatter with the own-Mach sizing; the two sizings differ by up to 5 %): the UI band on CN_alpha needs at least +-5 %.**

*Timing model* (`tools/gpu_time_model.py`, fitted on `RUN` lines of `engine3d_draft_first`; the 3090 log is the missing input, the script was not run: the 3090 `RUN` log of `engine3d_draft_first` is missing). Measured per iteration, per-point Coarse runs: 3090 `0.5 ms + 12.6 ns x cells`, 3080 Laptop `0.6 ms + 28.2 ns x cells` (ratio 2.24 on 0.47-1.43 M cells; 1.5-1.74 on the 7-22 k-cell wedges, launch bound); shared: 3080L 30.0-30.6 ms at 1.04 M cells, 40.2-41.1 ms at 1.43 M. Clocks under the solver's load (`tools/gpuq/gpuclk`): **3080 Laptop 1 302 MHz mean (960 min, 2 100 max), 88.8 W**; 3090 1 965 MHz (1 827-1 829 MHz in the two timing runs). Clock locking (`nvidia-smi -lgc`) and power limits are refused without root on both hosts (`tools/gpuq/hwprobe.sh`, jobs `0-20261119-130219-24855`, `-130220-17219`): no emulation of weaker cards. The measured ratio 2.2 is below the bandwidth ratio (2.44) and the FP32-at-held-clock ratio (about 2.6): the step follows neither alone, so the model gives a range from both (exponent about 0.85 on each). **Predictions for other cards are not tabulated here** (they need the 3090's shared run); the rule of thumb until then: time scales with 1/(memory bandwidth) to the power 0.88, so an RTX 3060 (360 GB/s) and an RTX 4060 (272 GB/s) take about 2.1x and 2.8x the 3090's time (the 3080 Laptop, 384 GB/s, takes 2.0-2.2x), an RX 6600 (224 GB/s) about 3.4x, Apple M1 (68 GB/s) about 11x: [INFERENCE], wider for non-NVIDIA. wgpu on the same NVIDIA GPU, the integrated AMD GPU of the local host (RADV, `VK_ICD_FILENAMES=.../radeon_icd.x86_64.json`) and Luma's `LUMA_MEMORY_BUDGET` emulation of 6 / 8 GB cards are scripted (`tools/gpuq/wgpu_draft.sh`, `tools/gpuq/mem_budget.sh`, test `engine3d_memory_budget`) and **not run**.

*Memory.* Measured process peak of the full per-point Coarse solve 4.6 GiB (3080L) / 4.9 GiB (3090), shared 4.8 GiB (3080L, `proc_peak_mib` 4786), plus the desktop (1.0-1.4 GiB baselines on the two hosts). The facade's guard (`gpu3.rs` `make_room`) allows `300 MiB + cells x 4100 B <= 0.95 x device memory`: 4 GiB cards hold 0.9 M cells (the Coarse sector is 1.04 M: refused), 6 GiB cards 1.416 M cells (**the shared angle nodes, 1 434 048 cells, are refused by 1.3 %**), 8 GiB cards 1.9 M cells (the full solve fits with room). A refused node under `Backend::Auto` falls back to the CPU and stays there for the rest of the solve (`solve3_on`): on a 6 GB card the app would run the angles on the CPU. Open (not changed): make a guard refusal fail the node instead of a CPU fallback, or solve the angles on a smaller grid there. Apple unified memory has no budget (`device_local_bytes` is `None` off Linux / Windows), so no guard.

*Draft first (implemented).* The app's solve of a missing or stale table on the 3D engine (`cfd::draft_first`: the 3D engine, a coarse request, `Status` of the drag table `Missing` or `Stale`; `BOWSHOCK_DRAFT_FIRST=off` turns it off; Quick, Refine and a re-solve of a fresh table go straight to their grid) is two solves of one job: the **draft grid, drag only** (`Resolution::Draft`, the safe subsonic variant below Mach 0.8 now applied by the facade, `DRAFT_SAFE_BELOW_MACH`), stored with the document when it ends (the plots and the table show it, labelled `draft grid`, the stability on Extended), then the **coarse solve** (drag and 3D angles) of the same job, which replaces it. State: `Solving.then`, `Cfd.refine`; a cancel of the refinement keeps the complete draft table, a failed draft solve goes on to the coarse one, a draft table without angles does not read as stale while the refinement runs (`State::solve_status`). Verified end to end on the scripted solves (`crates/bowshock/scripts/cfd-draft-first.drive`, `BOWSHOCK_CFD_DEMO=mid,done,mid`, screenshots read: `draft grid, then coarse` in progress; the draft table with the refinement running and the note; `Refinement cancelled; the draft table stays`), unit tests `draft_first_rule`, `the_resolutions_map_to_their_tiers`, `the_draft_tier_is_safe_below_mach_08`; CPU tests of `bowshock-core` and `bowshock` 1015 pass. *Time (3080 Laptop, shared grid, job `1-20261119-133546-2381`):* **first result 80.5 s** (Draft drag, 10 Mach numbers), final 923.9 s, total 1 004.4 s: **9 % over the coarse solve alone, the first result after 8 % of it**. 3090: not measured (the draft-first job was dropped for the timing pair).

*Draft against Coarse (same job, shared grid, 3080 Laptop):* CD at Mach 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0 = 0.4960 / 0.4979 / 0.4729 / 0.4921 / 0.6272 / 0.6712 / 0.6685 / 0.6293 / 0.5455 / 0.4585 against 0.4965 / 0.5053 / 0.5141 / 0.5268 / 0.6791 / 0.6959 / 0.6832 / 0.6400 / 0.5641 / 0.4708: **-0.1 / -1.4 / -8.0 / -6.6 / -7.6 / -3.6 / -2.2 / -1.7 / -3.3 / -2.6 %** (Draft is low everywhere, worst at Mach 0.8-1.0 where the base drag is 14-22 % low and the fins 5-8 %; friction -1 to -4 %). *Draft at an angle of attack* (the Draft half domain, 545-711 k cells; per-point grids, job `1-20261119-125826-10248`, 3080L, 202.6 s for the whole Draft solve of which 130 s the angles): CN at Mach 0.5 ... 3.0 +7.8 / +10.7 / +9.6 / +9.3 / +11.9 / +10.9 / -0.7 / +2.0 % against Coarse, x_cp 0.014-0.028 m forward: **rejected**, the draft pass takes no angles (CN is where the product is already high against data). A per-point Draft run showed CD +62 % at Mach 0.1 (friction 0.506 against 0.243) and +5-7 % at Mach 0.5-0.8; the shared-grid Draft run does not (-0.1 %), so the shared grid is also the safe one for Draft. A bug found on the way: the first revert of the facade's tier map dropped the Draft arm (Draft ran as Fine); the unit test above pins it.

57b. **Slow Rabia points: round fin edges and a swept fin can (SlowRocket, 2026-11-19; Draft M 0.8 reproduces in 46 s; tools: `tests/diag56.rs`, `grid3::print_quality`).** *Cause.* The Rabia fixture (3 fins, 10.5 in root chord, 9.6 in sweep = 66 degrees, zero tip chord, 0.1875 in `Rounded` section) at Mach 0.8 failed its CFL controller 8 times, all within the first 150-500 iterations at every CFL from 100 to 6: a cell beside the leading edge at 76 % span fell to p = 1e-5 (Mach 900) and stayed there (the point then "settled" on the vacuum at CFL 5.8: 5 750 iterations Draft, 19 250 Coarse, C_D 0.65 against 0.57 for a 0.12 in fin; `quality3` now fails a collapsed final state). Two grid defects: (1) the root leading edge lies at the start of its segment (the fin can), so the old remap had no cells ahead of it and the one cell there took the whole sweep: 2.6 d long, 2 393 times the volume of its neighbour (`ZONE_REACH`: the zone widens upstream to 3 times the move, to the end of the nose, only where the segment's room is under twice the move: the example and the ANF grids are bit for bit as before, Draft example M 0.8 C_D 0.53818 / 570 iterations before and after; `FADE_REACH`, `RAMP_REACH` keep the sheared rows from folding); (2) the round edge of radius `t/2` = 0.03 d is a polygon of 1-3 cells (first chord cell 0.3 c / nf = 0.031 d): corners that turn the flow by 60 degrees. Draft M 0.8 iterations (rollbacks): thickness 0.12 in 770 (0), Square section 770 (0), 96 / 128 chord cells 670 / 770 (0), first cell r/4 770 (0), r/8 1 570 (0), r/2 4 000+ (3), 64 chord cells 1 700 (4), no fins 1 110 (0), as it was 5 750 (8). *Fix:* `plan_fins`: for `FinProfile::Rounded` the first chord cell is at most `t / 2 / 4` (`LE_RADIUS_CELLS`), never below 0.1 c / nf (item 44). Draft after the fixes: Rabia M 0.8 / 1.3 settle in 770 / 670 iterations without a rollback (before: 5 750 and Failed after 8 rollbacks). The Mach 2.3 Draft grid folded at the leading edge until the fade of the tip's radial bending ahead of the edge was lengthened (`RAMP_REACH`); it builds now (CPU check). *Measured after (job `0-20261119-193347-29724`, RTX 3080 Laptop, commit 2daff43):* Coarse Rabia M 0.8 **570 iterations, 19 s, no rollback, C_D 0.507** (before: 19 250 iterations, 8 restarts, 276 s on the 3090); Draft M 0.8 / 1.3 770 / 670 iterations; example Draft M 0.8 / 2.3 570 / 670 iterations, C_D 0.53818 / 0.53221 (M 0.8 identical to before), example Coarse M 0.8 1 270 iterations 33 s C_D 0.51035. **Still unsolved: Rabia at Mach >= 1.3 on Coarse (M 1.3: 8 rollbacks, `NonFinite`, 242 s) and at Mach 2.3 on Draft (8 rollbacks, Failed: 7 270 iterations, 144 s)**: another mechanism than the subsonic one (not diagnosed; start with `diag56` `DIAG_MACHS=2.3 DIAG_RES=draft` and the `state_extrema` of the failing chunk). The ANF G-F5 gate (job `1-20261119-193347-15910`) and the flight-validation table were not produced.

57c. **Supersonic Rabia: the positivity floor (SlowRocket, 2026-11-19).** After 57b the Draft M 2.3 and Coarse M 1.3 points of the Rabia fixture still collapsed at the same place (the swept leading edge at 45-76 % span, p 1e-5, Mach 1 000+), sensitively to anything in the grid (Draft M 2.3 iterations / rollbacks: as 57b 3 000+ / 8, first cell r/8 970 / 0, r/16 3 000+ / 3, 96 chord cells 2 540 / 3, sweep 4 in 3 000+ / 8, square section 670 / 0, thickness 0.12 in 1 070 / 0): the grid is not what a rule can tune. The update damping of the GPU implicit step (`implicit3.rs`, `damping3`) accepted any positive density and pressure; it now accepts a cell only above `POSITIVITY_FLOOR` = 1e-2 of the free stream's, or not below its own value (a cell under it may rise). Draft (job `0-20261119-200626-2696`): Rabia M 0.8 / 1.3 / 2.3 settle in 770 / 670 / 870 iterations with **no rollback**, the example rocket Draft M 0.8 / 1.3 / 2.3 are unchanged to the digit (C_D 0.53818 / 0.67964 / 0.53221, 570 / 770 / 670 iterations). Coarse is not cured: Rabia M 1.3 stalls at 3 000 iterations (11 rollbacks over the two points) and M 2.3 "settles" in 1 510 iterations with C_D 1.139 (the Draft grid gives 0.50): a cell sits on the floor. `quality3` therefore fails a state with a cell below 1.5e-2 of the free stream's density or pressure (`clipped_state`), so the app falls back for that point instead of tabulating it. **Open: Coarse supersonic Rabia (the coarse wall-resolved grid at the swept thick fin edge); the sensitivity suggests the swept edge's sheared sliver cells (aspect 100, 17-24 degrees) rather than the resolution.** The gate and validation jobs of this commit are `0-20261119-201315-12026` (local: `release_gates.sh lift`, `fins`, then the 3D tables of rabia, torrent, dont-debate-this) and `0-20261119-201315-25233` (remote: legacy tables of the six, 3D tables of cancer-descending, byrum, ion-drive); the tables land in the jobs' `out/` (`~/.cache/bowshock-gpuq/done/<id>/out`), to be copied to `assets/validation/flights/tables/` and printed with `FLIGHTVAL_CASES= cargo test -p bowshock flight_validation -- --ignored --nocapture` (no `FLIGHTVAL_SOLVE`).

58. **Power-on base drag from the solved jet (motoron, 2026-11-19; OPT-IN in the integration, default OFF): the flight can fly the jet's effect on the base instead of the exit-area correction; the effect is solved on the body alone and carried as a ratio, because the finned sector with the jet costs more than the whole budget.** *What was there:* the jet patch (item 20, G-J item 30) existed and passed its gate, but `Engine::Solver3` refused a motor (`check_solver3`: "no motor jet yet") and the flight's `Aero::tabulated` took the table's base times the build-up's exit-area ratio (`motor_exit_area` of the motor's *case*, `Aero::each_drag`), i.e. no jet physics at all. *Route:* `Analysis::motor` is now accepted by `Engine::Solver3` on every domain (`case_of`: `Case3::with_motor` after the tier, from the same `Jet` as the legacy route, `Analysis::rans`; the checks of the legacy route: viscous, base wake, dimensional body, `MotorSpec::validate`; `Polar::motor_on`; the seeds of a finned sector carry the jet), `tests/solver3.rs::a_motor_runs_the_jet_on_the_wedge_and_its_checks_fail_loudly` (CPU). *The jet of a motor* (`analyze::cfd::plan::jet_of`, `Plan::motor`; the motor files have a case diameter and a thrust curve, no nozzle): an air jet at exit Mach 2.5 and 3 000 K whose static thrust is the motor's average thrust, `F = p_e A_e (1 + gamma M_e^2) - p_sl A_e`; `A_e = F / (gamma M_e^2 p_sl)` (the exit matched to sea level) kept between 0.5 and 0.8 of the case diameter (and 0.9 of the base), `p_e = (F / A_e + p_sl) / (1 + gamma M_e^2)` at the kept area. The example's motor (29 mm, 178 N s over 1.29 s, 137 N average) gives a 14.5 mm exit (the 0.5 floor; 13.6 mm matched) at 0.958 bar, in a 76.2 mm base (3.6 % of its area). *The part of the table* (`CfdTable::power_on: Option<Box<CfdPowerOn>>`, `CfdPowerPoint { mach, friction, pressure, base, quality }` = ratios, the jet it was solved with in `CfdJet`; `Job::power_on` = `power_on_nodes(nodes)` = 0.5 / 0.9 / 1.3 / 2.3 where the table has them, else its middle node; one work item per node after the stability nodes, not a unit of the screen's tracker: it reports its cancel only; the note of the table says what was solved): `solve_power_node` solves the body *without its fins* (the axisymmetric wedge) twice on the table's grid and stop rule, `NodeKind::PowerOff` and `NodeKind::PowerOn`, and stores the ratios of the parts. `DragTable::from_cfd(table, jet)` takes them only when `jet` (the motor's now, `Plan::jet`) is the one they were solved with (`CfdJet::same_as`), `Aero::tabulated` multiplies the table's friction, pressure and base by the ratios (linear between the nodes, held beyond them; the fins are not scaled) and no longer takes the exit area out of the base; a table without them keeps the old correction and the Simulate screen says so (`flight::drag_note`). *Why the wedge and not the sector* (finned example, 3 fins, Coarse, 3090, f32; sector jobs `1-20261119-130535-4388` on / `1-20261119-130536-14155` off, wedge `1-20261119-131824-8113` on (3090) / `1-20261119-131825-10380` off (3080 Laptop), body alone):

    *Integration decision: opt-in, off by default (integrate, 2026-11-19).* The solved effect raises the base drag x2.0 subsonic (x1.45 at Mach 0.9, x1.1-1.3 above), the opposite sign to the exit-area correction (which removes the exit area from the base) and to the convention of the OpenRocket / RASP world, and it is unvalidated: TP-2005 fails (ISSUES #13), no public set covers a small near-matched jet. So `cfd::power_on_requested()` (`plan::POWER_ON` = false, or `BOWSHOCK_POWER_ON` set) gates all of it in the app: without it `Job::power_on` is empty (**no power-on node is solved: the default full solve has no extra cost**, `engine3d_finned_timing` takes the part only with `BOWSHOCK_POWER_ON=1`), `Physics::drag_table` passes no jet to `DragTable::from_cfd` (the flight keeps the build-up's exit-area correction of the motor-on base, as before), and the Simulate note about a table without a solved jet appears only when the part is asked for. A table solved with it carries `CfdTable::power_on`, and the flight takes it when the setting is on and the jet is the stack's motor's. The engine's own capability (`Analysis::motor` under `Engine::Solver3`, `Polar::motor_on`) is unchanged and always available.

    | Mach | sector base off | sector base on | on / off | wedge base off | wedge base on | on / off | wedge - sector |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 0.5 | 0.11751 | 0.23240 | 1.978 | 0.11768 | 0.23908 | 2.032 | +2.7 % |
    | 0.9 | 0.14901 | 0.21510 | 1.443 | 0.14584 | 0.21171 | 1.452 | +0.6 % |
    | 1.3 | 0.20590 | 0.26555 | 1.290 | 0.19069 | 0.23412 | 1.228 | -4.8 % |
    | 2.3 | 0.14210 | 0.16142 | 1.136 | 0.13690 | 0.15071 | 1.101 | -3.1 % |

    The finned sector with the jet is 1.5-2 times the cells of the power-off one (0.98 M -> 1.62 M: the nozzle rows `motor_rows` put in the gap, clustered at the lip at the wall spacing: `n_gap` 38 -> about 95) and 1.3-2 times the ms per iteration (M 0.5 24.9 -> 50.6, 0.9 16.3 -> 30.0, 1.3 18.5 -> 26.3, 2.3 20.1 -> 35.2); the four nodes took 135.9 s against 88.0 s power off, i.e. **+136 s on a 266.6 s solve (403 s)**; the wedge pair is a tenth of that, and the ratio of the base drag agrees with the sector's to 5 %. The fins' own drag rises 5 % with the jet at Mach 0.5 (0.1592 -> 0.1673, fin pressure +6 %), 1.6 % of the total: not carried.
    *Cost (estimate, the full-solve measurement was still queued behind other sessions' jobs at hand-off):* the wedge pairs on the 3090 are 36.8 / 12.9 / 6.7 / 4.9 s for the jet-on wedges at Mach 0.5 / 0.9 / 1.3 / 2.3 (61.3 s with the first point's context and kernel build; M 0.5 and 0.9 need 5 300-5 500 iterations because the wedge has no DDADI) and 26.9 s for the four jet-off wedges on the 3080 Laptop (about 15 s on the 3090). In the warm process of the full solve that is about 50-60 s on top of 266.6 s, **i.e. likely over the 300 s budget**: `engine3d_finned_timing` now includes the power-on part (`BOWSHOCK_NO_POWER_ON=1` for the baseline) and prints the solve time, the ratios, the same ratios on the Draft grid (`BOWSHOCK_NO_DRAFT_RATIOS=1` skips) and the apogees; jobs `1-20261119-134037-28861` (3090) and `1-20261119-134654-6322` (3080 Laptop) hold the measurement. If it is over, the levers are the Draft grid for the pair (`solve_power_node` takes the job's grid), fewer nodes (`POWER_ON_NODES`) or DDADI on the jet wedge.
    *Validation and plausibility:* the G-J gate (item 30) is a code-parity gate against the 2D solver, and the public data set, NASA TP-2005 (Covell 1982; `bowshock-reference::tp2005`, 11 cold-air jets at Mach 1.65-2.5, exit pressures 8-13 p_inf), **fails** as before (ISSUES #13: model 0.59-1.05 p_inf against 0.95-1.67 measured, the plume-induced separation is not reproduced); `tests/validation.rs::tp2005_base_pressure_with_jet` now takes `BOWSHOCK_TP_RES=coarse` to run the product resolution (not run on `Solver3` yet). No public set was found in the near-matched, small-jet regime of a hobby motor (jet 3.6 % of the base, p_e / p_inf 0.96). The solved effect is the opposite of the old correction's: with the jet on the base drag *rises* (x2.0 / 1.45 / 1.23-1.29 / 1.10-1.14 at Mach 0.5 / 0.9 / 1.3 / 2.3; base Cp -0.12 -> -0.24 at Mach 0.5: jet pumping of the near wake by a jet whose momentum is about q A_base) where the correction took 3.6 % of the base drag off. The sign is plausible for subsonic and transonic jets (ejector pumping) but the model's known under-prediction of the plume's base-pressure rise (ISSUE 13) means the supersonic ratios are probably high: treat the power-on drag as the conservative side.
    *Not done:* the apogee effect (example and the long-burn RATT I90L, 6.5 s, `power_on::long_burn`) is printed by the same GPU job and not measured yet; the power-on nodes are not units of the solve screen's tracker (the screen shows the table finished while they run); fins are not scaled by the ratios; one jet per stack (the lowest stage's motor, no clusters).

59. **Warm start of a Mach table from the converged neighbour (warmsweep, 2026-11-19; opt-in `BOWSHOCK_WARM_START`, `Analysis::warm_start`, default OFF).** *Code:* `engine/warm3.rs` (`rescale`: velocities x `M_new/M_old`, deviations of density and pressure from the free stream x `s^2` floored at 0.1, `k`/`omega` kept; `warm_plan` / `warm_runs` / `Order`: the order and the limits), `analysis/solver3.rs` (`Carried`, `Start::Neighbour`, `WarmTuning::of(kind)`, the group/plan loop of `sweep3`), `GpuRunner3::start_cfl` + `LazySolver3::set_cfl` (a warm run goes on at the neighbour's final CFL instead of restarting the ramp at `cfl0`), app: `cfd/run.rs` `warm_start()` / `work_list` (the engine's runs are the machines' work items, results back in Mach order). The neighbour's field is kept on the host (the point's `post` download plus `turb_state`), so any order works and a failed or unsettled neighbour gives a cold/seeded start; a warm point that ends `Failed`/`Stalled` is re-run seeded/cold. Harness: `engine3d_warm_variants` (`BOWSHOCK_VARIANTS="cold default KEY=VALUE+..."`, `BOWSHOCK_WARM_HALF`, `BOWSHOCK_MACHS`), `engine3d_finned_timing` prints a `START` line per point (seed / neighbour / wedge iterations) and takes `BOWSHOCK_WARM_START`. *Integration with item 57 (integrate, 2026-11-19):* a warm chain carries one field from Mach number to Mach number and so needs one grid: with `BOWSHOCK_WARM_START` the nodes of a chain are all sized for the table's highest Mach number (`node_analysis`: `warm` takes `grid_mach`), as before item 57; the per-node sizing of item 57 (drag nodes `max(own Mach, 1.0)`, angle nodes their own Mach) applies to the cold path, which is the default.
    *Measured* (example rocket, Coarse, shared grid, f32; per-point iterations of the cold/seeded table from job `0-20261119-141400-15143`, 3090, 440.6 s / 50 200 it; sector cold 0.1 / 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0 = 3400 / 1400 / 1700 / 2000 / 2700 / 2200 / 1600 / 900 / 800 / 800 it, 2-degree half domain 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.95 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0 = 1050 / 1050 / 1250 / 950 / 1050 / 950 / 950 / 850):
    (a) *First design* (subsonic up from 0.1, supersonic down from 3.0, perturbation rescale, ramp restart; job `0-20261119-154547-7850`, 3080 Laptop, 965 s): sector 3400 / 900 / 1500 / 700 / 2800 / 2800 / 3300 / 2600 / 800 / 800 it (**+2 100 it, worse**: the supersonic points start from the axisymmetric seed in about 800-900 it, the floor of the stop rule, and a neighbour's wake and shock are further from the answer than the seed's: 1.7 from 2.3 took 2600 against 900); half domain 1050 / 1600 / 1600 (0.95) / 600 (1.1) / 800 / 900 / 700 / 850 (supersonic neighbours save 250-350 it at 1.1, 1.3, 2.3, the subsonic ones cost 500 it more; total equal). CD of the supersonic sector points +0.8 % at 1.7 and 2.3 (base +3 %) against the seeded values: the field keeps its neighbour's base wake and the drift rule stops on the early flat part.
    (b) *Subsonic sector only* (job `0-20261119-160834-8641`, remote, same process cold 17 200 it / 572.7 s): 0.1 cold 3370, 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 warm 910 / 810 / 1110 against cold 1370 / 1670 / 1970: **15 020 it, 501 s (-13 % it, -12 % wall)**; CD against cold -1.31 % at 0.5, -0.07 % at 0.8, -0.08 % at 0.9. The Mach 0.5 point from 0.1 (factor 5) keeps the 0.1 base drag (0.11037 against cold 0.1160): a stale wake the stop rule does not see. A drift tolerance factor 0.25 (instead of 0.5) does not repair it (-1.14 / -0.46 / -0.29 %) and costs 1 400 it; the velocity-only rescale is the same as the perturbation one (15 420 it, same -1.31 %); ramp restart against cap: 3 100 against 2 830 it on the subsonic chain, inside the +-50 % per-point scatter of the stop rule (the CFL ramp is 230 it to 100): no measurable effect, kept at the neighbour's CFL (the advisor's point; the 2D warm-start loss came from a different cause here, the stale wake).
    (c) *Resulting default* (`WarmTuning::of`): sector (finned) warm only for Mach <= 0.9, from the lowest upward, neighbour within a factor 2 (so 0.5 starts seeded and 0.8, 0.9 chain from it); half domain warm only from Mach 3.0 down to 1.1 (no seed exists for it, and its transonic/subsonic points settle slower from a neighbour); finless wedge never. The settings carry `BOWSHOCK_WARM_*` overrides for measurements. Small batch on the final settings (job `0-20261119-165552-16036`, 3090, sector 0.5 / 0.8 / 0.9 in one process): cold 1370 / 1670 / 1970 = **5 010 it, 82.6 s**; default 1370 (seeded) / 1310 / 1410 = **4 090 it (-18 %), 64.3 s (-22 %)**, CD +0.00 / +0.11 / +0.09 % against cold (inside the 0.5 % stop-rule noise); ramp restart 1370 / 1670 / 1470 = 4 510 it, 70.2 s (the neighbour's CFL is 9 % fewer it); velocity-only rescale 3 490 it but Mach 0.9 stops at 510 it **+1.20 %** (rejected: the perturbation rescale keeps the dynamic-pressure part of the field); subsonic *descending* from 0.9 (job `0-20261119-160834-8641`) 17 980 it against cold 17 200, no gain (rejected). Half domain, 2 degrees, Mach 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 2.3 / 3.0 (same job): cold 910 / 1010 / 910 / 910 / 810 = **4 550 it, 103.9 s**; default (3.0 cold, the others warm from the next higher) 410 / 510 / 510 / 910 / 810 = **3 150 it (-31 %), 75.1 s (-28 %)**, CN +0.02 / +0.03 / -0.05 / +0.18 / 0.00 %; ramp restart 3 690 it, 82.5 s; velocity-only 4 450 it, CN up to +0.27 %; no rescale at all (`RESCALE=none`, sector 0.5-0.9) 14 090 it and +4.0 % (the rescale is what makes a warm start work). **No full-table run on the final settings was made** (queue rules), so the table-level saving is an estimate: about 900 sector iterations of 17 500 (5 %) and about 1 400 half-domain iterations of 8 100 (17 %), 45-60 s of 440 s on the 3090 (10-14 %).
    *Recommendation:* do **not** make it the default. The gain is an estimated 10-14 % of a table, in two subsonic sector points and the supersonic half-domain chain, at the price of a stale-wake bias (up to -1.3 % CD at Mach 0.5 with the wide neighbour, 0.1 % inside a factor 2) and a modest table-level gain that the stop rule cannot see; the seeded cold start is already at the rule's iteration floor in the supersonic range. A rule that checks the base-wake drift directly (or the seed of the sector extended to the half domain) would be the next lever. Not done: live per-point events inside a chain (a chain's points reach the screen when its run ends).

60. **The `k` rate in the pseudo-time step: capping it at `L_i + L_j` is a 2x gain at supersonic Mach on the example rocket and not robust (wakedt, 2026-11-19; opt-in `ImplicitParams::dt_k_cap`, default off).** *Question:* `pseudo_dt3` sums `L_k` on every non-wedge domain although `k` is line-implicit; the fin stack's azimuthal clustering runs along every `k` line, so the wake cells near the axis are slivers `r dtheta_min` wide and `L_k` (`(|u.n| + c) S_k`, `S_k` independent of the azimuthal width, plus the diffusive rate) dwarfs the other two. Is that a large part of the slow base-wake settling?
    - *Diagnostic* (`finned_table --dt-diag`, new `GpuSolver3::dt_rates`: the `L_d` of the implicit step on the device, the step's own f32 numbers, low-Mach `u' + c'` and diffusion included; final state of a Draft sector run, 241 k cells; base wake = 2 d behind the base plane, `r < 0.5 d`). Histogram of `L_k / (L_i + L_j)` over the fluid cells at Mach 0.5: <0.25 27.7 %, <0.5 7.9 %, <1 8.5 %, <2 8.9 %, <4 8.6 %, <8 8.3 %, <16 7.6 %, <64 11.7 %, >=64 10.8 %; **`L_k` is the largest rate in 56 % of the cells, in 57-60 % of the wake (`r < 1 d`) and in 74-79 % of the base wake** (median ratio there 18 at Mach 0.5, 11 at 2.3, 21 for the Rabia sector at 0.8; p90 5 000, maximum 1e7). The sum's step is that much smaller than the two-direction step in the base wake: median `dt_cap1 / dt_sum` 9.7 (M 0.5), 5.9 (M 2.3), 10.9 (Rabia M 0.8); p90 2 500 / 1 500 / 2 500. The convective CFL `dt |u| / h` (largest direction, at `cfl_max` 100) in the base wake has a median of only 0.2 (M 0.5) / 1.3 (M 2.3) / 0.16 (Rabia) with the sum, 1.9 / 8.0 / 1.8 with the cap: the wake fluid is stepped at a small fraction of its own convective limit, which is the `N CFL U_inf / c'_w` = const of the item above made visible.
    - *The variant* (`ImplicitParams::dt_k_cap`, CPU `Solver3::compute_dt`, GPU `LazySolver3::pseudo_dt3`, `finned_table --dt-k-cap X`, engine experiment `BOWSHOCK_DT_CAPK=<cfl_max>,<cap>`): `dt = CFL V / (L_i + L_j + min(L_k, cap (L_i + L_j)))`; `inf` (default) is the sum, bit for bit. Not `DtMode::MinDirection`: that releases two directions at once and failed; this releases one. Tests: CPU `capped_k_time_step_is_bounded_by_the_sum`; GPU `euler3d::tiny_anf_dt_k_cap_state_after_30_iterations_matches_cpu` (tiny ANF with DDADI, M 0.5 and 2, caps 1 and 4, f32 and df32 against the CPU solver with the same parameters; the cap changes the CPU state by 3.5e-3 / 8.5e-4 at M 0.5 and 1.2e-5 at M 2 (the cap 4 does not act at M 2); GPU against CPU, job `0-20261119-174857-30329` on the 3090: f32 4.0-4.6e-6, df32 2.6-7.4e-8, targets 1e-3 / 1e-4).
    - *Draft example sector, supersonic, cap 1 at `cfl_max` 25* (`tools/wakedt.sh`, accepted iterations until the CA history stays within 0.5 % of its own converged value, no rollback): Mach 1.3 **550** against 1 250 (sum, `cfl_max` 100), Mach 2.3 **450** against 1 000, Mach 3.0 **400** against 900: **-55 %**; the drift-rule stop at Mach 2.3 1 910 against 2 910 iterations (-34 %), Mach 1.3 1 750 against 2 510 (the Mach 3.0 runs were cut at 3 000); CD 0.59287 / 0.47174 / 0.39599 against 0.59204 / 0.47159 / 0.39616 (**+0.14 / +0.03 / -0.04 %**). Step cost unchanged (3.7-4.6 against 3.7-4.1 ms). At `cfl_max` 100 the cap rolls back (4 rollbacks, `cfl_max` 24 after, net 1 200 against 1 000 iterations); `cfl_max` 50: 2 rollbacks; 12: 600 (no gain over 25). Caps 2 / 4 / 16 at `cfl_max` 100, Mach 2.3: 450 / 450 / 500 accepted iterations but 3 / 3 / 2 rollbacks (450 / 450 / 350 wasted, final `cfl_max` 34 / 34 / 49): net 900 / 900 / 850 against 1 000: the gain is gone.
    - *Not robust, so not kept:* (a) **Mach 0.5** (Draft): cap 1 at `cfl_max` 100 / 50 / 25 and caps 2 / 4 / 16 at 100 all roll back (2-5 times, 250-800 iterations wasted) and take 1 000-3 050 accepted iterations against 1 550 for the sum, (cap 1 at `cfl_max` 25: 1 000 + 350 wasted against 1 550, CD +0.2 %; at 100: 623 iterations, `ResidualDrop` at CD +1.6 %) and all but cap 1 at 25 settle at CD 0.3985-0.3999 against 0.3962-0.3969 (+0.5..+0.8 %, base +2-3 %): the base wake of this body at Mach 0.5 is at the edge of shedding (item 32), and the capped step lands on a different quasi-steady wake. (b) **Rabia short-fin-can sector** (`finned_table --rabia`, Draft, Mach 0.8, `--blunt-in` 0.031; the sum run reproduces the slow point: 8 rollbacks, `cfl_max` 100 to 5.8, 8 000 iterations without a stop, rollbacks also at 5 150 and 6 000, i.e. long after the start-up): the capped step is **non-finite** within 240 iterations at `cfl_max` 25 and within 700 at cap 4 / `cfl_max` 100; cap 16 survives, with the same 8 rollbacks and 3 000 iterations at CD 0.734 against 0.66 (not settled). Rabia at Mach 2.3: sum 6 rollbacks; cap 1 at 25 non-finite at 300 iterations. So the Rabia point's slowness is not the `k` rate: its late rollbacks (5 150, 6 000 at CFL 12 and 8) happen with the sum too. (c) The first half (300-450 iterations) rolls back at `cfl_max` 50-100 for every cap: the step in the sliver cells is 10-1e6 times larger and [INFERENCE] the `i`/`j` factors there then run at an effective CFL of 50-100 instead of about zero.
    - *Ramp (item 3 of the brief), not pursued:* the CFL ramp is `x 1.02` per iteration (CFL 25 at iteration 160, 100 at 230). A faster ramp (`--growth 1.05 / 1.1`, cap 1, `cfl_max` 25, Mach 2.3) rolls back at 250-350 (2 rollbacks each, 300-350 iterations wasted) where 1.02 needed none: the start-up is already at the stability edge, so the ramp is not where iterations are lost (450-1 000 iterations to 0.5 % of which the ramp is 160-230). SER (`CFL ~ R_0 / R`) would have to be added to the loop state of the traced step (no spare slot in `State3`); not implemented.
    - *Round 2: a start-up that survives* (`KCap { cap, from_iter, cfl_max }`: the plain sum at `cfl_max` 100 until `from_iter`, then the cap at `cfl_max` at most the cap's; `ImplicitParams::at_iter`, `finned_table --dt-k-cap X --dt-k-from N --dt-k-cfl C`, `BOWSHOCK_DT_CAPK=cap,from,cfl`; the GPU step is traced per phase (`phase_len3`, the compiled-loop key), the CFL state is cut to the phase's `cfl_max` at once as the CPU ramp is; GPU against CPU with a switch at iteration 12 and `cfl_max` 6: f32 3.6e-6, df32 1.8e-8 against an effect of 3.6e-2, job `0-20261119-175720-19640`). Measured on a tree with the flightval merge (`wakedt-rabia`: its grid changes move the Draft example CD by +2 %, so these baselines are not those above), cold start, accepted iterations until the CA history stays within 0.5 % of the plain run's end value:
      - Draft example sector, cap 1 from 300 at `cfl_max` 25: Mach 1.3 **550** against 1 250, 2.3 **500** against 1 000, 3.0 **450** against 900 (-56 / -50 / -50 %), no rollback, drift-rule stop 1 110 / 910 / 910 against 2 510 / 2 710 / 2 910, CD +0.18 / +0.09 / -0.04 %. From 600 at 25: 700 / 700 / 700 (-44 / -30 / -22 %), two rollbacks at Mach 3.0. At `cfl_max` 40 every start has rollbacks (0-5): the cap needs `cfl_max` <= 25.
      - **Coarse example sector, Mach 2.3, cold: 6 rollbacks for every start and CFL** (from 300 or 600, 25 or 40; failures at iterations 650-700 and 1 431 / 1 800 whatever the controller's cap, which stays above the phase's 25 for four rollbacks: 1 843-2 462 iterations wasted, the run ends at `cfl_max` 11.8): 1 250-1 350 accepted iterations against 1 600 (-16..-22 %), wall 95-103 s against 64 s; CD 0.50222 against 0.50234. The Draft grid does not predict the Coarse one.
      - **Facade path** (`diag56_point`, seeded sector, the product's): the example rocket on Coarse at Mach 2.3 takes **770 iterations** with the plain sum (the axisymmetric seed has removed the wake settling the cap was after) and 570-670 with the cap (-13..-26 %, 15.8 s against 13.7-14.8 s), CD 0.55222 against 0.55286-0.55304 (+0.12..+0.15 %), no rollback.
      - **Rabia short-fin-can fixture** (the real one from `flightval`, Draft): Mach 2.3 `run` returns an error for the sum and for every variant (message not logged); Mach 1.3 the sum already `Failed` (8 rollbacks, 3 000 iterations, CD 0.733 not settled), the cap from 300 non-finite at 1 200, from 600 at CD 0.510 not settled, the same 8 rollbacks. The harness cannot show a gain on this fixture.
    - *Keep rule (supersonic: >= 30 % fewer iterations, no rollback or non-finite on any case, CD within 0.2 %) not met:* the Coarse sector rolls back six times cold and gains 13-26 % through the seeded facade; Rabia fails with the plain sum already. **Not made the default for Mach >= 1.2; no Coarse full solve run.** `dt_k_cap` stays opt-in (default `None`, bit-identical). What remains is the Draft tier alone (cold or seeded, -50 %, `cfl_max` 25 from iteration 300), which the product does not run; if the Coarse failures at 650-700 iterations were understood (a Coarse-specific transient the capped step does not survive) the gain would only be the seeded 13-26 % at Mach 2.3 on top of 770 iterations, i.e. a few seconds a point: the iterations of the full solve are in the subsonic and angle points, which this does not touch (Mach 0.5 gets nothing from any cap).

62. **Subsonic drag of the finless Arcas long model (+16 % at Mach 0.6 / 0.8): the friction is right, the excess is the boattail pressure drag of the base-wake solve (subdrag, 2026-11-19).** *Code:* `tests/subdrag.rs` (`sd1_arcas_long_finless`: the gate's wedge case with the parts, the local `Cf` / `Cp` along the body and the variants of `SD_VARIANTS`: `trip`, `sting`, `lip`, `refNN`, `xtrN`, `short`, `euler`, tokens joined by `+`; `sd2_fox_boattail`: the boattail drag of NASA TM X-2439 on the Euler wedge; `variants_build`), `tools/subdrag_estimate.py` (flat-plate friction by Eckert reference temperature, Niskanen / Hoerner base drag, trip `Re_k`), `tools/subdrag_post.py` (the Fox integral, local `Cf` against the estimate). Jobs (all local 3090, `BOWSHOCK_WEDGE_BACKEND=gpu BOWSHOCK_FIN_TIER=coarse`, Coarse wedge, f32, `Settled` at 1110-1710 it, y+ 0.34-0.58 on the body, 12-31 s each): `0-20261119-173210-1127` (262aab1: base, trip, trip+sting, ref15+trip), `0-20261119-173937-2030` (ac8c174: euler, short, short+sting, xtr4 / 8 / 12), `0-20261119-174231-19282` (53a2f83: lip), `0-20261119-175420-26773` (dcb80e5: xtr6 / 7, sting+xtr, short+xtr7); the Fox run is the CPU f64 `sd2_fox_boattail` (1233 s, Euler wedge, levels 0 / 1).

    (a) *Like for like.* `C_A,corr` of NASA TN D-4013 Fig. 12 (p. 61) is the axial force "corrected to free-stream conditions at the model base" (symbols p. 2, p. 4: "not corrected ... except the summary data at roll 0 and alpha 0"): the pressure force on the base annulus (1.47 in base less the 1 in sting, 0.229 S) is taken out, `C_A,b` -0.01 .. -0.03 here (base `Cp_b` +0.055 / +0.08 at Mach 0.6 / 0.8, Fig. 5 p. 16). The gate's `total - base - friction.base` is the same quantity (the base force of the whole disc out); the reference area is `S = pi d^2 / 4`, `d` 2.25 in (`area_factor` 1.0000), Mach, `Re/m` 9.8e6 and `T0` 322 K are the test's. The differences: **(1) the test has a 1 in sting 11.5 in behind the base and then a 19.2 degree flare (p. 4), the gate none; (2) the base is drawn with a 15 degree tail cone to 1.308 in diameter and a reflexed lip back out to 1.470 in over 0.053 in (D-4014 Fig. 1(a) detail A), the gate has a straight 12.2 degree cone to 1.470 in; (3) the nose is the table of Fig. 1(a) (up to 9 % slimmer than the tangent ogive at x = 2.4 in, full radius from 9.375 in), the gate a tangent ogive of 10.6 in; (4) transition: a 0.10 in band of No. 120 carborundum 1.25 in behind the nose tip (p. 3), the gate fully turbulent.** The grit (0.0049 in, Braslow-Knox) on the Blasius layer there has `Re_k` ~ 750 at both Mach numbers (`Re/m` and `T0` are constant, `Re_k` does not depend on Mach), above the 600 an effective trip needs: the layer is turbulent from the band, to the accuracy of that criterion. `with_transition(1.25 / 2.25)` changes the answer by -0.1 %.

    (b) *The parts* (base run, Mach 0.6 / 0.8; `C_A` on `S`, base excluded): `v` **0.2918 / 0.2930 against 0.2500 / 0.2525 (+16.7 / +16.0 %)**; pressure of the nose +0.0012 / +0.0014, of the cylinder 0 (no axial projection), **of the boattail +0.0510 / +0.0589** (`Cp` -0.34 at the shoulder rising to +0.035 at the base, as the Fox data below); friction nose 0.0455 / 0.0444, cylinder 0.1905 / 0.1856, boattail 0.0036 / 0.0027, **total 0.2396 / 0.2327**; base pressure -0.0270 / -0.0306 (thrust: `Cp_b` +0.063 / +0.072 against +0.055 / +0.08 measured, the base state is the test's). Friction against a flat plate (Eckert reference temperature on White's `Cf = 0.455 / ln^2(0.06 Re_x)`, adiabatic wall, integrated over the wetted area of the body, `Re_L` 1.33e7, virtual origin at the band): **0.244 / 0.239, the solve -1.8 / -2.6 %; the local `Cf` of the solve is 0.96-1.07 of the plate's at every station** (x/d 2 to 21). Base drag of a blunt base in free flight (Niskanen / Fleeman, `bowshock_reference::base_drag`, 0.12 + 0.13 M^2 on the base area 0.427 S) is +0.071 / +0.087 S, the solve's +(-0.027) / (-0.031): the correlation is for a flat-based body, and Hoerner's boattailed-base formula `0.029 (d_b/d)^3 / sqrt(C_Df)` gives +0.016 / +0.017 for boattail plus base together, the solve +0.024 / +0.029 (boattail + base, free wake). **So the gate's 0.04 is not in the friction or the base: it is `0.052` of boattail pressure drag against about `0.250 - 0.244 = 0.006` the data leave for nose plus boattail pressure and the trip.**

    (c) *Variants* (`v` / data - 1, Mach 0.6 / 0.8):

    | variant | `v` | error | note |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | base (gate) | 0.2918 / 0.2930 | +16.7 / +16.0 % | |
    | trip (laminar to 1.25 in) | 0.2915 / 0.2927 | +16.6 / +15.9 % | |
    | ref 1.5, trip (270 x 186 cells, y+ 0.34 / 0.43) | 0.2921 / 0.2934 | +16.8 / +16.2 % | grid: nothing |
    | Euler (no friction) | boattail 0.0502 / 0.0760 | | the boattail drag is the inviscid wake flow, not the layer |
    | sting (0.222 d radius, to the outflow), trip | 0.2792 / 0.2784 | +11.7 / +10.3 % | boattail 0.039 / 0.045; `Cp_b` +0.101 / +0.109 (data +0.055 / +0.08); the sting wall is not resolved (y+ 155-164) |
    | lip (detail A) | 0.2934 / 0.3078 | +17.3 / +21.9 % | with the sting +14.4 / +18.0 %: worse |
    | laminar to 4 / 6 / 7 / 8 / 12 calibres | | +9.3 / +3.5 / **-0.5** / -2.6 / -15.1 % (Mach 0.8: +9.1 / +3.6 / -0.2 / -2.2 / -13.9) | needs a layer laminar for 0.4 m (`Re_x` 4e6) behind a trip of `Re_k` 750 |
    | sting + laminar to 3 / 5 / 7 | | +7.6 / +1.2 / -5.6 % (0.8: +6.5 / +0.3 / -6.0) | |
    | short model (data `arcas::TRANSONIC` 0.222 / 0.233) | 0.2397 / 0.2432 | +8.0 / +4.4 % | with the sting +2.3 / -1.9 %; laminar to 7: -12.0 / -13.5 % |

    The long minus short increment (12.55 in of cylinder) is 0.052 / 0.050 in the solve (0.054 / 0.053 for the plate, whatever the transition point: the extra tube is turbulent) against 0.028 / 0.0195 in the data, which put the fin anchors "not flush" on the short model (p. 5): the difference, 0.024 / 0.030, is what the anchors would have to cost (about three times a stagnation estimate of their frontal area, 0.008 S): the short-model data carry no independent information on friction against boattail pressure, only the long (flush) model does, and one data point cannot separate a 0.04 friction excess from a 0.04 boattail excess. Evidence for the second: (i) the friction is within 2 % of a flat-plate estimate, as a total and station by station, with a band that should be effective; (ii) the laminar run that closes the gap needs a layer laminar to 7 calibres against that band; (iii) the boattail drag does not change with the grid, the layer (Euler 0.050 against 0.051) or the trip; (iv) the Fox check below.

    (d) *Boattail drag against measured pressures* (NASA TM X-2439, configuration 4: tangent ogive 2 d, cylinder 2 d, **10 degree boattail of 1 d to a 0.647 d base**, sting 0.13 d, Re_D 0.5e6; tabulated wall `Cp` integrated over the projected annulus, `tools/subdrag_post.py fox`): boattail drag +0.0295 / +0.0283 / +0.0307 S and base annulus `Cp` 0.069 / 0.084 / 0.132 at Mach 0.404 / 0.608 / 0.831 (boattail plus base -0.001 at 0.608: d'Alembert). The Euler wedge at level 1 (CPU f64, the same `Case3::wedge` and sting) gives **+0.0325 / +0.0388 / +0.0730** (+10 / +37 / +138 %) with `Cp_b` 0.056 / 0.064 / 0.086, so boattail plus base is +0.013 / +0.016 / +0.043: the solver recovers the boattail pressure too weakly (`Cp` -0.0017 against +0.034 at 64 % of the cone, -0.063 against -0.030 at 35 %; the shoulder peak is -0.73 against the orifices' -0.39, no orifice sits at the peak). The Fox layer is laminar or transitional and the run is Euler, so this is the inviscid part of the error, not a proof for the Arcas.

    (e) *Verdict.* **Model error in the afterbody pressure drag (boattail), +0.03 to +0.045 S at Mach 0.6 / 0.8 on this 12-15 degree, 0.43 base-area-ratio tail, `[INFERENCE]`: not isolated to a mechanism** (the corner expansion and recovery of the second-order scheme on the cone against the free-wake or sting closure behind it are both candidates; refinement by 1.5 moves it by 0.003 and the Euler run has the same drag), **plus a comparison difference of 0.012 S (5 points) that is the test's sting** (item 64 refines this: the boattail excess against the measured pressures is mostly offset by the nose, and the Euler wake solve is not grid-converged at level 0 or 1), which the gate omits; the friction, the grid, the transition band, the reference area and the base subtraction are not the cause. The gate stays `FAIL KNOWN` with this attribution (`rocket_gates.rs`, the INFERENCE of item 55 about the friction is withdrawn); with the sting the two lines are +11.7 / +10.3 %, inside the 12 % band, but the sting wall is not wall-resolved (y+ 155-168) and its base `Cp` overshoots the data by 0.045 / 0.03, so the sting variant is not adopted as the gate.

    (f) *What it means for user rockets* (free flight, base included, fully turbulent default): **friction: no error** (flat plate -2 %, local `Cf` within -4 / +7 %); **nose and cylinder pressure: 0** at these Mach numbers; **boattail plus base (the aft end): +0.024 / +0.029 S in the Arcas solve against +0.016 / +0.017 from Hoerner's base-drag formula for a tapered afterbody**, i.e. the excess over the empirical estimate is **about +0.01 S at Mach 0.6-0.8**, 3-4 % of this body's `C_A` (0.29), and the Fox Euler comparison puts it at +0.013 / +0.016 / +0.043 S at Mach 0.4 / 0.6 / 0.83 for a 10 degree boattail (the data's own net is zero, the solver's is not): a rocket with a boattail and a base is over-predicted by up to 0.01-0.02 S at Mach 0.6 and more toward Mach 0.9 (`[INFERENCE]`; transonic boattails are not tested here), while a flat-based rocket (the Finner, +4.6..+6.4 % CD0) is not affected. The gate's +16 % is the base-excluded number, which puts the whole boattail pressure drag (0.052 S) against the 0.006 S the data leave; the free-flight total of the same solve (base thrust in) is 0.265 S.

    (g) *Found on the way.* The GPU Euler wedge with a sting (`euler+sting` on the Arcas, every Fox case) goes non-finite after 84-125 iterations at any level (CPU f64 does not; the viscous GPU wedge with a sting runs): not investigated. Left open: a viscous Fox run (turbulent at its Re_D 0.5e6 it is not the test's layer), a boattail geometry study with a resolved sting and the flare, and the lift tier's boattail.

64. **Boattail pressure drag of the Euler / RANS wake solve (boattail, 2026-11-19): no defect of the scheme; the excess of item 62 is the resolution of the inviscid base wake and a nose / boattail partition of the comparison; the GPU Euler + sting divergence is localised, not fixed.** *Code:* `tests/boattail.rs` (`bt1` Fox TM X-2439 configuration 4 on the Euler wedge with variant tokens, wall `Cp` against the data, entropy / total-pressure rows above the wall, the segment drag from the extrapolated and the first-cell wall pressure; `bt2` GPU against CPU iteration by iteration; `bt3` the legacy 2D engine on the same body; `bt4` one-step amplification of a perturbation), `tools/boattail_job.sh`. CPU f64 runs, Fox ogive, 10 degree boattail, sting 0.13 d, Mach 0.404 / 0.608 / 0.831. No scheme or grid code changed, so the Finner / ANF gates (flat base) are unchanged by construction and were not re-run.

    (a) *The attached flow conserves d'Alembert.* `closed` (the boattail continues as a sting of the base radius, no base, no wake): nose + boattail pressure drag **-0.0016 / -0.0002 / [+0.013 transonic, shock] S** at level 0 (nose -0.0103 / -0.0098, boattail +0.0087 / +0.0096), **-0.0009 at level 1 and -0.0011 at level 2** (Mach 0.608). The low-Mach flux correction off, the sensor off, the minmod and Venkatakrishnan limiters move the sum by at most 0.0014; the low-Mach preconditioning of the left-hand side, the entropy fix `eps_fix` (0.02, 0.3) and `cfl_max` 10 leave all four digits unchanged (they only act on the implicit left-hand side). Only the first-order scheme does not conserve it (net +0.028). A rounded shoulder (four cones, 2.5 to 7.5 degrees over 0.2 d) lowers the boattail drag by 36 % (0.0096 to 0.0061). The total-pressure loss along the wall is confined to the first cell row (0.36 % on the cylinder, 1.9 % on the boattail, entropy +0.06 % / +0.4 %); four rows above it is below 0.13 %: a numerical wall layer, not a source of drag.

    (b) *The wake solve (Fox, Euler, wall integral, S):*

    | Mach | level | nose | boattail | base | `Cp_b` | sum | data boattail / `Cp_b` / sum |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 0.404 | 0 / 1 | -0.0121 / -0.0099 | 0.0377 / 0.0325 | -0.0066 / -0.0198 | 0.019 / 0.056 | +0.019 / +0.003 | 0.0295 / 0.069 / +0.003 |
    | 0.608 | 0 / 1 / 2 | -0.0116 / -0.0097 / -0.0085 | 0.0431 / 0.0388 / **0.0303** | -0.0103 / -0.0225 / -0.0237 | 0.029 / 0.064 / 0.068 | +0.021 / +0.007 / **-0.002** | 0.0283 / 0.084 / +0.001 |
    | 0.831 | 0 / 1 | -0.0107 / -0.0090 | 0.066 / 0.073 | -0.0231 / -0.0302 | 0.066 / 0.086 | +0.032 / +0.034 | 0.0307 / 0.132 / +0.020 |

    (sum = nose + boattail + base; the data's sum by the trapezoid over the orifices.) The legacy 2D engine (`Engine::Legacy`, `fox_case`, `run_cold`) gives the same four digits as the wedge at level 0 (0.0377 / 0.0431 / 0.066, `Cp_b` 0.0187 / 0.0294 / 0.0657): it is the same scheme, a check of the wedge and not an independent solution. Level 0 to 2 at Mach 0.608: **the boattail drag falls 0.0431, 0.0388, 0.0303 (data 0.0283) and the sum nose + boattail + base to d'Alembert (data +0.001)**; the Euler solution of the wake is not grid-converged at level 0 or 1, and the product's Coarse tier is level 0.

    (c) *What moves the boattail drag (level 0, Mach 0.608, 0.0431):* the end-cell size at the shoulder and the boattail end `dx_tip` / 2 -0.0048 (0.0383; at level 1 -0.0083, 0.0305), the boattail cell count x2 -0.0025 (level 1 -0.0048), the first cell height /2 +0.0025, the gap rows x2 +0.0024, the cells behind the base and above the body ~0; rounded shoulder -0.0031; sensor off +0.0037 (level 1 -0.0009); the sting is irrelevant (no sting 0.0429, sting 0.06 d 0.0434, 0.13 d 0.0431; 0.3 d 0.0215); `nolm` -0.0071 (unsteady wake, 4000 iterations, not converged); first order +0.0088. The base pressure moves much more and independently (`Cp_b` -0.009 to +0.122 across these variants, the boattail drag not with it): the shock sensor (Rusanov blend at a relative pressure jump of 0.2-2 %, active in the subsonic shear layer) sets `Cp_b` 0.119 / 0.122 / 0.135 sensor off against 0.019 / 0.029 / 0.066 on (data 0.069 / 0.084 / 0.132), i.e. the sum -0.010 / -0.008 / +0.022 against +0.019 / +0.021 / +0.032 (data +0.003 / +0.001 / +0.020): the data lie between, neither setting is right, refinement converges to them. The hypothesis of item 62 (e) of an over-strong base suction pulling the boattail down is not supported: the boattail drag does not follow the base pressure. Refining only the boattail ends moves drag from the boattail into the base (level 0, `dx_tip` / 4: boattail 0.0364, `Cp_b` -0.033, sum +0.036), so it is not adopted.

    (d) *Comparison with the data.* With the same orifice trapezoid on the data and on the solver's `Cp` at the orifices, the nose of the Euler solution is 0.012-0.017 S more thrust than measured and the boattail 0.012-0.023 S more drag (Mach 0.404 / 0.608, level 1); nose + boattail differ by -0.0002 / +0.0056 S. The excess of item 62 (d) was the boattail alone against measured pressures; the nose (the measured aft-nose pressures are higher than the inviscid ones, a viscous effect) compensates most of it. The suction peak behind the shoulder (Euler -0.58 at x = 4.014 against -0.39 measured, -0.41 against -0.25 at 4.057) holds about 0.012 of the boattail drag; the orifice trapezoid itself overestimates the solver's own boattail integral by 0.010-0.016, so the data's boattail drag is uncertain by the same order. At Mach 0.831 the data carry a shock spike and a nose drag +0.036 the Euler solution does not have (-0.009): transonic, not a boattail error. **For the gate x4** (item 62): the Fox Euler nose + boattail pressure drag (0.027-0.036 S at Mach 0.4-0.6) matches the measured 0.027-0.031 within the scatter, while the Arcas solve has 0.052; a 12 degree boattail of 0.8 d is steeper than the 10 degree one of 1 d, but this makes the pressure part an unlikely owner of the whole +0.04 S, and the friction (laminar run, transition) is again open: `[INFERENCE]`, the data cannot separate them. The `FAIL KNOWN` attribution of `rocket_gates.rs` to the boattail pressure alone is therefore not established. Free-flight totals with a base: the Euler wake solve overpredicts the afterbody + base pressure drag by +0.02 S at level 0 and Mach 0.6 and +0.007 at level 1 against d'Alembert.

    (e) *GPU Euler wedge with a sting non-finite (diagnosis).* GPU f32 against CPU f64 on the Fox case (`bt2`, `BT_STEPS`): equal to 1e-6 at iteration 10 and for the same case without the sting (`nosting`) over 40 iterations; with the sting they diverge from iteration ~8 in the y-momentum of the **first fluid row above the sting** (`j = n_sting`, columns behind the base) and, earlier and slower, at the base corner, growing by x3.9 per iteration at CFL ~1.2 until the state is non-finite at iteration 84-125 (non-finite density from iteration ~100, level 0 and level 1, CPU stays finite). Not the line solves (`lines: false` the same, though the CPU does not converge without them), not the implicit boundary Jacobians (`implicit_bc: false`), not `sub_iters`, `cfl_max` 10, the limiter, the low-Mach correction, nor the arithmetic (`Precision::Extended` fails the same way, 10 iterations later: the seed is round-off and the growth is intrinsic to the GPU left-hand side); a CFL of 0.05 is stable, so it is the implicit operator, not the explicit residual. The growth is stronger for a thinner sting (0.06 d) and absent for a 0.3 d one: it depends on the radius of the wall row, i.e. on the wedge's radial-momentum source (`p / r`, point-implicit in the momentum rows, explicit in the energy row) next to a solid wall with cells of aspect ratio ~100. On the CPU the same one-step test (`bt4`, `BT_CPU_ONLY`) shows the coupling of a y-momentum perturbation into the other rows at the sting row (0.003 at CFL 1, 0.36 at CFL 3, 26 at CFL 10, 123 at CFL 30; 4.5 times less above the cylinder at r 0.5): the CPU left-hand side is only marginally stable there, and the GPU's is not at CFL ~1. The GPU one-step comparison jobs (`bt4`, ids 0-20261119-201325-22381 and the convergence job 0-20261119-204639-30826) were still queued behind other jobs when this was written, so the amplification on the GPU is not measured and the cause is not isolated or fixed. Next: read the `bt4` GPU rows; candidates are `factor3` in `gpu/implicit3.rs` (source block `src` against the wall fold at the first fluid row) and the energy-row source.

    (f) *Left open.* The GPU grid convergence of the Fox boattail (nosting level 0-3, `dx` variants) needs the queued jobs; a viscous Fox run; the Arcas gate at level 1-2.

### The viscous half domain at incidence: the CFL limit and the first four incidence points (phys3d, 2026-11-18)

**Correction to item 31:** the wedge takes `sub_iters` 1 only **below Mach 1** (`Numerics3::from_2d`): at level 1 `solver3d`'s G-V4 / G-J re-runs went non-finite with 1 at Arcas fins-off Mach 2.3 (iteration 250) and TP-2005 Mach 1.65, where 3 passes; the level-0 supersonic points of item 31 did not show it.

**The failure.** The Lift-tier half domain with fins (ANF nose on a tube of 1.5 d, ANF fins, `Case3::base_half`, grid 148 x 57 x 82 = 692k cells, 16 chord / 8 span cells, `Re_c` 7.0e5, sized for Mach 3.147) with the engine's viscous numerics (`cfl_max` 50 below Mach 1, 20 above, 200 first-order iterations, `sub_iters` 3) is non-finite at iteration 176 to 192 (CPU f64) and 158 to 198 (GPU f32) at Mach 0.8 and 2, 2 and 6 degrees, when the CFL ramp (1.02 per iteration) passes about 15 (Mach 2) to 40 (Mach 0.8). `Case3::with_tier` now takes `cfl_max` 10 for a viscous `Domain3::Half` (`HALF_DOMAIN_CFL_MAX`); all four cases run at it.

**Where it starts** (`TRACK_FROM`: the cells of the largest relative density / pressure change per iteration, CPU): at iteration 150 the first cells that change by more than 40 % in an iteration are `i` 108 to 110 (`x` 4.35 to 4.42, just behind the base plane at 4.34), `j` 28 to 30 (`r` 0.500, the body radius), at the fin azimuths (theta 0.75 to 0.81 and 2.39 to 2.41), volumes 1e-8 to 2e-8, `rho` 0.11, `p` 0.05 (the expansion behind the blunt fin trailing edge); five iterations later 1e6, then everything. From iteration 115 the largest changes (10 to 16 % per iteration, not growing) are along the fin root at the body wall, `x` 3.2 to 3.4 (the fin leading edge junction).

**What it needs** (Mach 2, 2 degrees, `cfl_max` 20 unless noted; CPU f64 unless GPU): baseline non-finite 182; **zero incidence** (same fins) non-finite 180; **no fins** stable to 260 (residual 0.068); **Coarse tier** (fin stack at `y+` 25) stable to 260; **Euler equations** on the same grid stable to 200 (residual 0.17); **laminar viscous** (no SST) non-finite at about 150; `sub_iters` 1 non-finite 200, 5 non-finite 160 (more defect passes do not help); `cfl_max` 14 stable to 200; **second order from iteration 0** (`first_order_iters` 0) stable to 260 on the CPU and to **3000 iterations on the GPU** (CN 0.38755, CA 0.70331, `x_cp` 3.3999), but with `cfl_max` 30 non-finite at 170 (GPU). Mach 0.8, 2 degrees: `cfl_max` 50 non-finite 176 to 192 (CPU and GPU), 20 with 200 first-order iterations non-finite 198 (GPU), 20 and 30 with `first_order_iters` 0 stable to 3000 (GPU: CN 0.70850 / 0.70749, CA 0.67570 / 0.68028, CN half range over the last 1000 iterations 0.05 % / 0.17 %), 50 with 0 non-finite 192 (GPU). A cap on the relative change of a cell per iteration (0.2, 0.05) keeps the run finite but wrong (CN -0.16 and -1.3, residual 2e2 to 2e3): not a fix. So it needs the fins and the viscous terms (the turbulence model not), not the incidence, not the Lift tier alone (the Coarse tier is stable at the same CFL), and the stable CFL is a limit that depends on Mach and on the first-order phase (Mach 2: 14 to 20 with it, 20 to 30 without; Mach 0.8: 10 to 20 with it, 30 to 50 without). **The cause is not found.** Suspects left: the diagonal viscous term of the left-hand side and the viscous flux at the corner cells where the fin trailing edge meets the base (two no-slip walls, a cell of volume 1e-8), and the three-factor error there; the `k` line (azimuthal cell count) was not varied.

**Update: the cause is the mesh behind the flush trailing edge** (`TE_TAPER_COLUMNS` in `engine/grid3.rs`; `tests/viscous3_fins.rs::corner_geometry` prints the cells). The layer cell behind the trailing edge of a flush fin is as wide as the fin (0.074 d) between the first cells of the fin stack (7e-4 d at `y+` 60) and narrowed to the stack width over 4 columns; its side faces tilt by up to 23 degrees (the cosine between a face normal and the line to the lower neighbour: 0.918 in `k`, 0.934 in `i`) and its volume falls by up to a factor 10 per column; the cells to go first are right behind it at the body radius (`i` 108 to 110, `j` 28 to 30). Taper over 2 columns (cosines 0.83 / 0.85): non-finite at ~135; **4** (the old value): 150 to 182; **5, 6, 8, 16** (0.946 / 0.957, 0.959 / 0.972, 0.968 / 0.986, 0.969 / 0.998): stable to 260 at Mach 2, 2 degrees, `cfl_max` 20 (CN 0.3873 to 0.3875, CA 0.703 to 0.708, `x_cp` 3.400, residual 0.18 to 0.25); 8 and 16 also at `cfl_max` 30 (Mach 2) and 8 at Mach 0.8 `cfl_max` 20 and 30 (240 iterations; with 4 columns Mach 0.8 and `cfl_max` 20 failed at 198 on the GPU); Mach 2, 6 degrees and Mach 0.8, 6 degrees at `cfl_max` 20 hold to 200 and 180 (CPU). The mesh now tapers over 8. **Why the Coarse tier (same taper geometry, cosines down to 0.86) was stable:** its first cells are 60 times thinner in `j` (1.2e-5), the pseudo time step of the cell is 60 times shorter, and the Courant numbers of the cell at `cfl_max` 20 are (hand estimate from the dump, `i` / tilted `k`) 0.42 / 13 on the Lift grid and 0.016 / 1.4 on the Coarse grid: the factorisation error that the tilt couples into, a product of the two, is 250 times smaller. Rejected: the diagonal viscous term of the left-hand side and of the time step (scaled by 3 and 10: non-finite at ~180 all), the turbulence model, the incidence, the Lift tier alone, `sub_iters`, the first-order switch, the wall state class (the first cells have no wall neighbour). A second limit remains at Mach 0.8: `cfl_max` 50 is non-finite at 200 to 228 with 8 and with 16 columns (blow-up in the wake behind the fin azimuths, `x` 5.2 to 5.7, after the taper ends); 20 and 30 hold, so `HALF_DOMAIN_CFL_MAX` can be raised to 20 once the GPU runs at 20 on the new mesh are in.

**First four incidence points** (GPU f32, `cfl_max` 10, 200 first-order iterations, `sub_iters` 3, the grid above; `examples/incidence_rans.rs`, 11.7 to 15 ms per iteration; stop rule as `Numerics3::viscous`: CN stationary 5e-4 over 1000, or the mean rule): Mach 2, 2 degrees CN 0.38737, CA 0.70236, `x_cp` 3.3996 cal, `CdStationary` at 2300 iterations (CN half range over the last 1000: 0.02 %); Mach 2, 6 degrees CN 1.15850, CA 0.70605, `x_cp` 3.3921, 1200 iterations (0.02 %); Mach 0.8, 2 degrees CN 0.70804, CA 0.67429, `x_cp` 3.4449, **`MeanStationary`** at 5991 iterations, oscillation of the last window 0.16 % (the largest of CA, CN and the moment); Mach 0.8, 6 degrees CN 2.12579, CA 0.62625, `x_cp` 3.4465, 3000 iterations (0.02 %). The short body steadies: no unsteady wake in these four. The CPU f64 CN at 250 iterations (0.386 / 1.158 / 0.709 / 2.12, the CA of Mach 0.8 not yet settled) and the `cfl_max` 20 / `first_order_iters` 0 runs agree (0.05 % in CN at Mach 2, 0.2 % in CA at Mach 0.8).

**Sector with `sub_iters` 2** (short body, 4 fins, 324k cells, Mach 0.5, `cfl_max` 50, CPU): stable to 800 iterations (residual 80 at 300, 68 at 800; with 1 non-finite at 370; with 3 stable to 500): nose 0.1175, fin friction 0.01448, body friction 0.4384, base 0.2124, still falling from 0.2416 at 300. 0.84 of the time per iteration of 3 (1427 against 1692 ms under the same load). Not judged to convergence: the sector residual does not fall and the base is still drifting at 800 iterations.

**G-L1 and G-F5 on the viscous Lift tier** (`tests/fin_lift_gates.rs`, `BOWSHOCK_WEDGE_BACKEND=gpu BOWSHOCK_FIN_TIER=lift`, local RTX 3090, f32, ANF body and fins, both roll phases, mesh with `TE_TAPER_COLUMNS` 8, `cfl_max` 10; the first gate lines the viscous engine has produced). **G-L1: all 32 lines PASS** (`CN_alpha` 13 %, `x_cp` 0.25 cal; 1400 to 4900 iterations, `CdStationary`). `CN_alpha` measured / data (between roll phase; inplane within 1.2 %): wind tunnel 0.69 16.53 / 15.70 (+5.3 %), 0.79 17.50 / 16.27 (+7.6 %), 1.728 12.92 / 11.92 (+8.4 %), 1.99 11.76 / 10.60 (+10.9 %), 2.48 10.16 / 9.34 (+8.8 %); free flight 1.8 12.59 / 11.29 (+11.5 %), 2.351 10.50 / 10.37 (+1.2 %), 2.665 9.76 / 8.88 (+9.9 %): high by 1 to 12 % (mean +8 %), inside the band. `x_cp` (cal) measured / data: 8.245 / 8.208, 8.311 / 8.243, 7.922 / 7.938, 7.709 / 7.678, 7.350 / 7.267; free flight 7.863 / 8.063 (-0.200: the closest to the band), 7.436 / 7.395, 7.240 / 7.294; the roll phase moves `x_cp` by at most 0.01 cal. 6 degrees (INFO): `CN_alpha(6)` is +1.4 to +6.5 % of `CN_alpha(2)`. **G-F5: all 12 lines FAIL, high** (band 8 %): `C_D0` (friction included, `pi d^2 / 4`) between | inplane against the table: Mach 0.5 0.506 | 0.505 vs 0.460 (+10 %), 0.7 0.522 | 0.518 vs 0.460 (+13 %), 1.832 0.745 | 0.746 vs 0.585 (+27 %), 2.375 0.590 vs 0.484 (+22 %), 2.718 0.524 vs 0.435 (+21 %), 3.147 0.467 vs 0.373 (+25 %); the parts (nose, base, fins with their friction): 0.5 0.037, 0.140, 0.241; 1.832 0.129, 0.185, 0.376; 3.147 0.104, 0.098, 0.228; what is left of the total (the aft body: 0.089 at Mach 0.5, 0.055 at 1.832, 0.038 at 3.147) is not separated into pressure and friction here; the roll phase moves `C_D0` by 0.4 % at most. `solver3d`'s Euler sector (pressure only) gives 0.614 / 0.421 / 0.350 / 0.298 at 1.832 / 2.375 / 2.718 / 3.147: 5 % above the table at 1.832 and 20 % below it at 3.147, so a friction offset alone does not reconcile the table with both tiers; whether the table or the solver is off at the high-Mach end is open (friction of the Lift tier: not checked against a flat-plate value on the body here).

**The example rocket's finned sector went non-finite at the ramp's top: the three-factor step has a CFL limit on every 3D grid; the rule is `cfl_max` 10** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; `Case3::sector` of the example body, ogive 3 d + tube 12 d, four swept square-section fins of root chord 1.575 d, tip 0.656 d, span 1 d, sweep 0.787 d, thickness 0.0417 d, `Re_c` 1.7e6, grid sized for Mach 3; Draft 125 x 76 x 21 = 199 500 cells; `viscous3_fins` `CASE=rocket`, `incidence_rans --domain sector --sector-fins example --body example --tier draft`). *The failure* (`gpu3d`, GPU f32, default viscous numerics: 3 solves, 200 first-order iterations, ramp x1.02 per iteration to 50 below Mach 1 and 20 above): non-finite at iteration 190 to 215 at Mach 0.1, 0.5, 0.8, 0.9 on the Coarse and the Draft sector, at Mach 3 after 411 (Draft) and 932 (Coarse) and at Coarse Mach 2.3 after 4508; Mach 1.0 to 1.7 converge. The CPU f64 solver reproduces it (Draft, Mach 0.5, non-finite at 200). *Not the mesh behind the trailing edge* (the tree has `TE_TAPER_COLUMNS` 8, the cells at the trailing edge have cosines of 0.93 and better) *and not the fins*: with one change at a time (CPU f64, Draft, Mach 0.5) the double-wedge section (196), no sweep (218), a thin fin of 0.01 d (200), the Euler equations (198), laminar flow (200) and 400 first-order iterations (200) are all non-finite where the default is; the blow-up starts in the nose, the wake and the far field at once, at regular cells. *It is the CFL level*: Mach 0.5 holds at `cfl_max` 10, 15, 20 and 30 (to 340 to 420 iterations; 800 at 30 with a ramp of x1.01) and is non-finite at 200 at 40 and 50 (the ramp passes 40 at iteration 186); Mach 0.9 holds at 20 and not at 50; the finless example body on a uniform-azimuth half domain (96 x 76 x 24, no fin stack) is non-finite at 50 (196) and converges at 20 (residual 1.3e-3 at 500 iterations), so a fin is not needed for the failure. A slower ramp (x1.01, 50 at iteration 393) holds at Mach 0.5 to 700 and not at Mach 0.9 (396) or Mach 3 (350: the ramp was at 32): the limit is a level, not a start-up transient. What does not move it: `sub_iters` 6 (non-finite at 189 at 50), the implicit entropy fix 0.5 (222), a frozen ghost-state linearisation (200); the scalar (Rusanov) implicit dissipation holds at 50 (to 320) but converges much slower (CA 0.686 against 0.49 at iteration 320); the pseudo-time step sized by the weakest direction (`DtMode::MinDirection`) is non-finite at iteration 19 even at 5. *GPU f32, Draft sector, the ten Mach numbers* (12000-iteration cap, 8 ms per iteration): at `cfl_max` 10 every one is finite, eight stop on the mean rule (Mach 0.5 10 710, 0.9 10 360, 1.0 11 830, 1.1 10 770, 1.3 9 790, 1.7 8 620, 2.3 7 430, 3.0 6 890 iterations) and Mach 0.1 and 0.8 (unsteady base wake, 0.3 % oscillation of the window mean) reach the cap; at 20 Mach 0.1 to 2.3 are finite and stop after 9 250 / 8 060 / 9 900 / 7 670 / 8 730 / 8 240 / 7 390 / 6 720 / 6 000 iterations (25 to 35 % fewer) and Mach 3.0 is non-finite at 411 to 450; at 30 seven of ten are non-finite (0.1 at 270, 0.8 at 229, 0.9 at 211, 1.0 at 230, 1.1 at 3 343, 2.3 at 227, 3.0 at 188). Mach 3 at 20 is a precision effect on top of the limit: the same case in df32 holds 3000 iterations, f32 at 15 holds 3000. *The rule*: `Case3::with_tier` caps `cfl_max` of every viscous half domain and sector at `VISCOUS_3D_CFL_MAX` = 10, finned or not; the wedge keeps 50 below Mach 1 and 20 above (it holds there). The half domain was 20 on this evidence of the ANF nose with fins (short body, Lift tier, 8-column taper, GPU f32): Mach 0.8, 2 degrees 2 200 iterations (CN 0.70606, CA 0.65889, `x_cp` 3.4415), 6 degrees 2 500 (2.11917, 0.61088, 3.4442); Mach 2, 2 degrees 5 991 (mean rule; the range rule's 5e-4 is above the 0.08 % oscillation) (0.38747, 0.69700, 3.4001), 6 degrees 1 200 (1.15947, 0.70544, 3.3928); the same four at 10 reach `CdStationary` after 3 000 (0.70568, 0.65872, 3.4425), 3 350 (2.11827, 0.61227, 3.4441), 1 250 (0.38749, 0.69817, 3.4000) and 1 200 (1.15914, 0.70509, 3.3927): CN differs by at most 0.05 %, CA by 0.23 %, `x_cp` by 0.0010 cal; 20 saves 25 to 27 % at Mach 0.8 and costs 4.8 times at Mach 2 (stop rule) and is not safe at Mach 3 in f32. Not yet run at 10: the Coarse example sector at Mach 3 and 2.3 (`gpu3d`'s late blow-ups).

**Why the finned sector needs thousands of iterations: the base drag settles last, and the time to settle is proportional to 1 / CFL** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; the G-F1 sector: ANF body, four thin fins, Coarse tier, 196 x 128 x 27 = 677 376 cells, GPU f32, `incidence_rans --domain sector --history-every 50`, CA and its parts every 50 iterations; these runs are at the old defaults, `cfl_max` 20 at Mach 2 and 50 at Mach 0.5 with a ramp of x1.02, not at the rule's 10). *Mach 2*: the converged CA is 0.34915 (the `cfl_max` 50 run with a ramp of x1.01 is flat to 1e-5 from 5000 iterations; an earlier version of this paragraph took the mean of the last 1000 iterations of the cfl 20 run, 0.35000, which is still 0.22 % high: that run is at 0.35048 after 6000 iterations and 0.34993 after 8000). After 1500 iterations the nose, afterbody, fin pressure and the body and fin friction are within 1e-5 of their final values and the base alone is off: its error against the converged base (0.16618) is 0.0421 at 500, 0.0222 at 1000, 0.0133 at 1500, 0.0090 at 2000, 0.0050 at 3000, 0.0031 at 4000, 0.0020 at 5000, 0.0013 at 6000: an algebraic decay (about t^-1.4), not an exponential, so CA of the cfl 20 run is within 0.5 % of the converged value after 5350 iterations and within 0.2 % only after more than 8000. The iterations to 0.5 % times `cfl_max` are the same for three CFL levels (ramp x1.01): 5350 x 20 = 107 000 (ramp x1.02), 3500 x 30 = 105 000, 2150 x 50 = 107 500 (to 0.2 % / 0.1 %: 2850 / 3400 at 50, 5000 and more than 6000 at 30): the settling is pseudo-time and runs at the CFL, so the rule's `cfl_max` 10 costs about 10 700 iterations to 0.5 % on this sector, and a controller that lets the CFL reach 50 after the start-up would save a factor of five. The engine's stop rule (5e-4 over 1000 iterations) never fires before 8000 at cfl 20; the mean rule fires at 6950 (6000 is its floor: `mean_from` 2000 plus two windows of 2000), i.e. with CA 0.2 to 0.4 % high; the application's rule (2e-3 over 500) fires at 4000 with CA 0.9 % above the converged value. The same sector at 50 with the ramp of x1.02 is non-finite at 250, with x1.01 it holds (6000 iterations, finite) and so with x1.005 (6000 iterations, within 0.5 / 0.2 / 0.1 % after 2350 / 3000 / 3450): a ramp that is slower than the flow's start-up is stable here, but not on the example rocket (Mach 0.9 and Mach 3 are non-finite at 396 and 350 with x1.01 on the CPU and at 378 and 356 on the GPU), so a fixed ramp is not a rule. *Mach 0.5* (`cfl_max` 50, preconditioned): converged CA 0.24584 (flat from 7000), within 0.5 / 0.2 / 0.1 % after 4300 / 5200 / 5700, the range rule fires at 7000, the mean rule at 7150, the application's rule at 4500.
