One change at a time (iterations to 0.5 % against the converged value, cfl 20 ramp x1.02 at Mach 2): the 8-column trailing-edge taper against 4: identical to 50 iterations; `sub_iters` 2: 5150 against 5350 (Mach 0.5: 4050 / 4850 / 5400 against 4300 / 5200 / 5700), at 0.84 of the time per iteration; 100 first-order iterations: 4250 against 5350 at Mach 2, 4750 / 5700 / 6400 against 4300 / 5200 / 5700 at Mach 0.5; `cfl_max` 30: 3650 against 5350 at Mach 2 and, at Mach 0.5 (50 is the default), 6000 / 6850 / more than 8000 with a 0.12 % oscillation. The finless example body on a uniform-azimuth half domain reaches a residual of 1e-3 in 500 iterations at `cfl_max` 20 (Mach 0.5, CPU), so the slow base is a property of the finned sector's grid or of its fins (a coarser fin stack, `y+` 160 against 40, lowers the residual 10 to 20 fold at equal iterations but not the CA drift), not of 3D itself; that cause is not isolated.

**The G-F1 sector at the rule's `cfl_max` 10** (phys3d, GPU f32, Mach 2, `incidence_rans --domain sector --body anf --tier coarse`, default numerics now: 3 solves, 200 first-order iterations, ramp x1.02, cap 10): CA is 0.36929 after 2000 iterations, 0.35744 after 4000, 0.35374 after 6000, 0.35199 after 8000, 0.35095 after 10 000 and 0.35036 after 12 000, against the converged 0.34915: within 0.5 % only after 10 200 iterations and still 0.35 % high at 12 000. Where the stop rules fire: the application's (2e-3 over 500) at 5550 iterations with CA 1.48 % high, the engine's mean rule at 8900 with 0.66 % high, 1e-3 over 1000 at 10 500 (0.47 %), the engine's range rule (5e-4 over 1000) not within 12 000. At the old cfl 20 the application's rule fired at 4000 with 0.88 % and the mean rule at 6950 with about 0.3 %: the rule of 10 costs accuracy at the stop as well as iterations, which the controller above is to give back.

**Taper sensitivity of the Euler tier** (`solver3d`, `tests/one_entry.rs`): the 8-column trailing-edge taper against 4 moves the frozen Euler Coarse finned lift entry by CA(pressure) +3.9 % at 6 degrees and +2.7 % at 2 degrees (CN +1e-5; with 4 columns the old bits are reproduced exactly): the fin trailing edge and the base-adjacent pressure depend on the mesh behind a flush edge. In the RANS Lift tier (short ANF body, Mach 2, 2 degrees, `cfl_max` 10 on both) the same change moved CA by -0.6 % (0.70236 with 4 columns, 0.69817 with 8) and CN by +0.03 % (0.38737, 0.38749); at 6 degrees CA 0.70605 and 0.70509 (-0.14 %), CN 1.15850 and 1.15914; in the G-F1 Coarse sector (thin fins) by nothing (4 against 8 columns identical to 50 iterations, CA 0.35000 and 0.24584).

**A CFL controller by rollback: prototype on the CPU, specification for the runners** (phys3d, 2026-11-18). The settling time of a finned sector is proportional to 1 / CFL (above) and the CFL that is stable differs by case, so a fixed cap leaves a factor of two to five on the table and a fixed schedule is not safe: on the GPU (f32, Draft example sector, ramp x1.01) Mach 0.5 holds at `cfl_max` 50 and stops on `CdStationary` after 4 800 iterations (CA 0.42848; the cap of 10 needs 10 710 with CA 0.43162, the base wake is unsteady there) while Mach 0.9 at 50 is non-finite at 378, Mach 2.3 at 30 at 436 and Mach 3 at 30 and at 50 at 357 and 356; the G-F1 sector at Mach 2 (Coarse) holds at 50 with x1.01 and x1.005 (6 000 iterations) and not with x1.02 (250). *The prototype* is `Solver3::snapshot` / `restore` (the conserved variables, the turbulence variables, the ramp and the iteration count; `a_snapshot_restores_the_run_exactly`) and `ROLLBACK=1` of `viscous3_fins` `fin_half_incidence_series`: a snapshot every 50 iterations; when a step's residual is not finite or above 1e5 the run returns to the last snapshot, `cfl_max` is multiplied by 0.7 and the run goes on. CPU f64, Draft example sector, start `cfl_max` 50, ramp x1.02: Mach 0.5 one rollback (failure at 196, cap 35) and CA 0.468 at 400 iterations (fixed 20: 0.476, fixed 30: 0.469), running to 650; Mach 0.9 rollbacks at 190 (cap 35), 159 (24.5: a second one straight after the restore) and 473 (17.1), running to 650; Mach 3 at 191 (35), 153 (24.5), 375 (17.1) and then 356, 357 and 353 (12, 8.4, 5.9: each failure comes within seven iterations of the restore, the snapshot at 350 already holds the growing mode) with the run finite to 700 at 5.9. *Specification* for `run3_on` and for `GpuRunner3::run_on` (chunks of 50 iterations, the snapshot a state download or a device copy): keep the last two snapshots, check the residuals of a chunk (non-finite or above 1e5, and the CN / CA monitor) before they reach `StopRule3`, on a failure restore the older snapshot if it follows a restore within 10 iterations and the last one otherwise, redo the chunk with `cfl_max` x0.7; the cap only goes down; nothing changes in the converged state, only the path and the CFL. Not done: the runners, the ten-Mach table on the GPU with the controller, a recovery of the cap, and the decision to take `VISCOUS_3D_CFL_MAX` back to the engine's 50 / 20 once the controller is in (until then the rule of 10 stands).

**The CFL controller as a rule, measured** (phys3d with `gpu3d`, `c764e0700e`; GPU f32, the example sector, `sub_iters` 2, `finned_table`; the controller is `CflControl` in `run3.rs`, shared by `GpuRunner3::run_on` and the CPU `run3_on`, every answer is a `CflEvent` in `PointResult3.cfl_events`; the stop-rule windows scale with `max(1, cfl_ref / CFL)`, `cfl_ref` 20 viscous / 10 Euler). Fixed `cfl_max` 10 (the rule of the engine without the controller, scaled windows) against the controller started at the engine defaults, 50 below Mach 1 and 20 above (iterations to the stop rule, `+n` iterations thrown away by rollbacks, wall seconds of the point with set-up, final CA = the window mean; the cap `cfl_max` ends where the rollbacks left it). **Draft**: Mach 0.5 (safe variant) 14 130 its, 127.7 s, CA 0.45279 against 6 000 (+14), 54.9 s, cap 35, CA 0.45310 (iterations -58 %, wall -57 %, CA +0.07 %); Mach 0.9 15 220, 88.3 s, 0.44856 against 8 920 (+110), 53.2 s, cap 17.2, 0.44867 (-41 %, -40 %, +0.02 %); Mach 2.3 12 000, 64.3 s, 0.49184 against 6 000 (+0), 32.5 s, cap 20, 0.48941 (-50 %, -49 %, -0.49 %, the window oscillates by 0.96 %); Mach 3.0 12 000, 64.2 s, 0.42694 against 8 580 (+37), 46.5 s, cap 14, 0.42657 (-29 %, -28 %, -0.09 %). **Coarse** (829 k cells): Mach 0.5 25 090, 617.5 s, 0.45864 against 11 300 (+31), 281.0 s, cap 24.5, 0.45842 (-55 %, -54 %, -0.05 %); Mach 0.9 24 040, 589.2 s, 0.46844 against 10 910 (+86), 271.6 s, cap 24.5, 0.46747 (-55 %, -54 %, -0.21 %); Mach 2.3 16 620, 375.8 s, 0.49736 against 8 580 (+33), 195.7 s, cap 14, 0.49607 (-48 %, -48 %, -0.26 %); Mach 3.0 15 230, 343.7 s, 0.41689 against 11 220 (+14), 255.4 s, cap 14, 0.41612 (-26 %, -26 %, -0.18 %). The rollbacks: Draft Mach 0.5 one (iteration 214, 50 to 35), Mach 0.9 three (183 twice, 194: 35, 24.5, 17.2), Mach 3 one at 2 387 (20 to 14); Coarse Mach 0.5 two (215, 216: 35, 24.5), Mach 0.9 two (194, 192: 35, 24.5), Mach 2.3 one at 5 583 (20 to 14: the late blow-up of the default, 4 508 on the first run), Mach 3 one at 264 (20 to 14: 932). Every controlled point reaches its stop rule; the answer differs from the fixed-cap run by 0.02 to 0.5 % in CA, the size of the window oscillation (0.2 to 1 %). *The stop rule at low CFL*: without the scaling the fixed `cfl_max` 10 stops 33 to 36 % earlier (Draft 9 500 / 10 360 / 7 470 / 6 910 iterations) and 0.37 to 0.47 % high against the scaled run (CA 0.45467 / 0.45066 / 0.49366 / 0.42850 against 0.45279 / 0.44856 / 0.49184 / 0.42694); on the G-F1 sector at Mach 2 (the history of `incidence_rans`) the application's rule (2e-3 over 500) fires at 5 550 iterations with CA 1.48 % above the converged value at cfl 10, and at 7 950 with 0.82 % when its window is the scaled 1 000 (0.89 % at cfl 20: the scaling gives the CFL-20 bias back). The bias that remains is that of a drift rule on an algebraic settling (the error left is about `rate x time`), about 0.8 to 0.9 % for the application's rule and 0.3 to 0.7 % for the engine's mean rule; it is not removed by any window of a few thousand iterations.

**Fin friction with roughness: the Aupoix wall state above the roughness layer runs away at the fin tips** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; Draft example sector, CPU f64, Mach 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3, `cfl_max` 10, 800 iterations, `viscous3_fins` `CASE=rocket KS_BODY KS_FIN WALL_REPORT`; reported by `app-integrate`: Coarse fin friction 0.036 / 0.055 / 0.099 / 0.093 at Mach 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 against 0.022 to 0.028 smooth, `y+` max 1e5). *The cause is the fin tips, not the fin sides*: the first cell above a fin tip is the cell of the far-field fan, `d` = 0.10 to 0.14 calibre against `ks` 7.9e-4 (`d / ks` 130 to 260, smooth `y+` ~7 000, `Re_d` 1.5 to 2.7e5). There `aupoix_wall` has two fixed points and neither is the rough-wall shear: `mu_t,w = k_w / omega_w` with `omega_w` capped by Menter's smooth `60 nu / (beta1 d^2)` grows with `u_tau^2 d^2`, and above `Re_d ~ 60 / (beta1 k_w+) ~ 240 to 340` the fixed-point map has no solution below the cap `1e5 nu`; with `ks+ < ~3` (`k_w+ = 0`) it stays on the laminar branch. Per-face wall report (Mach 1.0 and 1.1, ks 60 um): all twenty tip faces on the laminar branch, `mu_t,w = 0`, `cf` 1e-5, `ks+` 3 (the tips carry no friction); Mach 1.3: two on the capped branch (`mu_t,w / mu` 1e5, `cf` 0.80, `y+` 1.3e5), the rest laminar; ks 150 um at Mach 1.3: all twenty capped, `cf` 0.72 to 0.79, `y+` 1.2e5 to 1.9e5 (the `y+` the application reports); the same faces smooth: `cf` 0.0025 to 0.0030, `mu_t,w / mu` 300. The fin sides (`y+` 6 to 30, `ks+` 50 to 300, `mu_t,w / mu` below 10) and the body are untouched. *Friction parts* (`DragParts.friction`, on pi/4, Mach 1.3, ks body and fin 60 um): fin 0.04381 = sides 0.03941 + tips 0.00438 before, **0.04011 = sides 0.03957 + tips 0.00052 after** the fix; smooth 0.02374 = sides 0.02344 + tips 0.00029; so the rough fin sides are 1.69 times the smooth ones, the rough flat plate (Mills & Hang `x / ks` 2 000 against the turbulent correlation at the chord Reynolds number) gives 1.66, and the tips 1.8 (the shift of the law), not 15; ks 150 um: fin 0.04709 = sides 0.04646 + tips 0.00062 (2.0 times); Mach 1.0 0.04520, Mach 1.1 0.04223, Mach 1.3 0.04011 (falling with Mach, as the smooth friction does). A 60 um finish on a 0.12 m chord is a factor 1.7 above smooth, not tens of percent. *The fix* (`turbulence3::wall_state`, mirrored by `gpu3d`): a rough cell takes (1) the Aupoix state as before, (2) with its `mu_t,w / mu` from 300 to 1 000 the smooth automatic state mixed in (smoothstep: a runaway is a cell whose speed is that of the free stream), (3) the rough law of the wall for `d / ks` from 1 to 4 (smoothstep in `ln(d / ks)`): `u_tau` the root of `U = u_tau (U+_law(d u_tau / nu) - ln(1 + 0.3 ks+) / kappa)`, the shift of Cebeci & Bradshaw that reproduces Nikuradse's `U+ = ln(y / ks) / kappa + 8.5` at `y = ks` (8.56), then `k_w`, `omega_w`, `mu_t,w` as the smooth state at that `u_tau`. A wall-resolved cell has `d / ks` of 0.01 to 0.3 and keeps the Aupoix state bit for bit (the rough plate G2r: +0.72 % against Mills & Hang as before; the first-cell `y+` sweep at Mach 0.2: -2.5 / -8.1 % at `y+` 7 / 21, as before). Tests: `rough_wall_state_is_aupoix_verbatim_inside_the_roughness_layer`, `a_rough_cell_far_above_the_roughness_takes_the_rough_law_of_the_wall`, `g2r_yplus_sweep` (ignored). The `y+` report of the GPU runner includes the fin tips (the CPU one leaves them out: `y_plus_max`), which is what put 1e5 in the application's table; the faces of the fin tips are not wall-resolved by design.

**The rough fin after the GPU mirror, and the wall state of a fin-side cell** (phys3d with `gpu3d`, `47c9ccec77`). GPU Coarse example sector (191 x 140 x 31, cfl 20, sub 2, `rough-coarse`), ks 60 um: fin friction 0.04446 / 0.04106 / 0.03819 / 0.03711 at Mach 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3 / 1.7 (0.0355 / 0.0550 / 0.0990 / 0.0929 with the Aupoix state alone; the smooth table has 0.022 to 0.028), body friction 0.19947 / 0.19424 / 0.18528 / 0.16617 (the application's 0.20019 / 0.19487 / 0.18591 / 0.16669: within 0.4 %, the wall-resolved cells are not touched); Draft 0.04457 / 0.04193 / 0.03999 / 0.03938 (CPU Draft 0.0452 / 0.0422 / 0.0401 at Mach 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.3). The wall state of the cells of the CPU Draft run (Mach 1.3, ks body and fin 60 um, 500 iterations, `WALL_REPORT`): the *median fin-side face* `(99, 30, 1)` at x 14.945, r 0.52: `d` 1.07e-4 (`d / ks` 0.136), `y+` 7.10, `ks+` 52.4, `U` 0.349, `Re_d` 33.9, `cf` 0.00414, `k_w / u_tau^2` 1.077, `omega_w nu / u_tau^2` 2.22, `mu_t,w / mu` 0.485; the face at the 90th `y+` percentile `(95, 48, 1)`: `y+` 15.3, `ks+` 55.2, `U` 0.606, `cf` 0.00346, 1.13, 2.14, 0.53 (Aupoix, as before the fix: `d / ks` is below 1); the *median fin-tip face* `(92, 52, 0)`: `d` 0.103, `y+` 9 438, `ks+` 72.1, `Re_d` 1.9e5, `cf` 0.00382, `k_w / u_tau^2` 3.35, `omega_w nu / u_tau^2` 8.6e-4, `mu_t,w / mu` 464 (the rough law: before, `mu_t,w / mu` 0 or 1e5); the blunt fin edges (`i` faces, 76) have `cf` 1e-5 by construction (fin edges 0.00002 of the friction). Rough plate G2r, first-cell `y+` 0.72 / 20.8 (target 0.5 / 15, Mach 0.2, `ks+` 250): `cf` +0.70 % / -8.1 % against Mills & Hang, unchanged by the fix; `gpu3d`'s G-F1 Mach 0.5 fin friction at fin `y+` 1 (Fine, 978 k cells) is 0.9278 of the reference against 0.9194 on Coarse: the 0.92 is the reference's leading-edge / root boundary layer, not the wall treatment at `y+` 15 to 25. What a finish does to the fin: the rough flat plate at this chord (`x / ks` 2 000 for 60 um, 800 for 150 um, Re_x 2.7e6 to 3.5e6) is 1.6 times the smooth one for 60 um (local Mills & Hang at the trailing edge; 2.0 for the mean friction of Schlichting's formula) and 1.95 (2.5) for 150 um; the sector gives 1.69 and 2.0 (the tip faces add 4 % of the fin friction); "tens of percent" is `ks+` below ~10.

**What removes the CFL limit of the finned viscous step: three candidates, measured on the CPU** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; Draft example sector, f64, ramp x1.02, `sub_iters` 3, low-Mach preconditioning below Mach 1, `viscous3_fins` `CASE=rocket`, and the viscous block of `solver3::rans` tests, `ddadi_cfl_scan`). *Where the limit is*: the plain approximate factorisation is non-finite after 190 to 230 iterations at `cfl_max` 50, finned and finless alike (finless Mach 0.5: 196), so it is not in the fin stacks; the defect `|b - A du| / |b|` after the factored solve is 0.03 at an effective CFL of 2.7, 0.18 at 7.4, 1.1 at 18 and 1.4 at 20 (the cross terms `O_i O_j` of the factors grow with CFL^2) and the defect-correction passes diverge from CFL ~25 to 40; the late failures of the default CFL on the GPU (Coarse Mach 3.0 at 267, Mach 2.3 at 5 587: `late-worst`) start in the wake cells right behind the base plane (`i` = 151 to 156: p / p_inf 0.030 / rho 0.026 at Mach 3, p 0.378 / rho 0.227 at Mach 2.3, radius 0.28 to 0.50), not on the fins or the nose. *(b) damped or converged defect correction*: the passes of weight 0.7 are still non-finite at about 200 iterations at cfl 50; more passes diverge faster; and an accurate solve is not what the scheme wants (below): rejected. *(c) `k` lines in the fin-stack columns only*: not built, the finless domain dies at the same CFL. *(a) DDADI* (`ImplicitParams::ddadi = Some(omega)`, `implicit3::dd_diagonal`, `DDADI=1 DDADI_RELAX=omega` of the harness; default `None`, no change to any run): the unfactored operator `A = D + sum_d O_d` with the full diagonal `D = P + dt sum_d Dg_d`, the factors `D + O_d` and `D` between them where `P` stood, the correction multiplied by `omega`. Its defect is 0.2 to 0.8 from CFL 2.7 to 50 and every pass reduces it by 0.7. With `omega` 1 on the example sector (Mach 0.5): stable at CFL 100 (1 800 iterations; also Mach 0.9 and 3 to 800 iterations), non-finite at CFL 200 (iteration 650); CA at 1 000 iterations 0.43991 at cfl 50 and 0.43031 at cfl 100 against 0.45609 for the plain factors at cfl 20; the iterations to CA within 0.5 % of 0.4280 (the cfl-100 value at 1 800 iterations, still falling 4e-5 per 100) are about 1 050 at cfl 100, 2 200 at cfl 50 and about 5 300 for the plain factors at 20 (0.43529 at 3 500: +1.7 %), a `sum CFL x iterations` of 105 000 to 110 000, the 107 000 of the plain scheme on the G-F1 sector, and CA as a function of the summed CFL is the same curve (0.43997 at 50 000 for the plain factors, 0.43991 for DDADI at cfl 50): *no candidate changes the `t^-1.4` tail of the base drag, it is a law of the pseudo-time and the CFL cap only sets how many iterations the time costs*. *But the accurate solve is not safe*: on the viscous block (Mach 0.5 and 2, van Albada, SST, `re_c` 2e4, 400 steps) the plain factors reach a residual of 1e-11 to 1e-15 at CFL 6 to 10, 1e-5 at 20 and are non-finite at 50 (Mach 2; Mach 0.5 at 300), and DDADI with `omega` 1 sits on a period-2 limit cycle (residual 1.2e-2 at Mach 0.5 from CFL 3, 7.5e-3 at Mach 2 from CFL 6, constant for ever, the state 1e-2 / 3e-3 off the converged one, 0.12 at Mach 2 CFL 50); the plain factors do the same with `sub_iters` 10 at CFL 6 (6.4e-3): the accurate solve of the first-order left-hand side against the limited second-order residual has a period-2 mode that the factorisation error of the plain scheme was damping. `omega` below 1 damps it: the state of the plain scheme to 1e-12 for `omega` 0.7 at CFL 6 to 50 on both (to 300 at Mach 0.5; not CFL 3 at Mach 0.5, not 300 at Mach 2), 0.6 at 6 to 300 on both (CFL 3 and Mach 2 at 300 slowly: 1e-4, 5e-8), 0.5 at 3 to 300 (1e-7 or better); but it slows the example sector, CA at 900 iterations (cfl 100, Mach 0.5): 0.43102 (`omega` 1), 0.44880 (0.7), 0.45959 (0.6), 0.47410 (0.5) against 0.46182 for the plain factors at cfl 20, so 0.7 takes 1.7 times fewer iterations than the plain scheme at its own cap (CA 0.4485: 900 against 1 500) and 0.5 is slower than it (also non-finite at cfl 200, iteration 900). There is no `omega` that is safe on the block and fast on the sector; `omega` 1 follows the plain CA curve to 52 000 summed CFL (within 0.1 %) and shows no alternation of CA in 650 per-iteration prints, but I cannot say it is free of the cycle at the converged state. *Cost on the GPU, estimated, not measured*: the diagonal `D` replaces the three `d` arrays (-166 MB at Coarse), two matrix-vector products of `D` per factored solve (four at `sub_iters` 2) and one in the defect, the assembly of `D`, a scale: +10 to 15 % per step, the line solves unchanged (the shortcut of the first defect `r = N_i (X - dU) + N_j (Y - dU)` holds). *Decision*: not worth a port (at most 1.7 times in iterations at the stable `omega`, against 1.3 to 4 times already given by the controller); the plain factors with the rollback controller stay. What would remove the limit properly is a left-hand side that matches the residual (a limiter-frozen second-order left-hand side) with the accurate solve; not prototyped.

**The late blow-ups of the default CFL: two causes, neither is the reconstruction** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; `gpu3d`'s `late-worst`: Coarse Mach 3.0 non-finite at 267, Mach 2.3 at 5 587 at cfl 20, the lowest p / p_inf of the field in the first wake columns behind the base plane, 0.03 at Mach 3; fixed `cfl_max`, no controller, `finned_table --no-control [--precision f32|df32] [--cpu]`). *The CFL limit of the plain factors, independent of the precision*: the CPU f64 solver on the Coarse sector at Mach 3.0, `cfl_max` 20, is non-finite after 297 iterations (the GPU f32: 267); the Draft sector fails at `cfl_max` 30 in f64 after 200 to 245 iterations at Mach 3.0 and 250 to 350 at Mach 2.3 (GPU f32 218 / 205, df32 207 / 192; at cfl 50 at 200 in f64) and at Mach 3.0, cfl 20 in every precision, only later with more bits: GPU f32 at 1 277, f64 CPU at 1 900, GPU df32 at 2 433. *Where it starts in f64* (Draft, Mach 3.0, cfl 20, iteration 1 900, the state before the last step): density 2e22 and 2e29 in two cells of the fin root on the body surface, `(85, 16, 1)` x 13.46 and `(106, 17, 1)` x 14.74 at r 0.501 next to `Fin(0)` (volumes 1.4e-9 and 4.1e-9, among the smallest cells of the grid), and 1e41 at `(134, 36, 7)` in the far field; at cfl 30 (Mach 3.0, iteration 244) the explosion is at x 14.86 to 14.91, r 0.50 next to `Body(1)` / `Fin(0)`. So the wake cells that `late-worst` lists at 250 / 5 450 are where p and rho are lowest, not necessarily where the iteration goes unstable. *Mach 2.3 at cfl 20 on the Draft sector is f32-only*: f32 non-finite at 955, df32 holds 6 000 iterations (`MeanStationary`, CD 0.49717), CPU f64 holds 2 000: roundoff-excited at a margin that more bits keep. *Not the switch to second order*: with 400 first-order iterations the Draft failure at cfl 30 is the same (GPU f32 235 and 204 iterations), and the CFL ramp restarted at the switch does not change it either (`RAMP_AT_SWITCH=1`, CPU). *Not the reconstruction*: a relative floor in `reconstruct3` (a face state with density or pressure below `f` times the cell value is the cell value, first order; the positivity rule is `f` = 0): CPU f64 Coarse Mach 3.0 cfl 20, `f` 0.25 is hit by no face and the run is bit-identical, non-finite at 297, `f` 0.7 (12.4 M faces fall back) at 340, `f` 0.9 (15.7 M, nearly first order everywhere) at 400; GPU f32 Draft cfl 20 with the same floor in `reconstruct3` of `gpu/body3.rs`: `f` 0 / 0.3 / 0.6: Mach 2.3 955 / 955 / 956 and Mach 3.0 1 277 / 1 277 / 1 278 iterations, so neither a floor nor first order at the extreme cells moves the failure, and the base pressure (G-V3 / G-J) is not touched (the experiments are not in the tree). [INFERENCE] The instability is that of the plain factors (above) at the smallest, most stretched cells of the fin root, lower on Coarse than on Draft; the measurements exclude the reconstruction and, for Mach 3, the precision; they do not isolate the operator. The answer stays the controller: the cap of 14 to 24 it settles at after one rollback is what the plain factors hold, in f32 as in f64; a pressure floor is not a remedy. Not done: Mach 2.3 Coarse at 5 587 in f64 or df32 (80 minutes on the CPU).

**G-L1 and G-F5 on the corrected Finner section, the viscous Lift tier, with the controller** (phys3d, 2026-11-18; `fin_lift_gates` on the GPU, f32, remote RTX 3080 Laptop, both roll phases, `BOWSHOCK_FIN_TIER=lift`, mesh and section of `solver3d` abe24a6 (full-chord wedge, blunt 0.08 cal trailing edge), the controller on by default (engine `cfl_max` 50 below Mach 1, 20 above, rollback, floor 5; the gate lines do not print the rollbacks); jobs `phys3d-gate_f5_anf_cd0-ctl` 2 396 s, `phys3d-gl1-ctl-*` 2 017 s and 962 s (two Mach shards) against 2 700 s for all of G-L1 at cfl 10 on the local 3090 before). **G-L1** (`CN_alpha` 13 %, `x_cp` 0.25 cal): 30 of 32 lines PASS; all 16 `x_cp` lines PASS; `CN_alpha` measured / data, between roll phase (in-plane within 0.6 %): wind tunnel 0.69 17.54 / 15.70 (+11.7 %, in-plane +12.2 %), **0.79 18.43 / 16.27 (+13.3 %, in-plane +13.8 %: FAIL by 0.3 and 0.8 %)**, 1.728 12.90 / 11.92 (+8.2 %), 1.99 11.31 / 10.60 (+6.7 %), 2.48 9.63 / 9.34 (+3.2 %); free flight 1.8 12.38 / 11.29 (+9.7 %), 2.351 9.98 / 10.37 (-3.8 %), 2.665 9.23 / 8.88 (+3.9 %). Before (assumed bevel, 8-column taper, cfl 10): +1 to +12 %. The 6-degree INFO: `CN_alpha(6)` +1.1 to +6.5 % of `CN_alpha(2)`. Iterations 1 250 to 7 000. **G-F5** (`C_D0`, 8 %): subsonic 0.4963 / 0.5027 at Mach 0.5 (between / in-plane: +7.9 % PASS, +9.3 % FAIL) and 0.5140 / 0.5132 at Mach 0.7 (+11.7 %, +11.6 % FAIL) against 0.46; supersonic KNOWN (item 38, the band is not widened): 0.5300 / 0.5285 at 1.832 against 0.585 (-9.4 %, -9.7 %), 0.4297 / 0.4280 at 2.375 against 0.484 (-11.2 %, -11.6 %), 0.3845 / 0.3830 at 2.718 against 0.435 (-11.6 %, -11.9 %), 0.3387 / 0.3368 at 3.147 against 0.373 (-9.2 %, -9.7 %); parts at Mach 0.5 between: nose +0.0365, base +0.1561, fins +0.2141. The supersonic points moved from +21 to +27 % (the assumed section) to -9 to -12 % (the sourced one): 1 to 4 % outside the 8 % band on the low side; the three subsonic lines FAIL at +9 to +12 %.

### Solve time: from 41 minutes to about 4 (perf5, 2026-11-18)

The full Coarse solve of the example rocket (10 zero-incidence sector points Mach 0.1-3.0 plus the stability points, RTX 3090, f32, `analyze::cfd::tests::perf::engine3d_finned_timing`) took **2 481 s** on the old Luma pin at a fixed `cfl_max` 10 (226 000 iterations), and **1 654 s** with the rollback controller on Luma v0.5.0 (job `2-20261118-005748-27855`: 136 950 iterations, 16 angle points, 44 s fixed costs, 8.4 GiB device peak). The time was iterations, not step cost: a point needed 5 000-16 000 iterations at 5-17 ms each.

**Why so many iterations.** The base wake settles last, in pseudo-time: `N x CFL x U_inf / c'_w` is constant within +-7 % across the ten Mach numbers of the 3-fin Coarse table (gpu.md 9b), with `c'_w` the wave speed the local time step sees in the still wake fluid. The tail is geometric (base-CA error ratio about 1.5 per 20 000 summed CFL on the G-F1 sector). The levers are a larger stable CFL, a better initial field and a stop rule that does not wait out a floor; a faster step buys little (Luma v0.5.0's CUDA and wgpu backends run the sector step at the same 8.5 ms).

1. **Luma v0.5.0** (`7fafed6`). Same step time on the sector (8.5 ms/it f32, both backends); CUDA slower on small grids (wedge `run(50)` 0.59 against 0.41 ms/it, Luma issue #6); df32 sector 14.5 against 16.3 ms on CUDA; shared transient buffers: device peak of the full solve 8.4 GiB instead of 10.3.
2. **Negative: a lower Turkel cut-off of the low-Mach preconditioning** (`ur_min = K |u_inf|`, K 0.3 / 0.1, also above Mach 1), Draft sector. Non-finite at Mach 0.9 / 1.3 and (K 0.1) at every supersonic point; more rollbacks and twice the iterations at Mach 0.5. The left-hand side is preconditioned against an uncorrected right-hand side dissipation (scalar model: unstable once `c / U_r > 2`). Not tried: preconditioned dissipation on both sides.
3. **DDADI at omega 1 with a boosted implicit dissipation** (`ImplicitParams::{ddadi, lhs_dis_boost}`, now also on the GPU). `Case3::with_tier` sets `ddadi = Some(1.0)`, `lhs_dis_boost = DDADI_DIS_BOOST` (0.7) and `cfl_max = DDADI_CFL_MAX` (100) on every non-wedge viscous domain.
   - The period-2 cycle of omega 1 (CPU viscous block, `ddadi_boost_scan`, `ddadi_cycle_cause`) comes from the left-hand side's Roe `|A|` dissipation falling short of the HLLC right-hand side's, not from the limiter: a first-order right-hand side cycles too, the scalar Rusanov left-hand side does not. Scaling the face dissipation by `1 + delta` removes it from `delta` 0.2 at Mach 0.5 and 0.7 at Mach 2 (CFL 20-300).
   - GPU Draft sector at CFL 100: CA within 0.5 % of the plain run's converged value after **800 / 850** iterations at Mach 0.5 / 2.3, against 3 100 / 4 150, no rollback. +5.7 % per step (G-F1 sector 9.07 against 8.58 ms). `DtMode::MinDirection` with DDADI is non-finite.
4. **Extrapolated-drift stop rule** (`StopReason::Settled`, `Numerics3::{drift_block, drift_tol}`): block means of CA (CN and the moment at incidence) over 10 000 summed CFL; stop when the geometric tail's extrapolated remaining change is within 5e-3. Replayed on the stored G-F1 histories (CFL 20-50) it stops at 2 000-6 000 iterations, 0.2-1.1 % from the converged CA. The mean rule (`mean_from` plus two windows) has a 6 000-iteration floor at CFL 20 and above and stops at 6 000-8 000.
5. **One stability angle** (`LIFT_ALPHAS = [2.0]`). CN(6)/CN(2) is 2.97-3.04 up to Mach 2.3 (3.27 at Mach 3); CN_alpha and x_cp come from the 2-degree point. The incidence mean rule judges CN and the moment only (`Numerics3::{incidence_window, incidence_from, incidence_tol}` 1000 / 1000 / 2e-3): Mach 2.3 6 000 -> 3 000 iterations, CN within 0.01 % of the long run.
6. **Axisymmetric seed of the finned sectors** (`Analysis::seed_axisymmetric`, default on; `BOWSHOCK_NO_SEED` off). A cold finned sector starts from the converged wedge of its body, Mach number and tier (`engine::axisym_init::axisym_to_sector`, `GpuSolver3::set_state_turb`), solved first (f32, at most 3 000 iterations), with `drift_tol` halved (`SEED_DRIFT_TOL_FACTOR`).
   - Draft sector, 10 000 iterations each way: within 1 % of the final CD after 150-650 iterations, against 2 050-3 700 cold. Seeded and cold finals within 0.2-0.4 %.
   - Half-domain angle points were slower seeded (Mach 0.5: 1 570 against 810 iterations), so half domains start cold.

**Result** (3090, f32, 10 sector points and 8 points at 2 degrees):

| run | job | wall | iterations |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.5.0 baseline, plain factors, controller | `2-20261118-005748-27855` | 1 654 s | 136 950 |
| DDADI + drift rule + one angle | `1-20261118-021751-31799` | 306 s | 25 500 |
| + axisymmetric seed of the sectors | `1-20261118-024355-31072` | **245 s** | 38 300 (with the wedges) |

Sector CD of the seeded run against the baseline: -0.1 to +1.1 % (Mach 0.8 +1.1 %). CN against the baseline's 2-degree points: within 0.6 %.

Open: unseeded, the Mach 1.3 sector at CFL 100 left a non-physical state (minimum density 6e-10 in the wake, a far-field cell 7.6x off the free stream) that the rollback controller did not catch: it watches the residual (non-finite or above 1e5) and the force monitor, not positivity. The point ended `Failed` and the app interpolated it. The seeded run does not reach that state.

69. **Fixed costs of the GPU point loop: the next run's set-up built while the GPU iterates (fixedcost, 2026-11-20; `BOWSHOCK_NO_FIXEDCOST=1` takes it all back for A/B runs).** *Profile first* (job `0-20261119-232224-2303`, `fc-ab1`, 3090, `BOWSHOCK_TIMING`, new `PointTiming::parts`, `PARTS` / `FACADE` lines of `engine3d_finned_timing`, CPU-only `host_setup_timing` / `cpu_seed_timing`): of the 319.2 s baseline (load 11 at the start, other agents' jobs after) the iteration is 243.6 s of rest chunks; seeds 17.5 s (23 150 wedge iterations at 0.52 ms, 12.4 s, the rest set-up); finned points 8.5 s host grid + solver build (0.5-1.1 s each, none of it hidden), 10.2 s `from_host` (geometry 5.2, viscous 4.5), 14.9 s first chunks (trace about 4 s), 4.2 s state snapshots, 4.4 s final readings, 1 s teardown, 2 s `Context::trim`; outside `run_on` 19 s (sector) + 20 s (half domain). CPU-bound: the wall distance (BVH, rayon: 13 s of the 18 points at all threads, 40 s at one) and the mesher (serial, 0.3-0.6 s per grid); the iteration loop is not (231.4 s of rest chunks at load 11 and at load 64 alike). *Changes (results bit-identical; the A/B switch gates all but the BVH hint):* (1) the host solver of the next run is built on a thread while the GPU iterates this one (`Case3::prefetch_solver`, `Analysis::hint_next` from `cfd/run.rs`; key `Case3::build_key` = grid, `config3`, equations, jet, not the stop-rule settings, so a seeded run finds it); with Luma's context shared between threads the GPU solver of the next run too (`GpuRunner3` built on a background thread, geometry and state uploaded during the iterations; `BOWSHOCK_NO_RUNNER_AHEAD` for the host-only variant; refused when the device cannot hold both, `room_for_next`); test `a_runner_built_on_another_thread_runs_as_one_built_here` (CPU backend, bit-equal state). (2) The exact wall distance is kept with the cached grid (`GridCache`, `RansOpts3::dwall`; the drag nodes at Mach <= 1 share one grid) and its BVH walk starts from the neighbour cell's nearest triangle (bit-identical, -10 %). (3) State snapshots are read back into recycled host buffers (`Array::read_into`, `CflControl::accept` returns the evicted ones), the end-of-run readings (wall forces, viscous fluxes, state) are kept for the facade's report instead of read twice. (4) A seed wedge is no change of shape for the buffer pool (no `Context::trim` on the way to and from it). *A/B, same job, 3090* (`fc-ab1`, first three changes only; the second run at load 64): wall **319.2 s -> 300.1 s**, finned build 8.45 -> 0 s, half-domain outside-`run_on` 20.0 -> 8.5 s, snapshots 4.2 -> 2.8 s; sector outside-`run_on` 19.1 -> 16.0 s (the prefetch missed seeded sectors: key fixed afterwards). The full-state A/B (`fc-ab2`: off / host only / runner ahead) was still queued when this was written. *Seed* (`fc-seed1`, drag only, `BOWSHOCK_SEED_MAX_IT` 3000 / 1500 / 800 / 500 and wedge `drift_tol` x4): the seed cap trades one sector iteration (10.8 ms) for about 20 wedge ones and the sector's iteration count moves by +-500 at subsonic Mach with the limit cycle (item 63): 183.1 / 181.4 / 181.2 / 187.7 s, CD within 0.5 % except Mach 1.0 at cap 1500 (+0.8 %); **no seed policy gain, default kept**. The seed wedge is launch-latency bound; on the CPU it is 100 ms per iteration (200x slower), so it cannot run beside the GPU either. Remaining: the seeds (17 s, 5.5 %), first-chunk traces (about 4 s), `from_host` if the runner built ahead does not overlap.
